# Storyboard , Reel 61 OS v2 · "Claude vs Agent DONE"

Stage-6 board, REBUILT around a single villain and one story arc after Alex's v1 notes:
**(1)** every scene needs far more DETAIL · **(2)** some scenes are too ABSTRACT (the driving car; "no solo work until proven" not detailed or interesting) · **(3)** he wants a VILLAIN and a real arc, not scattered scenes. He likes the camera angles.

Read `61-os-VILLAIN-BIBLE.md` first: it holds the villain design sheet and the LOCKED 8-beat arc. This file is the per-scene detail.
Authored by 10 parallel scene agents, each hammered by 3 adversarial critics (abstraction / detail-density / arc), rewritten against every binding fix, then reconciled by a continuity editor (section at the bottom, which OVERRIDES the cards where they disagree).

VO 55.41s · 10 scenes · 1663f · comp `ClaudeOsReel` · keyword **OS**.

**Number spine (locked):** a week of work in ONE night · ONE rule · 20 passes · 95% record · below 90 = licence lost.

---

### S0 THE LOCKOUT

- **Takeaway:** This thing burns a whole week of work in one night and ships it out the door all night long, and a black silhouette hauls a steel shutter down across the delivery line and padlocks it. The work never stops. None of it ever gets out again.

- **Base object:** **THE PADLOCKED ROLLER SHUTTER, ACROSS A LIVE DELIVERY LINE.** One corrugated steel shutter in a workshop mouth, its coil box, a welded hasp on the sill, one fat brass padlock, and the roller run that carries finished crates out through that mouth onto the apron in front of us. The shutter is the hero prop and the roller run is what makes it cost something: the door is not covering a room, it is covering an EXIT. The verb is unique to this scene, LOCK, and it happens once.
  **⛔ ANTI-COLLISION, S1 FIRST (this is the real risk, it cuts immediately after us):** per the binding fix at `61-os.md:59`, S1 is a **NIGHT STRIP-MALL FORECOURT under buzzing sodium, wide open, cars on an apron, engine fire lighting the whole frame ORANGE, LOW HERO ANGLE**, same coveralled hero. So S0 gives all of that up and takes the opposite: **camera DEAD LEVEL, head-on, centred, no tilt, no low angle**; **no street, no facade, no receding exterior, no apron depth**; the void around the mouth is **flat dead black `#12141A` with zero geometry in it**; and the interior key is pushed to **harsh white-blue ARC light `#C8D8E8`** (it is a grinder, not a bonfire), with warm clay `#D97757` only as a low bounce on the crates and smoke INSIDE the rectangle. **No sparks, no amber and no warm bounce may touch the apron before f178.** S0 reads as ONE WARM HOLE IN A BLACK FIELD being sealed. S1 reads as a wide orange forecourt on fire with depth and a hero shot from down in it. Thumbnail test: a bright rectangle inside a black frame versus an open forecourt on fire.
  **ANTI-COLLISION, S4 SECOND (cited correctly this time):** per the binding fix at `61-os.md:113` S4's turntable is CUT. S4 is a **cold blue-grey DAWN interior, camera INSIDE, high angle, one hard shaft**, and its hero object is the **raised inspection RAMP over a pit, the wind-up tin bot and the tarp he pulls**. Its roll-up door is open scenery. At thumbnail size S4 is a cold room walked around from inside. Ours is a hot rectangle sealed from outside. No other scene owns a shutter, a hasp, a padlock or a roller run. S1 moves to the sticker gun and the forecourt, S2 to the brake pedal, S3 to the rule gate and the height bar, S7 to the licence machine.

- **Story / metaphor:** **THE NIGHT SHIFT NOBODY IS ALLOWED TO SHIP.** This is the pre-villain beat, so per the bible the villain does not lose anything here, he **gains**, and what he gains is **the exit**. The scene splits in two. First the capability is made undeniable and *legible as work that leaves*: crates stacking, a MON to SUN calendar tearing itself down and **piling its pages in the light where you can count them**, a grinder throwing arc-white sparks, and a roller run carrying finished crates out through the mouth and dropping them onto a delivery stack on the apron **on our side of the sill**. That stack is the work reaching the world. A stranger does not have to be told this is a week: seven pages are lying face-up in a fan on the floor.
  Then the door. He does not fight the hero and he does not out-argue him. **He was in there the whole time.** At f118 the hero's own cast shadow on the right-hand interior wall **detaches at the shoulder and stands up**, and at f120 it steps forward out of the dead corner as a hard black silhouette. He hauls the shutter down **from the inside**, across the live roller run, ducks out under the falling slat, and clicks a padlock through the hasp. **No shades, no face, no stamp, no slate tint: he is a black cutout, and his code rain does the identifying.** He is not born here (that is S1's job, peeling off the hero's own body); S0 only plants that **something came off him**, which is why S1's peel plays as a payoff and not an invention.
  What makes the beat land is that **the lock does not stop the work, it stops the delivery.** The machine keeps producing at the same speed and now fires crates into the inside of the steel. The run backs up. The apron stack sits frozen in the cold, the last thing that ever got out. Light gets out. Sparks get out. The work does not. He took the exit, not the capability.

- **Set detail (Alex's #1 note):** Panel-local **1012x792**. Camera outside on the apron, dead level, axis at **y 448**. Doorway mouth **x 140 to 880, y 196 to 700** (504px tall). Sill line **y 700**. Interior floor line **y 660**. **Kicker keep-clear: x < 330, y < 120, and per the geometry fix NOTHING LEFT OF x 340 MAY SIT ABOVE y 124.**
  1. **THE SHUTTER + COIL BOX.** Coil box **x 140 to 880, y 124 to 196**, riveted steel, top edge hard at y124 so the top-left stays empty under the Kicker. At f0 the shutter is rolled up inside it, a fat coil of slats, with **one slat hanging loose below the box lip at y 196 to 210 and vibrating** from the machinery underneath (re-checked against the new box position: it hangs INTO the mouth, not into the Kicker). It **descends in three ratcheting hauls** and becomes the scene's guillotine. Rust weeps in two brown streaks down the left rail **x 150 to 170**. **The bottom slat is bowed and dinged from years of being slammed, which is why it cannot seat flush: when it lands it rests at y 675 and leaves a 25px gap across the whole run.** That bow is the motivated reason the frame never goes black.
  2. **THE HASP + PADLOCK.** Hasp welded to the outboard face of the sill at **505, 690**, dead centre, facing camera. Empty and swinging faintly for the entire first half, a loaded gun in plain sight. The **fat brass padlock** is his only possession: he brings it up at f166, it closes at f176, and it hangs at **505, 720** with its **round black KEYHOLE at 505, 726, 18px across, pointing straight at camera**. Scaled twice the size the hasp needs, so the read is not "secured" but "punished."
  3. **THE MON TO SUN CALENDAR + THE SEVEN-PAGE DRIFT.** Re-nailed **inside the hero's own work loop**, on the near roof post at **570, 455**, nub height, one step from the bench, hanging in the left lamp's cone. A thick block, day name per page. It **tears its own pages off** in the draft, one every ~16 frames, and **the pages do not exit frame: they tumble down through the beam and pile on the floor under the nail, landing face-up and fanned** in a drift at **x 500 to 660, y 630 to 660**, so at f104 there are **exactly SEVEN day-pages readable in one drift**. That drift IS the number spine, and it is countable with your eye. **Builder fallback, per the binding fix:** if the drift cannot hold seven legibly at thumbnail size, kill its count role and move the count onto the crate buffer as **seven visibly banded tiers, one per day**. Do not carry the spine on a single hanging page.
  4. **THE CRATE BUFFER.** Base **x 190 to 340, y 660**, climbing to **y 260**. Stencilled clay crates, each with a short job word branded on the end grain (SHIP, PATCH, MIGRATE, TESTS, DOCS), banded with steel strap. It grows from two crates to a wall by f172 and is the WIP the run pulls from.
  5. **THE CHUTE, THE LINE SHAFT AND THE OUTBOUND ROLLER RUN (the fix that gives the lock a cost).** Belt-driven overhead line shaft, **x 200 to 860, y 196 to 214**, four pulleys and slapping leather belts, driving a roller chute that spits finished crates down. **The run is aimed OUT:** it crosses the sill at **x 620 to 820, y 672 to 690** (clear of the hasp at 505), its outboard section is a **hinged flap** that carries crates over the sill and drops them onto **THE APRON DELIVERY STACK at x 620 to 820, y 690 to 780, on OUR side, in the cold**. The stack grows f0 to f172 in blue-black and cold sodium, never warm. **At f168 the descending bottom slat SHEARS the run at x 660**, snapping the hinged flap down onto the apron and cutting the delivery line in two: three outboard rollers stay out here **spinning, empty, forever**, and the rest is sealed inside. The apron stack **stops dead and never gains another crate.**
  6. **THE OPEN NEON** (replaces the cut job board). A classic red-and-blue neon OPEN oval hung on the inside of the mouth at **660, 300**, swinging on two chains. Zero reading required, it is a shape everybody on earth knows. It is **still lit and still swinging behind the closed shutter at f192**, its coloured glow smudging out through the slat gaps at y 290 to 310. The shop is open. The shop is padlocked.
  7. **THE SPARK BENCH.** **470, 560**. A grinder on a steel bench, the hero's station, throwing a live **arc-white** spark fan down and to the right. It is the scene's key light. After the lock, **the same arc sprays out under the door onto the apron toward camera**, which is the thesis in one image and the first warm thing to touch our side all scene.
  8. **TWO CAGED WORK LAMPS.** Cage centres **350, 250** and **700, 235**, cages 20px, hung **BELOW the coil box and BELOW the line shaft** on visible twisted flex dropping from the shaft line at y214 (26px and 11px of flex). Swaying out of sync from the shaft's vibration. Both cones now start at a visible source and rake down through the dust to the floor line, so the shadows never hold still for one frame, and the left cone is what lights the calendar and its drift.
  9. **THE WALL CLOCK.** **640, 215**. Plain workshop clock, hands **visibly spinning**, minute hand sweeping like a fan. Says "running for hours on its own" with no numeral lockup.
  10. **THE SHOP SIGN.** **x 430 to 860, y 46 to 116**, above the coil box, pushed right of the Kicker zone. Buzzing knockoff neon: **"NIGHT OWL FAB CO."** **No numeral.** The O stutters and drops out every ~40 frames and the buzz is in the bed. The flicker and the dropout are the value; the joke is the OPEN sign, not the sign.
  11. **THE HERO'S TOOL BELT, WITH THE STICKER GUN HOLSTERED.** On the hero at **470, 505**. A chunky sticker gun with a visible spool sits unused on his hip all scene. **This is the Chekhov plant**: it is the exact weapon the copy peels off his body and seizes in S1. Never fired, never pointed.
  12. **THE FORKLIFT CAMEO.** **300, 600**, tracking left to right, knee-high Claude in hi-vis and ear defenders on a tiny pallet truck, ferrying crates in a loop. **He never notices the lock.** At **f184 he drives a pallet straight into the inside of the shutter and we see it FROM OUR SIDE**: the steel bulges outward in a dent, paint flakes jump. Then he reverses and goes around again.
  13. **THE OFFERED CRATE (the distrust prop, dead centre, at readable size).** A single finished crate the hero kicks out through the mouth at f110 as an offering, coming to rest on the apron at **505, 730**, half a metre from the hasp, dead centre. At **f124 the black boot shoves it back inside**, without looking at it. Work offered, work refused, in half a second, at the biggest readable size in the frame. That is "nobody trusts it," acted.
  14. **THE GRIME + FOREGROUND JUNK.** Oil rainbow puddle on the apron **560, 748** carrying a **cold sodium rim only** and rippling on every haul. Swept swarf pile and a stiff broom **820, 670**. Chipped paint and flyer staples all over the door rail. Foreground occluders, near black and soft: **pallet stack (0 to 150, 640 to 792)** bottom left, **oil drum plus coiled air hose (860 to 1012, 600 to 792)** bottom right.
  15. **THE CODE RAIN (now seeded INSIDE).** Three thin columns of sickly yellow-green `#A8B84A` glyphs, per the bible, always on wherever he stands. From **f96 they fall INSIDE the mouth**, in the dead unlit corner behind the hero's right shoulder at **x 700 to 840, y 260 to 660**. The dread lands 24 frames before he moves and it lands in the same room as the hero. From **f168 they fall on the apron on OUR side at x 60 to 200 and x 890 to 1010, y 380 to 792** (never above y 380, never anywhere near the Kicker), and they are **the last thing identifying him after he walks out of frame.**
  16. **THE SHUTTER FACE AS A LIVE SURFACE (from f172 this IS the frame, so it is spec'd like a prop).** Closed, it covers **x 140 to 880, y 196 to 675**, about 18 slats at 26px. **(a) A 4px lit gap between EVERY slat across the full run, plus the 25px hot bar at y 675 to 700 under the bowed bottom slat.** (b) Face detail at coords: rust weep **x 150 to 170**, torn flyers and staple scars across the rail **x 140 to 880, y 690 to 700**, a **stencilled black-and-yellow hazard-chevron block at 760, 400** (a chevron, NOT a bay number: the numeral ban outranks the dressing), the dinged bottom slat. (c) It is a drum: it bulges and rattles on the crate-thud cadence and holds the f184 dent.

- **CONTINUOUS ACTION (fills the whole line):** 193 frames, **eleven named layers** running independently. Nothing lands and parks, and from f172 the card only claims what the steel actually lets through.
  - **L1 HERO BURN (f0 to f192, never stops).** Frame 0 is already mid-action, no ramp: leaning into the grinder, arc-white sparks already flying. A repeating five-beat loop at double speed, roughly every 22 frames, accelerating slightly: grind, turn, band a crate, kick it to the run, back to the grinder. He never looks up at the door. **From f176 the loop turns into a fight with a jam:** he keeps kicking crates at the run and they keep coming back at him off the steel, and at f192 he is still feeding a jam. Visible only as **silhouette shadows cutting across the slat-gap light bars.**
  - **L2 CALENDAR + DRIFT (f0 to f192).** A page tears every ~16f: MON f8, TUE f24, WED f40, THU f56, FRI f72, SAT f88, SUN f104. Each page tumbles down through the left lamp's cone and **lands face-up in the drift**, fanned, so the count accumulates instead of blowing away. **At f104 seven pages are readable in one drift.** At **f118**, as beat five of his existing loop and one step from the bench, he **scoops the spent drift, slaps a fresh block on the nail reading MON**, and starts week two while the shutter is coming down. The scoop is physically possible now because the nail is at 570,455 inside his loop.
  - **L3 CRATE BUFFER + FORKLIFT (f0 to f192).** A crate lands on the buffer every ~11 frames, climbing y660 to y260. The forklift loops continuously. **f184 he bonks the shutter and we see the dent from our side.** He reverses at f188 and **starts a second, smaller bonk at f190 that is un-landed at f192.**
  - **L4 CHUTE, LINE SHAFT, ROLLER RUN, APRON STACK, CLOCK (f0 to f192).** Belts slapping, pulleys turning, rollers rattling, clock hands sweeping. **A crate rides OUT and drops onto the apron stack every ~22 frames from f0 to f168.** f168 the slat shears the run. **f172 to f192: the three outboard rollers are still spinning, empty, and the apron stack has not moved.**
  - **L5 LAMPS, DUST, SPARKS (f0 to f192).** Lamps sway out of sync, dust falls through both cones, the grinder arc never stops. **From f178 the arc sprays OUT under the door onto the apron toward camera**, the only warm light on our side in the whole scene.
  - **L6 CODE RAIN (f96 to f192).** Inside column one f96, two f108, three f116, all in the dead corner at x700 to 840. From f168 they spread onto the apron at x60 to 200 and x890 to 1010, y380 to 792. **Still falling at f192, after he has gone.**
  - **L7 THE VILLAIN (f118 to f186).** **f118: the hero's own cast shadow on the right-hand interior wall detaches at the shoulder.** **f120: it steps forward out of the dead corner into the mouth's right third as a FULL hard-backlit silhouette**, body centred **x 790**, occupying **x 700 to 870**, standing on the interior floor line y660, head crown at **y 330**, sharp shoulder block, tie edge, glove, all pure black cutout against the interior glow. He grabs the pull strap at **x 700** and hauls three times, cuff reaching to x700 on each haul. **One excursion only: f124 to f132 he steps to sill centre, boots the offered crate back inside, and returns to the strap by f142.** f162 he shoves haul three and releases. **f163 to f169 he ducks under the falling slat and comes out onto the apron.** f166 to f172 he brings the padlock up. f174 aligns. f176 closes it. **f176 to f186 he turns and walks straight out of frame right, x880 to x1012, into his own rain, and never looks at what he did.** He is gone by f186. **Shades, face, stamp and slate tint are held back entirely: he is born in S1 and this scene must not spend that.**
  - **L8 SHUTTER (f120 to f176).** Haul one **f120 to f140**, mouth's bottom edge drops y196 to **y364**. Haul two **f142 to f160**, down to **y532**, the interior light narrows to a hot band. Haul three **f162 to f172**: **f168 the slat passes y640 and SHEARS the roller run at x660**, the hinged flap snaps down; **f172 the bowed bottom slat hits the sill with a boom and bounces once** to y662; resettles at **y675 at f174, leaving the 25px hot bar.** Hasp aligned f174, padlock through and closed at **f176**. The padlock is **still rocking on the hasp at f192.**
  - **L9 THE OFFERING AND THE REFUSAL (f110 to f132).** f110 the hero, without breaking his loop, kicks a finished crate out through the mouth to **505, 730**, dead centre. f124 to f132 the black boot shoves it back inside with its side, no look, no pause. The coldest half-second in the scene and it is at the biggest readable size in the frame.
  - **L10 THE SHUTTER FACE, LIVE (f172 to f192).** The steel is the frame now and it never rests. **The slat-gap bars STROBE on the grinder's own rhythm** as the arc fires behind them. **Silhouette shadows rake across the bars** as the hero and the forklift pass. **The forklift's lamp sweeps the bars left to right on every lap.** The face **bulges and rattles on the 11-frame crate-thud cadence** as the jam fires into it, and **dust and paint flakes jump off the face on every thud**. The OPEN neon's colour smudges through the gaps at y290 to 310 and swings. **f184 the dent pops outward and stays.**
  - **L11 CAMERA (f0 to f192).** See camera section. Never static.
  - **AT FRAME 192, and ONLY what the steel actually lets through:** the slat-gap light bars strobing on the grinder's cycle, silhouette shadows raking across them, the forklift's lamp sweeping the bars, the OPEN neon's glow smudging and swinging in the gaps, the shutter face bulging and rattling on the jam cadence, dust and paint flakes still falling off the face, the f184 dent popped out with a second bonk un-landed behind it, the spark spray hosing out under the door onto the apron toward camera, the three outboard rollers spinning empty, the apron delivery stack sitting frozen and finished in the cold, the padlock rocking on the hasp, the code rain falling on the apron where he stood, and the camera still pushing into the keyhole. **The hero, the crates landing, the belts, the clock and the fresh MON page are all behind steel and are NOT claimed.** Nothing has settled and the frame is cut away from mid-motion.

- **Escalation:** The curve fights the scroll, not the previous scene, because this is frame zero of the reel. **f0** is a hard cut from black straight into a grinder at full song, no fade, no logo, no build. **f0 to f104** the energy is *joyful and rising*: the burn accelerates, the calendar strips itself and the drift fans out under it, the buffer climbs, crates ride out and thump onto the apron stack, and it peaks on SUN tearing at **f104**, the moment seven pages are lying on the floor and the audience realises they just watched a full week vanish in one night. **f96 to f118** is the dread inversion and it is the best move in the scene: the music does not swell, it **drains**. Three columns of sickly green start falling **inside the room, over his right shoulder**, while the workshop keeps happily banging away, oblivious. **f110** he offers a crate. **f118** two gags land on the same frame: he starts week two, and his own shadow stands up. **f120 to f172** is three hauls, each louder and heavier, the interior light narrowing from a wide hot rectangle to a band to a slit, so the scene physically gets darker while the work inside gets *faster*. **f124** the offering is booted back, without a look, and that is the coldest beat in the reel's first ten seconds. **f168** the guillotine cuts the delivery line. **f176** the padlock is the hardest transient in the scene and the loudest hit in the first ten seconds. **f176 to f192 deliberately refuses to calm down and now has something concrete to be angry about:** the machine keeps producing at full speed into a sealed wall, the sparks come out under the door, the forklift dents the steel, the rollers spin empty, and the apron stack sits there finished. The last three seconds are not a resolution, they are an injustice that is still happening at full production rate.

- **Gag / pop-culture:** All geometric and iconic, no organic references, no trademarks.
  1. **FACTORY LINE** (approved geometry): the overhead line shaft, leather belts, pulleys, roller chute and the outbound run. Big dumb industrial motion, dense and crisp, running the whole time, and now it is the thing the door betrays.
  2. **MATRIX CODE RAIN** as the villain's tell, exactly per the bible. Sickly yellow-green `#A8B84A`, arrives before he does, and **arrives inside his own room**. First seeding of the reel's antagonist grammar, so when S1 opens on the same green the audience already flinches.
  3. **THE SHADOW THAT STANDS UP:** at f118 the hero's own cast shadow detaches at the shoulder and walks. Pure geometry, zero organic content, and it is the plant that makes S1's peel a payoff instead of an invention.
  4. **KNOCKOFF BRAND:** "NIGHT OWL FAB CO." in buzzing neon, invented, with a stuttering O. Texture, not a joke, and no numeral.
  5. **THE OPEN SIGN NOBODY TURNED OFF:** the neon OPEN oval still lit and still swinging behind the padlocked shutter at f192, glowing out through the slat gaps. Zero reading, universal, and it reinforces the light band.
  6. **THE FORKLIFT WHO DIDN'T GET THE MEMO:** he bonks the freshly locked shutter from behind and **we watch the dent pop toward us**, paint flakes jumping, the padlock hopping on the hasp. Pure Looney Tunes timing, played on the biggest object in frame, and it is funnier than the version we could see because the guy doing it has no idea we exist. It is also the loudest possible proof that the work never stopped.
  7. **LIGHTS ON BEHIND THE SHUTTER:** sparks and arc light spraying out through the slat gaps and under the door after the lock. Everyone has walked past a shut roller door with light leaking under it. Universal, wordless, free, and now it says something sharper: the light gets out, the work does not.

- **Sprite costume:** **HERO:** canonical clay Claude mascot, warm `#D97757`, silhouette unchanged, **eyes visible at all times** (the bible's tell). Head-top: welding goggles pushed **up** on the brow so the eyes read. Body: navy hi-vis coveralls with reflective chevrons, sleeves shoved up, soot smear on one cheek that grows across the scene, the **red L learner badge velcroed to his chest** (the running unlicensed marker that survives to S7 and comes off exactly once, there). Nubs: grinder in one, crate strap in the other. Tool belt with **the sticker gun holstered and unused**. Expression arc: absorbed and happy at f0, still absorbed at f104, absorbed and now mildly baffled by a jam he cannot see the cause of at f192. He never once registers the door, and the door now takes something from him anyway, which is what makes it hurt.
  **VILLAIN:** the `<Villain/>` component, unmodified, staged as a **FULL HARD-BACKLIT SILHOUETTE, committed, not a partial**. From f120 the whole shape is in frame inside the mouth's right third: sharp shoulder block, skinny tie edge, glove, padlock, all **pure black cutout** against the interior glow. That reads "somebody in a suit" instantly and it is a far better thumbnail than an arm in a void. **Explicitly withheld: no shades, no face detail, no earpiece detail, no stamp, no slate `#5A5F6B` tint, no code-rain ON him. Silhouette only.** He is born in S1 by peeling off the hero's body and this scene must not spend that; all S0 plants is that **he came out of the hero's own shadow**, which S1 pays back exactly. He never speaks and stamps nothing. **He gains the padlock and the exit. That is all, and it is enough.**
  **CAMEO, FORKLIFT CLAUDE:** same base sprite, knee-high, hi-vis vest and ear defenders, no face detail needed, permanently busy, blissfully unaware. Every body in frame is a Claude sprite.

- **Camera:** **Lens feel:** wide, roughly a 28mm equivalent, faint barrel distortion on the door rails so the mouth flares slightly and the shutter reads huge. **Key from inside the mouth is harsh white-blue arc `#C8D8E8`** off the grinder, with warm clay `#D97757` bounce only on the crates and smoke inside the rectangle; **the apron carries a cold sodium rim on the wet puddle and nothing else**; blue-black `#12141A` void everywhere around the rectangle with **zero geometry in it**. **Height and axis:** **dead level, head-on, centred, no tilt, axis at y 448**, the mouth's own centre. The emotional geometry is that we are OUTSIDE, which is camera POSITION, not camera height, so this costs nothing and it hands the low raking angle to S1 where the binding fix put it. **Move:** a slow continuous **4 percent push** from f0 to f116, creeping toward the mouth, drawn in by the work. **On the first haul at f120 the camera flinches back 2 percent and drifts 1 percent LEFT**, which opens frame-right for the silhouette to work in and **keeps the hasp dead centre at 505**, where the f176 push and the geometric iris both require it. **The right-hand-third claim is struck: centring is correct and the third was the error.** From **f184 a slow push into the padlock's KEYHOLE**, which becomes the transition. Handheld float of about 1 pixel throughout, plus a 3 pixel shake on each of the three hauls, growing. **Depth stack, four layers, no exterior far layer:** **FOREGROUND OCCLUDER,** near-black pallet stack bottom left and oil drum with coiled air hose bottom right, both soft-blurred, with dust and the odd spark crossing in front of them, plus **the apron delivery stack** sitting cold in the near field. **MIDGROUND ACTION,** the shutter, the hasp, the sill, the roller run, the silhouette, the descending slats. **BACKGROUND,** the interior: hero, spark bench, crate buffer, calendar and its drift, forklift, all sharp and hot. **FAR BACKGROUND,** the interior back wall, the line shaft silhouetted against the glow, the OPEN neon, the smoke haze. Every layer parallaxes at a different rate on the push. **There is no street and there is no facade.**

- **Transition + SFX:** **IN:** no transition. Hard cut from black into f0 with the grinder already screaming. The pattern interrupt is the absence of a ramp. **BEAT LIST:** f0, grinder shriek plus a low industrial sub plus the line shaft's leather slap, already at full level. f8 onward, a soft paper tear every 16 frames plus a crate THUD onto the buffer every 11 frames, the two cadences interleaving into a work groove. **f0 onward, bed: neon buzz, lamp hum, the roller run's continuous rattle (this holds the frequency slot the cut flip-board used to occupy, and it is unique to S0), the forklift's reversing beep looping.** Every ~22f, a crate rumbling down the run and thumping onto the apron stack, close and dry because it is on our side. **f96, the bed drains 6dB** and a thin sickly digital hiss slides in under everything as the first code-rain column falls **inside**. Nothing else changes, which is why it is frightening. f118, a fat paper rip as the spent week comes off the nail. f120, haul one, a steel ratchet plus rolling slats plus a body-shake thud. f124, a single dull scrape as the offered crate is booted back over the sill, no music, no sting, which is what makes it cold. f142, haul two, heavier and lower. **f168, a hard metallic SNAP as the slat shears the roller run.** f172, haul three, the bowed bottom slat BOOMS onto the sill, bounces once and rings. **f176, the padlock KA-CHUNK, the single loudest transient in the scene, with the bed ducking hard around it.** **f178 onward, everything inside comes back muffled and boxy behind the steel and it is still going, and the jam becomes a dull irregular boom on the 11-frame cadence, while out here on our side, unmuffled and bright: the spark spray hissing onto the apron, and three empty rollers rattling with nothing on them.** f184, the forklift bonk, a comic muted clang with a paint-flake tick. f190, a second, smaller bonk starts and does not land. **Transient budget holds: three hauls, one padlock, one bonk; the shear is inside the haul-three envelope and the f190 bonk never lands.** **OUT into S1:** from f184 the camera pushes into the padlock's **KEYHOLE**, which is round, black, dead centre of the hero prop, and pointing at us because the hasp faces us. It is the whole reel in one hole: the hole the keys go in, and our guy does not have them. It swallows the frame at f192, a clean geometric iris to black. The workshop is still audible, muffled, for the first four frames of S1, then cuts dead as S1's line lands.

- **Mute check:** **PASS, comfortably under two seconds.** A stranger with the sound off says: **"A guy is working all night and somebody rolls a steel shutter down across his delivery line and padlocks it, and the work keeps coming and none of it gets out."** The week is countable as seven pages fanned on the floor. The lock is a padlock. The cost is a stack of crates sitting frozen on the apron and three rollers spinning with nothing on them. The light still leaking out under the door tells you he never stopped. There is no header and **no numeral on screen anywhere, which is now true against the set list**: the sign lost its 24, the bay number is a hazard chevron, the flip-board is cut. The only readable things in frame are prop dressing: a neon shop name, a neon OPEN oval, day names on a calendar, short job words stencilled on crates. Delete every word in the frame and the scene still tells itself.

- **Hook into next:** **The object carried is the padlock's keyhole**, which the camera pushes into and which becomes S1's opening black. **The question planted is "why won't they let it out?"**, and S1 answers it immediately and brutally: because of what this thing calls DONE. That is the scene's ONE open loop and nothing competes with it. **The objects planted for the arc, both untouched here:** (1) **the sticker gun holstered on the hero's own belt**, which is the exact weapon the copy peels off him and seizes in S1, so his birth is a payoff and not an invention. (2) **the silhouette that came out of the hero's own shadow**, which is S1's peel promised in geometry and paid four seconds later. **The keys do not appear in this scene at all.** Per the board rules the keys get exactly one home (S3's hook under the wired glass) before their single handover at S7, so S0 plants no ring, no glint and no custody. He arrives holding the padlock, he spends it into the hasp, he walks off having sealed the mouth. The possession is the padlock. The gain is the sealed rectangle.

- **⛔ CRITIC FIXES (binding on the build):**

The slat shears the run at f168. From f176 the machine at the same speed fires crates into the steel, the run jams, the apron stack freezes, and the outboard rollers spin empty. He took the exit, not the capability. The hero's want is legible without him ever looking up: he keeps kicking crates at the run and they keep coming back at him.
  - **Fix 2 + 12, the f192 inventory is now honest.** Deleted from the last-frame claim: hero's arms, crates landing, flaps clacking, clock hands, MON page flapping. New L10 makes the steel a live surface: 4px lit gap between every slat, a motivated 25px hot bar at y675 to 700 under the bowed bottom slat, strobing bars on the grinder's cycle, silhouette shadows raking, the forklift's lamp sweeping, dust jumping off the face on the jam cadence, the f184 dent. The mouth never seals to zero.

The street, the facade and all exterior depth are cut; the void is flat dead black with zero geometry. Interior key pushed to white-blue arc; no orange wash touches the apron before f178. Anti-collision paragraph now argues S1 first and cites S4 correctly (dawn ramp over a pit, tin bot, tarp; the turntable is dead per `61-os.md:113`).

The card's claim that the glint is the reel's central open loop is struck; S0's loop is the door and S1 answers it. **Fix 8's value is transferred to the padlock**, which is now his sole possession: f166 to f172, in the pause after he ducks out and before the hasp, he brings it up in a clean uncontested beat, black against the closing hot band, with nothing competing. **Fix 18's demands are honored in full and transferred**: L7 now has explicit coords, size and blocking; the specular is motivated and dated (f178 the spark spray he just locked away is the first warm light on our side, f180 to f182 one hard held specular on the brass face and the keyhole rim), and the padlock reads as a shape at thumbnail size. **Binding note raised:** the bible's S0 row ("he holds the padlock + the keys on his belt") should be amended to "he holds the padlock" so the reel's key prop keeps one home before its single S7 handover.

Nothing left of x340 sits above y124. Left-hand code rain starts at y380, never above. The f0 loose slat is re-checked and now hangs at y196 to 210, into the mouth, not into the Kicker.
