---

### S1 VILLAIN BORN

- **Takeaway:** The reason nobody hands Claude the keys is not that Claude is slow, it is that a slate copy of Claude peeled off its own back, took the pass-stickers off it, certified a burning car DONE, and then backhanded the row of cars it passed last week so hard they knocked each other down like dominoes. The distrust is earned, live, in one shot.

- **Base object:** **THE COUNTERFEIT DONE STICKER GUN ("the tagger").** A fat orange price-tag gun re-loaded with windshield inspection stickers: chunky moulded body, an exposed spool of die-cut yellow-green stickers on top, a ratchet trigger, a spring-loaded feed foot, a hard rubber head, and a small brass flip-counter on its flank. This scene exists to hand that gun from the hero to the villain, on camera, and it becomes the villain's signature weapon for the rest of the reel. **Ledger (pending the two board rulings in CRITIC FIXES, and written here as the ruling this card ships against): S3 puts a permanent HAIRLINE CRACK across the tagger's head (not a shatter) and JAMS the brass flip-counter, both visible in every later shot of the gun; S6 rejects and bins its output; S7 confiscates and crushes it.** No collision: S2 owns the chrome brake pedal, S3 the rule gate, S4 the turntable bay and the tin bot, S5 the order rail, S6 the brass press, S7 the laminator, S8 the revolving stage, S9 the manual. The verb here is unique too: **STICKER**, never stamp, drive, inspect or grade. It is the only handheld weapon in the reel and the only prop that changes owner.

- **Story / metaphor:** THE SHOP THAT PASSES ITS OWN WORK, and the moment it grows a second employee. Our rookie is a beaming night-shift mechanic on a strip-mall forecourt, sincerely happy, tagging his own repairs. Then the reel's thesis happens as a physical event: a **slate copy peels off his back like a sticker coming off its backing paper**, because that is exactly what he is, a self-issued pass with a body. Same silhouette, no eyes, black suit. He peels off on the hero's LEFT, the fire side, so he is born INTO the key light with a hard rim-lit gap of flame opening between the two overlapping bodies, and a glossy white hero-shaped BACKING-PAPER LINER curls off the hero's back with him and flutters to the apron, where it lies for the rest of the scene. He takes the gun out of the hero's nub without a word, walks the wreck left to right tagging it DONE, thwacks a sticker onto the last-week row's lead car and backhands its rear quarter with the same swing so the whole row knocks itself over, shoves the hero onto the oil slick, tags the man he just shoved, and puts the last sticker on the glass in your face. This is the villain's **PEAK POWER, pre-S3, so he loses nothing and gains everything: the weapon, the authority to certify, the key light and the frame itself.** What the HERO loses is the point: not his skill, his **say**. He can still do the work; he no longer holds the pass. Every scene after S3 walks this backwards, prop by prop, until S7 takes this same gun off him and crushes it. **CHRONOLOGY, declared and binding: S0's locker is never a body. It is a slate nub and one shades-glint in the shutter's shadow, no silhouette, no code-rain, no keys on any belt. S1's VO is past tense, so S1 is the receipt, story-time earlier than S0, and it is the answer to who that nub was.** The villain's entry state here is therefore absolute: he enters as NOTHING, still inside the hero at f0, and exits owning everything.

- **Set detail (Alex's #1 note):** Night strip-mall forecourt, open apron under sodium light, the engine fire is the key light and paints the whole frame orange. Panel-local coords, origin top-left, 1012x792. Deliberately opposite S4 (cold blue dawn interior, high angle) so the two never twin at thumbnail size. Cars here are **props, never characters**: no eyes, no faces, they fold like furniture. **Texture pass, everywhere, not a numbered prop:** soot bloom on the facade above the fire, a rust weep under the gantry, tape residue ghosting the glass, ash grit in the apron cracks, worn chalk bay outlines, arrows and hatched boxes half scrubbed away with **zero digits and no resolvable glyphs anywhere on the ground**. **UNIT 04 is an author-side label only: "04" is never painted, plated, stencilled or stickered anywhere on the wreck.**

  1. **THE TAGGER (base object)** starts in the hero's left nub at **(392, 500)**, ends locked in the villain's nub at **(612, 468)**. Its spool visibly unwinds all scene, its feed foot clacks on every shot, its brass flip-counter on the flank is the scene's ONE number, ticking **03 → 09 across exactly seven shots, one flip per shot, no gaps**: 03 fender f0 (hero) · 04 bare hub f70 · 05 burning hood f78 · 06 taped door f86 · 07 car1's glass f104 · 08 the hero's chest f140 · 09 the panel glass f150, mid-flip at the cut. The sticker die itself carries **no legible characters**: the mono serial line and the punched month wheel drop to unreadable micro-hatching and a die-cut punched arc. The word DONE is a die on a prop, not copy.
  2. **UNIT 04, the wreck**, three-quarter front, **x 40-330, y 372-608**. Hood popped to 80 degrees and hanging on one hinge, bouncing on its spring the entire scene. It is the thing being certified. **It is also the S4 carry, and its silhouette signature is locked here in three parts: (a) the hood at 80 degrees on ONE hinge, (b) the bare hub with the disc still lazily spinning, (c) the villain's fat crooked sticker 05 smeared across the hood with its corner curled by the heat.** Those three are the first things the tin bot pulls the tarp off at S4.
  3. **THE FLAME COLUMN**, base **(150, 400)**, licking to **y 150** on a flat-geometric 6-plane flicker. It DOES the lighting: every lick re-keys the rim light and swings the cast shadows across the apron. Growth cycle 1.4x every ~40f, **held flat f24 to f36 so the peel owns the frame**, hood belch on the hero's rag beat at f116, jumps to a rear tyre at f138 so the last half-second is escalating. Its climb from f100 is the motivation for the row's re-lighting (item 12).
  4. **THE BARE HUB**, front-left corner of the wreck at **(60, 580)**. Wheel already gone, brake disc still lazily spinning on its stub, wobbling, never stopping. It says "not finished" without a word. Takes **sticker 04** at f70. S4 carry (b).
  5. **THE TAPED DOOR** at **(300, 470)**: the driver door hangs off on two crossed strips of gaffer tape, with an old DONE sticker already slapped over the tape. At f86 the villain thwacks **sticker 06 on top of the first one**, double-certifying a door held on with tape, and the door swings on the impact. It also swings a few degrees on every other impact in the scene.
  6. **THE ESCAPING WHEEL**, enters at **(330, 620)** on f22, rolls a long slow arc toward camera-left and dies in the clear gap between the tyre stack's right edge (x 140) and the oil slick's left edge (x 200), doing its Looney-Tunes spinning-coin wobble at **(238, 690)** at the cut, still wobbling, the stack cropping behind it.
  7. **THE OIL SLICK**, **(200-560, 640-700)**, a black mirror holding the fire's reflection and rippling on every THWACK. Planted banana skin, and it pays twice: the dead extinguisher rolls into it at f106 and rocks there until f154, and the hero's shove at f133 lands him on it at f137.
  8. **POST 2**, the sodium pole, base **(110, 620)**, head at **(130, 110)**. Buzzing, one bad ballast making the cone stutter, dust and ash ripping upward through the beam continuously (frozen f24-f36 for the peel). A moth cooks itself on the lens and drops at f66.
  9. **THE JIFFY LOOB OVAL**, invented brand, chunky yellow-and-red lozenge on the shop facade at **(610, 96) to (900, 176)**. **SHAPE ONLY: no tagline, no readable words, zero legible characters.** One dead letter tube flickers on a broken 7-frame cycle all scene. It reads as a logo shape at thumbnail size. The CERTIFIED IN 5 MINUTES / NOW 20 MINUTES WE LEARNED escalation is S5's to spend; S1 is the setup and only needs the shape.
  10. **THE LAST WEEK GANTRY SIGN**, **(680, 236) to (930, 292)**, swinging on two chains off a bar at y 210. **This is the scene's one HEADER.** Plain, no working parts of its own beyond the four hooks along its bottom edge.
  11. **THE FOUR PASS TAGS**, hanging from those hooks, one above each car in the row: **(700, 300) · (790, 296) · (860, 292) · (912, 288)**. Small die-cut yellow-green paddles, **no glyphs**, chunky enough to read as tags at thumbnail size, swinging constantly. Working machinery: **when a car folds, its tag unhooks, falls, clips the car's shoulder and CLATTERS onto the apron where we watch it land.** Tag 1 lands big and readable in the midground at **(640, 602)**, tag 2 at **(836, 554)**, tag 3 at **(924, 520)**. Three tags on the apron by the cut, **car4's still hanging and still swinging.**
  12. **THE LAST WEEK ROW.** **Car1 is MIDGROUND, in the villain's arm's reach, on his exact blur and exposure plane: three-quarter REAR view facing away into the depth, centre (740, 478), w300, near rear quarter at (606, 504), rear glass at (660, 432). Zero blur, full exposure, never dimmed.** Behind it the row recedes to a vanishing point at **(1000, 452)**: car2 **(872, 434)** w176, car3 **(950, 412)** w118, car4 **(996, 398)** w88 with its right edge cropped by the panel. Each wears a faded yellow-green sticker on its glass, so you can see they were passed by the same gun. **The row carries 1.5px blur and sits 18% dim until f100, then lifts to 0.5px and full exposure across f100 to f154 as the flame column climbs and re-keys them.** By the time they fall you can see them fall.
  13. **THE LONELY HAZARD LAMP** on car3 at **(940, 430)**. Starts blinking at f130 with an audible relay tick and is still ticking on the last frame. One small amber pulse in the dark.
  14. **THE EXTINGUISHER ON ITS STAND**, **(566, 520-620)**, already wearing a faded DONE sticker, pressure needle buried in the red, seal broken, hissing a thin useless plume from a cracked valve. **It is the hero's second loss and it is used, hard (LAYER HERO).** After f106 it lies in the oil slick at (520, 660), still hissing, rocking, until f154.
  15. **THE TOOL TROLLEY + MUG + THE FOLDED TARP.** Trolley at **(470, 600)**, half-drunk mug at **(486, 588)**, and **the TARP folded and soot-edged on the lower shelf at (462, 614)**. The mug's surface rings on every gun THWACK and gets a fat lurch on the backhand at f106. A loose 17mm socket rolls off the top tray at f118. **The tarp is the S4 plant and nobody touches it in S1: the villain loses nothing here, so the concealment happens off screen between S3 and S4, exactly when he starts needing to hide.**
  16. **THE TYRE STACK**, foreground occluder, **(0-140, 660-792)**, blurred ~2px, cropping the frame's bottom-left corner. It shudders when the hero hits the deck, and one tyre rocks off the top **at f146**, a separate later beat that never fires on the same frame as anything else.
  17. **THE BACKING-PAPER TAIL**, the spent sticker liner streaming out of the gun's throat and coiling into a growing snake on the apron at **(500, 660)**, feeding the roll-bin. It gets longer every single shot and is still feeding at the cut. Free proof of volume.
  18. **THE ROLL-BIN OF SPENT LINER**, **(430, 690)**: an open drum the tail (17) feeds into, **already overflowing at f0 with a week of spent liner** and visibly heaping higher on every shot. The volume of counterfeit passes is a physical mass in the frame before the villain is even born.
  19. **THE LINER SILHOUETTE.** The glossy white hero-shaped negative that peels off the hero's back with the copy at f30, flutters down through the flame wash, and lands face-up on the apron at **(500, 660)** beside the tail and the bin, where it stays for the rest of the scene. It is what makes the peel legible instead of poetic: sticker, liner, and the thing it came off, all three in frame at once.
  20. **CODE-RAIN, PARENTED TO THE VILLAIN.** Four thin columns of sickly yellow-green glyphs declared as **offsets from his body origin: x-24, x+32, x+88, x+136**, falling from y 0 down to his shoulder line, travelling with him for the entire walk. **Born f30 at 2 columns, thin and faint. Third column at f56 on the rip. Fourth at f70 on his first DONE. Full opacity by f104 as he takes the row.** The fixed-x spec (columns settling to 506-centred) applies ONLY to the f142-f154 turn-to-lens, where he fills mid-frame.
  21. **CAMEO, THE ATTENDANT**, foreground-right in a cracked plastic chair at **(880, 600-720)**, big and readable. Claude sprite, headset on, steaming mug in one nub, sitting calm in the fire wash with a thumbs-up: the unnamed this-is-fine parody. He does not move for the fire. He flinches once, hard, at the backhand on f106, and slowly raises the mug higher.
  22. **CAMEO, THE CUSTOMER**, small, at **(952, 520)**, in front of the row and below it, arms up, backing out of the exit lane one step per 20 frames, gone past the panel edge by f150. The audience surrogate leaving the building.
  23. **POST 2's DEBRIS CIRCLE**: the apron directly under the lamp head, littered with a season of cooked moths, dead tube glass and yellow-green liner scraps that lift and skate on every whump. Wear that moves.

- **CONTINUOUS ACTION (fills the whole line):** Nine named layers run independently on their own clocks. None of them lands on a resolved pose.

  **LAYER FIRE (f0 to f154, never decays):** flame column on a continuous flicker, growing 1.4x every ~40f, **growth held flat f24 to f36 so the peel owns the frame**, hood-pop belch on f116 into the hero's rag, jumps to a rear tyre at f138 so the last half-second is escalating. Smoke rolls up-left through Post 2's beam for the entire scene. From f100 its climb is the on-screen reason the LAST WEEK row is being re-lit.
  **LAYER ROW (f0 to f154):** parallax drift and heat shimmer over car1 and the three receding cars from frame 0, before anything touches them. Blur and exposure ramp at f100 (1.5px/18% dim → 0.5px/full) on the fire's growth.
  **LAYER FORECOURT (f0 to f154):** sodium buzz-stutter, ash motes (frozen f24-f36), the JIFFY LOOB dead tube, the extinguisher hiss, the swinging gantry sign and its four swinging tags, the moth at f66, the socket at f118, the debris circle skating.
  **LAYER COUNTER (f0 to f154):** the tagger's flip-counter, 03 → 09, one flip per shot, seven shots, mid-flip at the cut.
  **LAYER WHEEL (f22 to f154):** the escaped wheel's arc, dying into its coin-wobble at (238, 690), still wobbling at f154, clear of the tyre stack.
  **LAYER LINER (f0 to f154):** the tail lengthening out of the gun's throat on every shot, the roll-bin heaping, the hero-shaped liner silhouette landing at f38 and lying there forever.
  **LAYER RAIN (f30 to f154):** villain code-rain, parented, 2 columns → 3 at f56 → 4 at f70 → full opacity at f104.
  **LAYER CAMEO (f0 to f154):** attendant's mug, his one flinch at f106, the mug raised higher after; customer's retreat, gone by f150.
  **LAYER ACTION:**
  - **f0 to f18, HERO ON FRAME 0, ACTING.** Open mid-THWACK: hero's nub slams the tagger onto the wreck's rear fender, **sticker 03** bites, ratchet recoils, counter flips, the tail jumps a length into the bin, he grins and half-turns toward the flaming hood. No ramp, no establishing beat. **This is his only shot. The gun leaves him before he fires again.**
  - **f18 to f44, THE PEEL. STAGED LEFT, INTO THE FIRE.** A die-cut notch line opens down the hero's flame side. On f24 a **slate copy starts sliding out of him sideways, toward the flame column at (150, 400)**, peeling like a sticker off backing paper: two overlapping silhouettes pulling apart with a hard rim-lit gap of firelight opening between them, the copy lifting away at a 12 degree tilt, then snapping flat and standing up at **(330, 505)**, backlit-free and fully keyed by the fire. On f30 a **glossy white hero-shaped LINER** curls off the hero's back with him, flutters down through the wash and lands at (500, 660) on f38. Shades resolve last, on f40, with a single hard white glint bar. **The hero never sees it, and he does not fire during it: f24 to f36 the flame growth is held and the ash motes freeze, so every other layer stays alive but nothing competes with the one image the reel cannot afford to lose.**
  - **f44 to f56, THE SEIZE.** The villain's nub closes over the tagger at **(356, 496)**. One beat of resistance, the hero's grip pulls back for 4 frames, then the gun **rips free** on f56 and the hero's nub is left grabbing air, still opening and closing on nothing until f70.
  - **f56 to f86, THE CERTIFICATION, ONE CONTINUOUS LEFT-TO-RIGHT TRAVERSE. Declared waypoints, binding, no jumps:** (356, 496) f56 → (300, 516) f62 → **(150, 524) f68**, the wreck's front-left corner, where he plants and works with his arm, not his body. **f70 he reaches down-left and thwacks sticker 04 onto THE BARE HUB (60, 580).** **f78 he reaches up-right and thwacks sticker 05 onto THE BURNING HOOD (250, 400): fat, crooked, ink smeared, corner already curling in the heat. This is the money shot and the S4 carry.** Body steps right f78 to f86, (150, 520) → (270, 486). **f86 he thwacks sticker 06 onto THE TAPED DOOR (300, 470), on top of the sticker already there, double-certifying a door held on with tape; the door swings.** Unhurried throughout, the walk of a man who cannot be late.
  - **f72 to f126, LAYER HERO, THE SECOND LOSS (the hero is never parked).** **f72** he breaks right for the extinguisher stand. **f80** he rips it off its bracket. **f88** he squeezes and gets nothing: needle buried in the red, seal broken, and a DONE sticker on the body. It was certified full and it is empty. **f94** he shakes it and squeezes again: **f98** one weak grey cough of powder that the fire eats. **f100 the villain walks straight past him without a glance.** **f106** he drops it; it clatters, rolls into the oil slick and rocks at (520, 660) until f154. **f106 to f118** he snatches the rag from his pocket and beats at the hood fire; **f116** the hood belches and drives him back a step, rag smoking. **f120 to f126** he turns and finally SEES the row folding, the only look either character gives it, eyes wide.
  - **f86 to f104, THE TRAVERSE.** One unbroken unhurried walk right: (270, 486) → (360, 478) f90 → (470, 484) f96 → **(566, 476) f100, passing the extinguisher stand while the hero squeezes a dead can, no glance, no pause** → **(612, 468) f104**, arriving at car1's near rear quarter. Code-rain travels with him the whole way.
  - **f104 to f106, STICKER 07 AND THE BACKHAND, ONE GESTURE.** **f104 he thwacks sticker 07 onto CAR1's rear glass, on top of its faded existing sticker: re-certifying the thing he is about to destroy.** The follow-through of that same swing is the backhand: **f106 the back of his nub lands on CAR1's rear quarter at (606, 504), on contact, with contempt.** He does not look at it. He never looks at it.
  - **f106 to f154, THE DOMINO, WITH CLEAN AIR.** Nothing else big happens in the frame until f126. **Car1 rolls away from the blow f106 to f114**, rear quarter buckling on f108, **tag 1 unhooks f112, clips car1's shoulder and clatters onto the apron at (640, 602) on f116.** **Car1's nose hits CAR2 at f114**, car2's roof folds inward f118, **tag 2 drops f122, lands (836, 554) f126.** **Car2 hits CAR3 at f126**, car3 sinks onto its rims f130 and its hazard lamp starts ticking, **tag 3 drops f134, lands (924, 520) f138.** **Car3 hits CAR4 at f140, car4's sag begins f144 and it is STILL FOLDING at the cut, tag 4 still hanging.** Every fold is caused by the collision one car up the line. Soft, undramatic, apologetic, dust drifting up for a full second after each.
  - **f126 to f140, THE LUNGE, THE SHOVE AND THE WORST STICKER.** Hero lunges from **(470, 485)** on f126, both nubs out for the gun. The villain does not turn his head. He puts one flat nub on the hero's chest on **f133** and shoves; camera snap-back and a 2 degree roll on the same frame. Hero hits the oil at **f137** at (540, 670), boots up. **f140: without looking, without breaking his stance, the villain reaches down and thwacks STICKER 08 onto the hero's chest.** He certifies the man he just shoved. It is still crooked on his chest at the cut, and it is the gun S7 crushes.
  - **f142 to f154, THE GLASS.** The villain turns to lens, fills the mid-frame, code-rain settling to 506-centred, raises the tagger past the camera and **thwacks sticker 09 onto the panel glass in your face on f150**. At **f154** the sticker is still smearing down the glass and has not adhered, his arm has not come back down, the hero is still sliding on the oil toward (300, 720) with his cap coming off and sticker 08 on his chest, car4 is still folding, tag 4 is still on its hook, the wheel is still wobbling at (238, 690), the counter is mid-flip, the tail is still feeding the bin, the fire is still climbing the rear tyre, the extinguisher is still rocking and hissing in the oil, and the hazard lamp is still ticking. Hard cut on motion. Nothing settles.

- **Escalation:** False triumph (f0-18, it looks for half a second like a competent shop) → dread (f18-44, something is coming out of him, into the firelight, and he cannot see it) → theft (f44-56, the first real violence is silent and takes 4 frames) → obscenity (f56-86, DONE goes onto a spinning bare hub, a burning hood and a taped-on door, confidence rising while the object degrades) → the hero's private humiliation (f72-106, he goes for the extinguisher and the counterfeit disarms him: the can wearing a pass does not work) → quiet horror (f106-126, the loud half stops, the frame hands the row twenty clean frames and full exposure, and the things that used to work knock each other down one at a time) → assault (f126-140, the hero is removed from his own scene and then tagged where he lies) → a direct dare to you (f142-154). The curve does not peak and hold, it hands off: every time one engine cools another lights, and three are still accelerating at the cut. Lighting escalates with it: the hero owns the key light at f0, loses it to the copy the instant it peels into the fire, the copy carries it across the frame on the traverse, and by f150 the hero is a silhouette on the ground behind him.

- **Gag / pop-culture:** All geometric, all knockoff, no organic references, no real marks. (1) **THE STICKER PEEL as the duplicate-slide, and the ONLY peel in the entire reel.** The bible's Smith-style copy is rendered as the scene's own material: a die-cut notch, a backing-paper release, a peel at a 12 degree tilt, a snap flat, and a hero-shaped liner on the floor. The villain literally is a self-issued pass that grew legs. (2) **MATRIX CODE-RAIN**, mandated, but yellow-green, sickly, parented to his body in 4 hard columns instead of a curtain, so it reads as an aura not as wallpaper. (3) **JIFFY LOOB**, the invented strip-mall lube brand, as a yellow-and-red oval SHAPE with a dead tube and no words. (4) **THIS IS FINE**, never named: the attendant Claude in the plastic chair with the mug and the thumbs-up, sitting in the flame wash. (5) **THE LOONEY-TUNES CAR DOMINO**: one contemptuous backhand, four cars knocking each other over in a staggered chain, four pass tags raining off their hooks. (6) **DUCT-TAPE REPAIR**: crossed tape holding the door on, with a DONE sticker over the tape, and then a second DONE sticker over the first. (7) **THE SPINNING-COIN WHEEL EXIT.** (8) **THE DEAD EXTINGUISHER**: a fire extinguisher wearing a pass, needle in the red, coughing one grey puff the fire eats. The reel's thesis performed instead of narrated. (9) **THE STATE-INSPECTION STICKER** as authority prop: die-cut notches, micro-hatching, a punched arc, a tiny fake seal, all crooked and smeared, zero legible characters, because it is counterfeit and should look counterfeit.

- **Sprite costume:** **HERO:** canonical clay Claude, warm `#D97757`, silhouette unchanged. **The workshop COVERALL carried unchanged from S0's night shift**, worn open over the **hi-vis chevron vest** from that shift, blank oval name patch, rag in the pocket, greasy backwards trucker cap with a flipped-up welding visor on the brim, **the red L badge velcroed to the chest** (the safest identity token in the reel: it survives to S7's tear and S9's corkboard), a soot smear on one cheek that grows through the scene. **No racing suit, no white collar, no white sleeve-cuff: the dragster is cut and nothing on this sprite may reference it.** **Eyes always visible.** Arc: sincere beam (f0) → an unbothered blink as something moves beside him (f30) → the first honest confusion of the whole reel when his nub closes on nothing (f58) → hard resolve as he breaks for the extinguisher (f74) → disbelief at the dead squeeze (f88) → desperation on the shake (f94) → fury with the rag (f110) → the belch drives him back (f116) → alarm, seeing the row for the first time (f122) → flat on his back, cap gone, eyes wide, wearing his own counterfeit pass (f154). Never smug, never evil, sincerely happy at the top, which is what makes it unnerving. **VILLAIN (per the bible, use `<Villain/>`, never hand-roll):** the canonical Claude mascot, **slate `#5A5F6B`**, opaque black wraparound shades with **one hard white glint bar and never any eyes**, black jacket with sharp shoulder blocks, skinny black tie over a white collar, coiled earpiece wire down the neck (2 stacked rects + a curl), mouth a flat line only in the f142 turn, **never speaks, only stickers**, weapon = the seized tagger loaded with `#A8B84A` ink, aura = 4 parented columns of code-rain, always on and always with him. He does NOT multiply here; that is S5's one card. He stands ~6% taller than the hero from f104 onward, sold by the low camera, not by a scale change. **CAMEO 1, attendant Claude:** headset, plastic chair, mug, thumbs-up. **CAMEO 2, customer Claude:** small satchel, nubs up, retreating.

- **Camera:** Wide anamorphic feel, 28mm equivalent, slight barrel and a subtle horizontal flare off the sodium lamp whenever the flame column spikes. **Height: knee height on the apron**, eyeline around y 640, so the hero is heroic at f0 and the villain is a monolith by f104 and you are lying in the oil with the hero at the end. **Move:** a slow continuous 4% push-in across the whole 155f, with a 6px handheld drift so nothing is ever nailed down; a 3px shake on each THWACK; **a hold on the drift f24 to f36 so the peel is the only thing moving toward you**; one hard snap-back and a 2 degree roll on the shove at f133; and the push accelerates to 9% over the last 18 frames as the villain comes at lens, so the frame is closing on you when the sticker hits the glass. **Depth stack, three planes always moving at three different rates. FOREGROUND OCCLUDER** = the tyre stack cropping bottom-left, the escaping wheel crossing lens-left, drifting smoke, the attendant's chair-back bottom-right, all blurred ~2px. **MIDGROUND ACTION** = wreck, fire, hero, villain, tagger, oil slick, trolley + tarp, roll-bin + liner tail + liner silhouette, the extinguisher stand, **and CAR1, which lives here from frame 0 at zero blur and full exposure, the same plane as the villain, which is why the backhand is an event and not a rumour. From f100 the rest of the row joins this plane's exposure (0.5px, full) as the fire climbs.** **BACKGROUND** = car2/car3/car4 receding to (1000, 452), the gantry sign, the four pass tags, the JIFFY LOOB oval, Post 2's cone, the customer leaving. **The tags are the one element that crosses planes on purpose: they unhook in the background and clatter to rest in the midground, dragging the eye down into the readable plane on every fold.**

- **Transition + SFX:** **IN: the previous frame is wiped by a yellow-green DONE sticker THWACKING down flat over it**, which lands us mid-THWACK at f0 and matches the f0 cue, with the room tone dropping out for 3 frames first so the first hit lands naked. **Every peel, every backing-paper release and every adhesive tear in the entire reel is reserved for f18-f44 and nothing else. One peel, one birth.** **Beat list:** f0 rubbery **THWACK-ka-chunk** plus a bright chirpy task-complete DING, deliberately clashing with what we see. f24 a long **peeling adhesive tear**, wet and slow, the ugliest sound in the reel, previewed by nothing. f38 the liner's dry paper flutter and settle. f40 a single cold **glass-glint chime** as the shades resolve, and the code-rain bed fades in underneath and never leaves. f56 a **rip** plus a nasty ratchet snap as the gun changes hands. f70, f78, f86 THWACK, THWACK, THWACK, cadence tightening from ~0.55s to ~0.4s, with a sizzle-curl on f78 as the hood sticker's corner cooks and a hinge creak on f86 as the door swings. f88 a **dead pneumatic clack**, the loudest anticlimax in the reel. f98 a weak powder cough the fire swallows. f106 the extinguisher clatter and roll, a low **leathery whump** for the backhand, and a metal bang off car1's rear quarter, all inside four frames. Then the chain, soft and apologetic against it: a metal groan (f108), a hollow bump (f114), a dusty crumple (f118), a bump (f126), a suspension sigh (f130) with a **hazard relay tick-tick-tick that starts at f130 and never stops**, a bump (f140), a slow crumple beginning (f144). Each tag landing is a tiny dry click (f116, f126, f138). f116 a **fwoomp** as the hood belches. f133 a dull chest thud. f137 an oil-slide squeal. **f140 a close, soft, wet THWACK on the hero's chest, the nastiest sound in the scene.** f146 the tyre rumble off the stack. f150 the loudest hit of the scene: a **flat, dead THWACK on the glass**, close-mic'd, no reverb, plus the counter's brass flip. Bed the whole thing under a rising crackling fire, the sodium ballast buzz, the cracked extinguisher's thin hiss and a distant radio. **OUT:** hard cut on the smear, on motion, mid-arm.

- **Mute check:** **"A shady copy of the mechanic stole his sticker gun, slapped DONE on a burning car, then backhanded the row he passed last week and knocked them all over."** One sentence, no VO, and it now scores BOTH halves of the line: the broken thing called done, and the working things wrecked. The two greens tell the rest without reading: the pass is sickly yellow-green and crooked, so it looks fake before you know it is fake. Kill every word on the panel and the frame still says it: a peel with a liner on the floor, a theft, a fire wearing a pass, an extinguisher wearing a pass that does not work, a car knocking over the car behind it, and a shove. **Text budget, declared and held: ONE header (the LAST WEEK gantry sign). ONE number (the tagger's flip-counter, 09).** Everything else is shape or texture: DONE is a die on a prop, the JIFFY LOOB oval has no words, the pass tags have no glyphs, the sticker's serial and month wheel are micro-hatching and a punched arc, the name patch is a blank oval, the apron chalk is outlines and arrows with zero digits, and UNIT 04 is an author-side label that is never painted on the car. No chat bubble, no headline, no ELAPSED counter, no captions echo.

- **Hook into next:** **The sticker on the glass, and the gun that nobody took back.** The open loop is not "what went wrong," S1 just answered that. It is **"then who stops him?"**, asked while his arm is still up and the man he tagged is still sliding. S2 gives the ugly answer: you do, personally, with your foot, forever. The carried object is literal: that crooked yellow-green sticker is still smeared on the glass when we cut, and S2 reveals the glass was a **windscreen**, the learner car's, with your oversized boot already over the second brake and the villain riding the back seat with the tagger still in his nub. The gun does not leave his grip again until S7 crushes it, and it carries S3's hairline crack and jammed counter from S3 onward. Per the board ruling, **no keys are thrown, shown or handed over here.** The keys do not exist until S3 puts them behind glass, and they change hands exactly once, at S7.

- **⛔ CRITIC FIXES (binding on the build):**
  - **Backhand given a physical transmission.** Car1 moved to the midground at (740, 478) w300, zero blur, full exposure, the villain's own plane, near rear quarter at (606, 504) inside his reach. f106 he backhands it ON CONTACT and car1 hits car2 hits car3 hits car4, one collision causing the next fold. Row blur lifts to 0.5px and full exposure f100-f154, motivated on screen by the flame column's climb.
  - **Sticker 06 taken off the fire.** No sticker ever touches a flame. 06 lands on THE TAPED DOOR at f86, on top of the sticker already there, and the door swings. The burning-hood sticker (05, f78, corner curling) is the money shot and is not repeated.
  - **Tick-holes replaced with objects.** Four die-cut yellow-green PASS TAGS on hooks under the LAST WEEK sign, no glyphs, one per car. Each unhooks on its car's fold, clips the car's shoulder and CLATTERS onto the apron in the midground at declared coords. Three down by the cut, car4's still hanging.
  - **The peel restaged left, into the key light**, separating against the flame column with a rim-lit gap opening between two silhouettes; a glossy hero-shaped LINER curls off and lands at (500, 660) for the rest of the scene; flame growth held and ash motes frozen f24-f36 so the reel's thesis image owns the frame for 12 frames. Villain then carries the key light right across the traverse.
  - **Domino and assault separated in time.** Clean air f106-f126 where the row is the only big new motion; lunge f126, shove f133, oil f137. The hero's business in that window is deliberately small, low-contrast and on the opposite side of the frame.
  - **Sticker 08 and the counter math fixed, and BOTH proposed homes shipped.** The two 08 proposals collided (car1's glass vs the hero's chest). Resolved by cutting the hero's old sticker 04 during the peel so he fires exactly ONCE (03 at f0), which frees two slots: **03 fender f0 · 04 hub f70 · 05 hood f78 · 06 door f86 · 07 car1's glass f104 (folded into the backhand's own swing, re-certifying what he is about to destroy) · 08 the hero's chest f140 (he certifies the man he just shoved) · 09 the glass f150.** Seven shots, seven flips, spine 03 to 09 intact, mid-flip at the cut. Cutting the hero's peel-time shot also pays fix 4.
  - **Numbers re-indexed to the walk, props unchanged.** The v1 label assignments (07 on the hub, 05 named as the hood carry) could not both survive a monotone left-to-right traverse. Props and geometry preserved exactly as ruled; the hub is 04 and the hood is 05, so the S4 carry sticker keeps its named number.
  - **Car1's faded pass reads on its REAR GLASS, not its windscreen**, because car1 is a three-quarter rear view facing into the row. Sticker 07 lands there and the follow-through backhands the rear quarter directly below it. Same beat, physical.
  - **Text budget held at one header + one number.** JIFFY LOOB = shape only, tagline killed (the CERTIFIED IN 5 MINUTES escalation is S5's to spend). Apron chalk = outlines, arrows, hatch marks, zero digits. Sticker serial and month wheel = micro-hatching and a punched arc. Name patch = blank oval. UNIT 04 = author-side label only, never painted, plated or stencilled on the car.
  - **Item 20 replaced with a prop that does something.** THE ROLL-BIN OF SPENT LINER at (430, 690), overflowing at f0, heaping on every shot. Grime demoted to a texture note in the set-detail preamble. Added the liner silhouette (19) and the debris circle (23).
  - **⛔ ESCALATED TO THE BOARD, S0 must lose the villain's BODY.** S1 is built on the birth and the bible agrees ("born on screen in S1 by peeling off the hero's own body"), but the locked arc's S0 row puts him on screen a scene earlier. Ruling requested in S1's favour: **S0's lock is closed by a single slate nub and one shades-glint in the shutter's shadow. No silhouette, no shades reveal, no code-rain, no keys on any belt.** S1's card declares the chronology (S1 is past tense, the receipt, story-time earlier than S0) and carries it. If the board refuses, this card cannot ship as written: it would have to restage f18-f44 as a SECOND copy, which pre-spends S5's one multiplication card.
  - **Villain teleport killed.** Certification re-ordered into one continuous left-to-right traverse with declared waypoints: (356,496) f56 → (300,516) f62 → (150,524) f68 → hub f70 → hood f78 → step to (270,486) f86 → door f86 → (360,478) f90 → (470,484) f96 → (566,476) f100 → (612,468) f104. Backhand slid to f106 and the fold chain to car1 f108 / tag1 f112, car2 f118 / tag2 f122, car3 f130 (hazard) / tag3 f134, car4 sag f144 still folding at the cut. The waypoints are binding so the author cannot re-introduce the jump.
  - **The hero's 48-frame hole spent on the extinguisher, and "Nobody uses it" deleted.** f72 break, f80 rip off the bracket, f88 dead squeeze (needle in the red, seal broken, a DONE sticker on the body), f94 shake, f98 one grey cough the fire eats, f106 dropped into the oil where it rocks and hisses until f154. Then f106-f118 he beats the hood fire with the rag and the f116 belch drives him back, f120-f126 he turns and sees the row. Expression arc filled at f74, f88, f94, f110, f116, f122. His want is legible for the whole scene (put it out, get the gun back) and his failure is caused on screen by the counterfeit itself.
  - **Code-rain parented to the villain's transform**, offsets x-24, x+32, x+88, x+136 from his body origin, falling to his shoulder line, travelling with him. 2 columns f30, 3rd f56, 4th f70, full opacity f104. Fixed-x 506-centred only for the f142-f154 turn.
  - **Foreground corner collision cleared.** Wheel dies at (238, 690), in the gap between the tyre stack (x 140) and the oil slick (x 200), stack cropping behind it. The tyre rocks off the stack at f146, not f134. Hero's slide terminates at ~(300, 720). Three separate silhouettes left to right at the cut, no dark smear.
  - **Identity anchor re-pointed at v2's S0.** Carried garment is the workshop coverall worn open over the hi-vis chevron vest, plus the red L badge. The white racing collar and sleeve-cuff are cut entirely; the dragster does not exist.
  - **The peel no longer fires twice.** IN transition restored to a DONE sticker THWACKING down flat over the previous frame, matching f0. Every peel, liner release and adhesive tear in the reel is reserved for f18-f44.
  - **UNIT 04 planted as the S4 reveal.** Three-part silhouette signature locked and named: hood at 80 degrees on one hinge, bare hub with the disc still spinning, sticker 05 smeared across the hood with a heat-curled corner. The TARP is planted here, folded and soot-edged on the trolley's lower shelf at (462, 614), and the villain never touches it in S1 (he loses nothing here); the concealment happens off screen between S3 and S4.
  - **⛔ ESCALATED TO THE BOARD, two ledger contradictions.** (a) **S3 = a hairline CRACK across the tagger's head, not a shatter**, visible in every later shot, with the brass flip-counter JAMMED from S3 onward so the damage is legible and permanent. Amend bible line 38 to match line 27, otherwise S6 cannot reject it and S7 cannot confiscate and crush it. (b) **Strip the keys off the villain's S0 belt** (he keeps the padlock, which is the better image: he does not need the keys, he needs the lock). The keys do not exist until S3 puts them behind glass and they change hands exactly once, at S7. S1 cannot ship a Chekhov chain the arc breaks in two places.

---

### S2 BOTTLENECK

- **Takeaway:** You are the only thing standing between Claude and disaster, and your one control does not reach the thing that actually needs stopping. Your boot stops the car. It does not stop the stamp. And every time your boot goes down, the line behind you gets longer, so the guard IS the jam.

- **Base object:** THE CHROME DUAL-CONTROL INSTRUCTOR'S BRAKE PEDAL, hero-lit in the side-cutaway cabin of the reel's ONE learner car. An aftermarket bolt-on: a raw welded steel rod punched through a hacked hole in the firewall, rubber grommet stuffed round the gap, sloppy weld bead left unground. Nothing else in the reel is a pedal. It collides with nothing (S0 owns the roller shutter and the dragster, S1 the sticker gun and the burning forecourt, S3 the rule gate, S4 the tarp, S5 the pod, S6 the press, S7 the punch rail and the keys, S8 the turntable). **Forward reference (corrected):** the pedal is the prop the LICENSING AUTHORITY takes off you. S3 sets that in motion the instant the rule plate lands, because S3's whole design is that no instructor appears in it, the institution has replaced him. That is the payoff the board can actually pay, and S7's own binding critic note confirms it in writing: the pedal punt is CUT from S7 because "the pedal is already dead as a threat by S3."

- **Story / metaphor:** THE BACK-SEAT BOSS. The bible has him untouchable in S2, so this is his high-water mark: the only scene where he takes something in every single beat and gives nothing back. He is not fighting you. He is being chauffeured by the rookie he is grading, feet crossed on the headrest, shades on, stamping his own work DONE on a metronome you cannot hear. What he GAINS, one surface at a time, on a clock that never flinches: his own ticket supply, your rear window, your headrest, your grab handle, your mirror, your clipboard, and at f83 your brake pedal itself, stamped DONE while it is still red-hot. The cruelty is jurisdictional. Your boot has authority over the axle and zero authority over the certificate. And here is the part that makes the VO literal rather than decorative: **his stamps change nothing in the world, and your boot changes everything for the worse.** He certifies into a vacuum. You brake, and another work-ticket slams into the back of the line. Six stomps, six more cars. You are the only guard in the building, you are guarding the wrong object, and the guarding is what is causing the pile. That is exactly the ache S3's institution is built to fix: not more of your foot, a rule with teeth.

- **Set detail (Alex's #1 note):** Panel 1012x792. Car in profile, nose panel-left, rear panel-right, road line y≈668. Car body spans x≈120 to x≈775. **The cutaway aperture is the CABIN ONLY, x≈150 to 680**; the rear quarter panel aft of it (x≈690 to 775) is intact near-side body skin, sharp, facing camera. Front row staged as a technical exploded cutaway per the row-read lock below.
  1. **The instructor brake**, x≈345 y≈608. Chrome, oversized, one hard rim glint. Dull at f0, glowing f24, orange f50, red-hot with a rising smoke wisp f78, still climbing at f88. It kills the car every cycle and it never gets to cool. No paint blister (cut: unperceivable).
  2. **The accelerator**, x≈235 y≈614. Small, worn rubber, pad rubbed clean through to bare metal in a hero-foot-shaped oval. Thousands of presses, zero metres. The hero's only input.
  3. **Two black scorch arcs in the carpet**, x≈330 to 380, y≈640, fanned under the brake. Pre-existing burn texture, static. They prove this has been happening long before frame 0. No third arc burns in live (cut per the beat cull).
  4. **THE ROW-1 LOCK, part A: one unbroken front bench seat-back**, a single silhouette running x≈270 to 500 behind BOTH front heads. This is a load-bearing object, not dressing: row 1 is one object with two heads on it.
  5. **THE ROW-1 LOCK, part B: one dash slab**, x≈150 to 500, both front footwells hanging off it, both sprites facing the same windscreen at panel-left.
  6. **The B-pillar belt anchor**, slicing x≈520, separating row 1 from row 2. Row 2 is a separate bench past it. The line reads 1-1-2, never 1-2-3.
  7. **The rear-view mirror**, mount x≈420 y≈268, on a visible ball joint. Starts tilted flat to the headliner: dead glass, no reflection, no glyph, no explanation. It is a mirror showing ceiling, which needs no reading. Its 8-frame event is speced in the ACTION block and it is the best thing in the scene.
  8. **The cardboard pine-tree air freshener**, hanging x≈425 y≈300. No text, just the shape. Swings on every stomp. No wind-up-around-its-own-string arc (cut: 89 frames cannot hold it).
  9. **The villain's crossed boots on YOUR headrest**, soles x≈560 y≈415. Black, flat geometry, spotless, because he has never done any work. **They never come down, in any frame.** They bounce and re-cross on the lurches and that is all they ever do.
  10. **The counterfeit DONE stamp on a LAZY-TONGS ARM.** Fat crooked stamp body, ink sickly yellow-green `#A8B84A`, smeared, mounted on a flat-geometry scissor-lattice extension bolted to the body, origin x≈690. **His body, shades and boots do not move for all 89 frames. Only the tongs travel.** The lattice racks out exactly one segment per hit, 1 through 8, strictly monotonic, and that segment count is the scene's visible power meter. Where the extension is longer than the reach (f72), the lattice visibly BOWS in a slack arc, which reads as more arm than he needs. His reach grows every beat while your reach stays welded to one pedal. That IS the takeaway, rendered as a machine.
  11. **The ink-pad tin**, armrest x≈660 y≈500. Lid off, ink slopped over the rim and soaking a spreading stain into the upholstery. Grime with a purpose: his approval is literally leaking into the car and nowhere else.
  12. **The steel receipt spindle of work-tickets**, parcel shelf x≈730 y≈430. Fat, static, never shorter, never taller. **No headliner slot, no ticket drop** (cut: third competing ticket mechanic).
  13. **ONE readable hero ticket** on the spindle, mono, sharp midground: `ship the migration`. The rest of the spindle is mono squiggle texture, no words. Four labels killed.
  14. **The blank clipboard**, tucked under YOU's arm x≈470 y≈450. **A BLANK WHITE SLAB.** No grid, no rules, no checkboxes, no ruled lines. One clean bone-white rectangle, deliberately the single emptiest object in a frame packed with stuff, so at any size and any speed it reads as nothing has been written here. His crooked yellow-green DONE at f72 is the ONLY mark that ever touches it.
  15. **The grab handle**, x≈500 y≈330. Your other nub white-knuckled on it. You are bracing, not assessing. Promoted to a live prop at f50 when the tongs stamp DONE around your knuckles.
  16. **The amber PAUSE-LAMP**, dash binnacle x≈185 y≈445. A physical instrument, not a string: a rounded-rect amber dash idiot-light with the universal pause glyph moulded into the lens, pulsing slow for the entire 89 frames, never going green, never clearing. The human gate that never opens. Survives at thumbnail size, reads at zero attention cost, no reading required. **The dash terminal prompt is killed as text.**
  17. **The knockoff NOS button**, x≈235 y≈515. Big red arcade button, unbranded, shift-light ring. Deliberately 75px and a shape-class away from the pause-lamp so the two never render as one blob. Claude reaches for it at f47 and gets stomped before contact.
  18. **The star-cracked windscreen corner**, x≈150 y≈330, crooked yellow-green DONE sticker slapped over the crack. Direct S1 artifact: he was working before we arrived. **On the stomp clock:** the crack ticks one more leg outward on every stomp with a dry audible tick under the horn wall, and by f84 its legs have crept out past all four edges of the sticker. His certificate is visibly failing to hold the glass together in the same frame where his stamp certifies your pedal. No prop in this card is decoration.
  19. **Two code-rain columns**, x≈600 to 660, tight around the villain, y≈240 to 600. They light HIM. They do not own the back third and they do not curtain the queue.
  20. **THE QUEUE**, x≈760 to 1012, **0.75px blur**, the panel's entire right quarter. Bumper-to-bumper ticket-cars in straight perspective recession. **No neck, no narrowing here.** Every queue car carries the TICKET SILHOUETTE: rectangular card, punched spindle hole, torn top edge, same rounded card corners as the spindle stock. The jam reads as YOUR backlog by SHAPE, at any blur, with zero legible words. **Cameo A:** a queue-Claude leaning out of the third car, x≈900 y≈545, both nubs cupped round his mouth, static angry lean, held. (Newspaper cameo cut.)
  21. **The SAFEDRIVR decal**, rear quarter panel x≈690 to 775, sharp midground, facing camera: "SAFEDRIVR ACADEMY · PLEASE BE PATIENT", peeling at one corner over a ghosted older decal. **The scene's ONE header, and the only text on intact bodywork.** The first queue car's nose sits 20px off it at x≈780, so the decal and the angriest horn arc share one pocket of frame: the honking jam is staring straight at PLEASE BE PATIENT.
  22. **The painted lane**, running the full panel width. At the panel-LEFT edge it funnels down toward something we cannot see and is swallowed by the cold slice. One choke, off-panel, upstream. Open loop for 89 frames.
  23. **The frayed seatbelt**, diagonal across Claude, webbing worn white at the shoulder from a thousand snaps.
  24. **Dust in the key light**, a volumetric shaft down through the cutaway roof aperture x≈300 to 500, wobbling with every suspension bounce. This shaft IS the front row's key source.

- **CONTINUOUS ACTION (fills the whole line):** Seven layers, each on its own clock, all still running at f88. **Dense frame, FIVE eye-beats.** 89 frames at 30fps (confirmed, `video/src/OsRoot.tsx:12`) is 2.97 seconds, which buys about five events. Everything else here is BED: static crafted stuff and running texture, which is free, versus beats, which are not.
  **L1 GO/STOMP** accelerating: cycles f0-22, f22-40, f40-52, f52-66, f66-71, f71-84, f84-88. Six stomps: **f22 / f40 / f52 / f66 / f71 / f84.** This is the scene's engine, not six separate meanings.
  **L2 THE STAMP METRONOME**, dead even every 11 frames, **f6 / f17 / f28 / f39 / f50 / f61 / f72 / f83**, deliberately off-beat against L1 so the two rhythms grind. **No event in this scene may sit off the 8-hit clock. No exceptions, no easing, no drift.** It is texture, not a beat: the eye does not have to catch any single hit for it to work, the ear does. **All eight targets are named**, each racking the tongs one segment further: f6 a fresh ticket on the spindle (x≈730, his own supply, unnoticed, 1 segment) · f17 the next ticket (x≈730, tongs dead level while the whole cabin bounces, 2) · f28 the rear window glass (x≈755, 3) · f39 the back of YOUR headrest (x≈560, 4) · f50 YOUR GRAB HANDLE (x≈500, DONE stamped around your white-knuckled nub, 5) · f61 the rear-view mirror (x≈420 y≈268, the tongs clip it on their way through, knock it back up to the headliner and leave a crooked DONE across the glass, 6) · f72 your clipboard (x≈470, lattice bowing slack because it is now longer than it needs to be, 7) · f83 the pedal (x≈345 y≈608, full extension down into the instructor's footwell, 8). **His stamps have zero consequence in the world. Nothing he certifies changes, moves, or arrives.** That is what makes him untouchable and what makes S3 necessary.
  **L3 QUEUE, bound to the STOMP, never to the stamp.** On every one of the six brake stomps, **ONE more ticket-car slams into the back of the line with a horn, in the same frame as the boot.** Six stomps, six new cars, one causality, zero reading: your foot goes down, the line gets longer. Underneath, the whole line slides in from panel-right and compresses continuously, still sliding at f88.
  **L4 HEAT**, per item 1, still climbing at f88.
  **L5 CODE-RAIN**, unbroken f0 to f88, two columns, tight on him.
  **L6 ENVIRONMENT**: lane line twitches two dashes forward on every GO and snaps back on every stomp (net progress across the whole scene: **zero metres**); roadside cone, bollard and fence post jerk past in the near plane and snap back with it; the star-crack ticks a leg on every stomp; the freshener swings; the pause-lamp pulses; dust drifts.
  **L7 CAMERA**, a continuous **15%** dolly-out that never holds.
  **THE FIVE BEATS.**
  **(1) f0, COLD, MID-CYCLE, no establish:** CLAUDE's foot is already flooring it, cabin already pitched back, road already streaking, and the VILLAIN's stamp is already 60% through its first downswing on a 1-segment arm.
  **(2) f22, THE FIRST BOOT SLAM:** YOUR boot lands, dead stop, seatbelt cracks taut, lane line snaps back, and **the first ticket-car slams into the back of the line with a horn, in the same frame.** The causal pair is established once, in one frame, and then repeats five more times as rhythm.
  **(3) f52, THE MILLIMETRE + THE MIRROR:** CLAUDE tests one millimetre of gas at f47, reaching for the NOS button. YOU stomp on the millimetre at f52; pedal goes orange; third ticket-car slams in. **And YOUR OWN STOMP rocks the mirror down on its ball joint.** For 8 frames, **f54 to f61**, the mirror FILLS: the VILLAIN's shades, the white glint bar, code-rain pouring down, dead centre in the glass, aimed straight at the lens. **YOU never looks up.** The audience catches him. The car does not. Your own brake is the thing that briefly shows you what you are failing to watch, and you miss it. At **f61** the tongs clip the mirror on their way to their scheduled hit, knock it back flat to the headliner and leave a crooked DONE across the glass. Dead to f88. He kills the sightline ON the clock, indifferent, not reacting.
  **(4) f72, THE CLIPBOARD:** the tongs reach over YOUR shoulder and stamp DONE on the blank white slab. **It is the only mark on it.** The only assessment in this car is his. YOU do not turn, because your eyes are on the road and your boot is on the brake.
  **(5) f83-84, THE PEAK:** at **f83** the eighth stamp lands on the CHROME BRAKE PEDAL ITSELF, tongs at full extension into your footwell, crooked yellow-green DONE on your only control, hissing and smoking through the sticker. At **f84** the hardest stomp of the scene answers it, sixth ticket-car, hardest horn. Two hits inside four frames.
  **AT f88:** boot mid-slam and un-landed, cabin mid-lurch, smoke mid-rise, code-rain still falling, freshener mid-swing, lane line mid-snap-back, star-crack legs mid-tick, pause-lamp mid-pulse, and **the villain's stamp is at the TOP of its arc on a 9-segment arm, raised for a ninth hit that never lands in this scene.** The **final 10 frames give the frame's largest single motion to the LINE GETTING LONGER**, the sixth car slamming home and the whole jam rippling and compressing, so the last thing the viewer carries into the cut is the queue, not the stamp. Nothing in the frame is parked.

- **Escalation:** hopeful, then annoyed, then the 8-frame mirror the audience catches and the car does not, then defeated, then the brake itself certified. The stomp tempo roughly triples across 89 frames while the stamp tempo never changes by a single frame, so the energy curve is one system accelerating into panic beside another system that is completely serene. Two meters run against each other in plain sight: **his tongs rack out one segment per hit, 1 to 8, and your reach stays welded to one pedal at 0.** The dolly-out is its own escalation and now actually earns its billing at 15%: the longer you look, the more queue there is, and the queue is the panel's whole right quarter at near-sharp focus, not a blurred garnish behind him. Peak is f83 to f84, the stamp on the hot pedal answered by the hardest stomp of the scene, both landing inside four frames, with the sixth ticket-car arriving on the same boot.

- **Gag / pop-culture:** All geometric, all knockoff. (1) The universal driving-school car: chevrons and the rear-quarter decal "SAFEDRIVR ACADEMY · PLEASE BE PATIENT" peeling over a ghosted older decal, twenty pixels from the angriest horn in the jam. The sign begs patience while an impostor certifies everything and your boot builds the line. **No L-plate on the car.** (2) THE BACK-SEAT EXECUTIVE: code-rain, shades, feet up, being driven by the guy he is grading, body never moving once. (3) THE RUBBER-STAMP BUREAUCRAT, escalated into a machine: ink pad, spindle, and a LAZY-TONGS scissor arm, the iconic geometry of a clerk who approves without reading, now with a reach that grows while he does not. (4) REVERSE FAST-AND-FURIOUS: Claude reaches for the arcade NOS button and gets brake-stomped before contact. (5) The jam staged as arcade traffic: squashed impatient ticket-shaped rectangles and angry horn arcs, no organic vehicles, no human drivers, every honker a Claude sprite. (6) Living world: the star-crack creeping out from under its own DONE sticker, one leg per stomp.

- **Sprite costume:** **Every beat names its sprite. No bare pronouns anywhere in this card.**
  **HERO (CLAUDE)**, driver's seat x≈300: canonical warm clay `#D97757` mascot, **eyes always visible**, **a red-on-white L-PLATE worn square on the chest like a marathon bib** (matching S0's chest badge, S1's velcroed plate and S3's bib; it rattles on every stomp and is torn off exactly ONCE, at S7's 20th punch), diagonal frayed seatbelt, small wheel in both nubs. Face arc: eager wide eyes f0, flat ears and side-eye f40, crossed eyes and a resigned flat mouth f78.
  **YOU**, instructor seat x≈445, **pushed 25px DOWN and rendered 12% LARGER than the hero**, so the offset reads as nearer the camera in the same row, never as further back: same mascot silhouette tinted cool cream-grey, orange hi-vis instructor cap, hi-vis chevron vest with a small gold **"YOU"** badge, blank white slab under one arm, other nub on the grab handle, one comically oversized boot permanently poised over the chrome.
  **VILLAIN**, back bench x≈640, exactly per the bible, via `<Villain/>`, never hand-rolled: canonical Claude silhouette, slate-grey `#5A5F6B` clay, opaque black wraparound shades with one hard white glint bar, black jacket with sharp shoulder blocks, skinny black tie over a white collar, coiled earpiece wire, fat counterfeit stamp on the lazy-tongs arm with sickly yellow-green `#A8B84A` ink, two code-rain columns always on. He never speaks. He does not multiply here; that is S5's one use.
  **⛔ VILLAIN GUARD, absolute:** **the villain has NO EYES in any frame of this scene.** His shades never lift, never reflect, never crack, never fog, never catch a pupil. **His only face event in all 89 frames is the flat mouth at f83.** Nothing in this card asks for a villain eye, a villain pupil, or a villain gaze, and no beat may be built that implies one.

- **Camera:** **Lens:** long, roughly 85mm-equivalent, flattening the cutaway into a near-orthographic side elevation so it reads as a technical diagram of one failing system rather than a perspective sketch. **Staging cheat, speced not wished:** a technical EXPLODED cutaway. The near-side door skin and the near-side seat are REMOVED (the torn steel lip is already the foreground occluder, so the removal is motivated in-frame), the cabin flank is open x≈150 to 680, and the two front sprites are staggered on a shallow diagonal per the Sprite block. The row read is locked by two continuous physical objects row 2 never touches: **one unbroken bench seat-back x≈270 to 500 behind both front heads, and one dash slab x≈150 to 500 both front footwells hang off.** Row 1 is one object with two heads on it. Row 2 is a separate bench past the B-pillar at x≈520. **Height:** seat height, y≈470 panel-local, dead level with the villain's shades. We are in the row, not above it. **Move:** a continuous **15%** dolly-out across all 89 frames that never holds, plus a 2-frame shake on every stomp.
  **⛔ BUILD CONSTRAINT, the scene's best idea dies silently without it:** **the stomp shake is applied PER-LAYER, never on the panel root.** The Villain group AND his two code-rain columns are excluded from the shake and counter-offset by the inverse of the shake transform, so they hold dead still through every one of the six stomps. He scales with the dolly, he never jitters with it. Your system convulses, his does not. A root-level shake makes this vanish with no error and no visual tell.
  **Depth stack. FOREGROUND OCCLUDER, 2px blur:** the torn steel lip of the cutaway flank with door-skin layers and foam exposed (pure structure, **no text on it**), the B-pillar belt anchor slicing x≈520, and roadside cone, bollard and fence post flicking past in the near plane and snapping back. **MIDGROUND, sharp:** the cabin, the three sprites, the spindle, and the intact rear quarter panel with the header decal. **THREE LIGHT SOURCES, one per department:** (1) **WARM CLAY KEY** down through the roof aperture, item 24's volumetric dust shaft at x≈300 to 500, lighting the front row (**not** from the windscreen); (2) **SICK GREEN** from the two code-rain columns at the rear, lighting HIM only; (3) a narrow **COLD SLICE** through the windscreen at panel-left, the only cool light in frame and the one place this car cannot get to. **The cold slice stays an ANONYMOUS GLOW: no building, no signage, no seal, no readable architecture, no barrier.** Nothing names the authority until S3 assembles it, and nothing visible ahead of the car may compete with your boot as the cause of the stop. **BACKGROUND, panel-LEFT, 1.5px blur:** the cold glow only, above the lane funnelling off-panel. **BACKGROUND, panel-RIGHT, 0.75px blur:** the queue, the horn arcs, straight perspective recession, no neck. The two do not share an edge and do not fight over the cause of the jam.

- **Transition + SFX:** **IN:** hard cut on CLAUDE's gas-chirp, mid-cycle, no ramp. **Beat list:** f0 engine rev + tyre chirp · **f6/17/28/39/50/61/72/83** a dry wooden STAMP-THUNK on a perfectly unchanging 11-frame clock, the spine of the mix, with a small lattice ratchet-click racking under each one so the arm's growth is audible · **f22/40/52/66/71/84** fat rubber SQUEAK-THUNK + seatbelt ratchet + suspension boing, closing up, **each one landing simultaneously with a fresh ticket-car CRUNCH and a new horn**, so the ear learns your-boot-equals-more-line before the eye does · a dry crack-tick on the windscreen star under each stomp · horns layering to a jammed chorus by f66 · from f50 a low heat sizzle on the pedal · **f83 the one time the two layers touch**, stamp on hot chrome, a wet SSSS and a curl of smoke · f84 hardest stomp, brake squeal plus horn wall plus the sixth crunch · bed: pause-lamp relay tick, freshener creak, code-rain as a soft granular hiss so silence never opens. **OUT:** whip-pan **LEFT** off the nose, riding the tyre squeal, with the pedal's smoke wisp as the wipe element, **travelling down the EMPTY lane** to the licensing authority's gates in the deep distance. The car was never nosed against anything. The gates were reachable the whole time and 89 frames bought zero metres, which sharpens the jurisdictional question instead of answering it early. The stamp metronome CONTINUES across the cut and its ninth hit lands in S3 on the steel rule plate as a CRACK. The clock crossing the cut is the reel's best audio bridge.

- **Mute check:** "One guy is slamming the brake to stop the car, and every time he does the line behind him gets longer, while the guy in the back seat approves everything anyway." Under two seconds, no reading. Boot plus dead-stopped learner plus a growing jam of ticket-shaped cars plus a back-seat man stamping. **Honest text inventory, five sources, no more:** (1) the rear-quarter decal "SAFEDRIVR ACADEMY · PLEASE BE PATIENT", the scene's ONE header; (2) the letter **L** on the hero's chest plate; (3) the **YOU** badge; (4) the word **DONE** on the stamps; (5) one mono ticket label, `ship the migration`, on the sharp spindle only. That is the whole list and the card specifies nothing beyond it. Killed since v2: four ticket labels, the dash prompt string, the car's L-plate square, the QUEUE counter (stays killed per the board's binding S2 note; the physical length of the line does the job harder than a digit). **No text in this scene is load-bearing.** **The single load-bearing PROP is the amber pause-lamp**, which carries "a human gate that never clears" with zero words, survives at thumbnail size, and is the mechanism the entire VO line rests on. **No number in this scene**, so the locked spine (one week in one night / ONE rule / 20 passes / 95% / below 90) stays untouched.

- **Hook into next:** The stamp, frozen at the top of its arc at f88 on a nine-segment arm, raised for a ninth DONE that this scene refuses to give it, over a line of work that got longer six times because of your foot. The whip-pan runs LEFT off the nose and travels down the open, empty lane to the authority's gates, far ahead, cold, untouched, and never once approached. The open question is jurisdictional, not emotional: if your foot can only reach the pedal and never the stamp, who has authority over the stamp? S3 answers by bolting a steel rule across the arch, and that raised ninth stamp comes down on it and shatters. First loss, set up in this frame. **The escalation ladder is left with two landed rungs and one loaded:** f72 your job, f83 your power, f88 the unfired ninth. Two landed and one raised is a stronger ladder in three seconds than three landed rungs nobody separates.
