Fix 21's actual grievance, the mute check lying about its own inventory, is honored in full: the inventory is now declared honestly and the load-bearing prop is named (the pause-lamp). (c) **Fix 1 vs fixes 12/19 on f56.** Fix 1 cuts the beat; 12/19 patch it. **Resolved for fix 1** (fix 3's cull removes it independently); 12/19's generalizable demand, explicit sprite naming in every beat plus the no-eyes guard, is applied across the whole card. (d) **Fix 4 vs fix 11 on x-coords.** Fix 4 gives the queue x≈760-1012; fix 11 names the spindle at x≈820 and the rear window at x≈880, which sit inside it. **Resolved for fix 4** (the thesis object's real estate); fix 11's substance is the target SEQUENCE, not the pixels, so the car's rear geometry is restaged to x≈730 (spindle) and x≈755 (rear window) with the sequence intact.

---

### S3 THE ONE RULE

- **Takeaway:** Nobody fixed Claude. A night crew bolted a gate across the forecourt, and the gate has exactly one rule on it. It does not punish the cheater and it does not reward the rookie. It just refuses to open for anybody who has not proven it, and the first thing it proves is that the cheater's stamp is ceramic.

- **Base object:** **THE ONE-RULE GATE.** A ground-anchored steel intake gate, weigh-station scale, driven into the tarmac across the whole forecourt: two heavy uprights, a chain-link skirt you can see the lot through, and a cross-rail slot rack above it built to hold FIVE stencilled plates, with exactly ONE plate in it. It is ARCHITECTURE, not a machine. It has no head, no ink, no verdict. It never strikes anything and it never issues anything. Things strike IT. It has one moving part, a shaft along the cross-rail carrying two lift arms, one per passage, and the shaft has exactly two answers: up, or down. **S3 owns three verbs nothing else in the reel owns: BOLT DOWN, BLOCK, HERD.** It does not own STAMP, GRADE, ISSUE, INSPECT or PRESS.
  **It is a DOOR, per the board's Collision-1 ruling (S3 = a door, S6 = a machine, S7 = a counter).** No overhead portal, no crossbeam over the subject's head, no head descending from top of frame onto a rookie looking up. That silhouette and that move are S6's now and this card does not touch either. The word for S6's object does not appear anywhere on this card. S3's plate arrives HORIZONTALLY, swung in from off-frame left on a crane jib at rail height and planted DOWN into sockets in the ground, driven from below, standing up rather than hanging over.
  Clean of S0's roller shutter and christmas tree, S1's sticker gun, S2's brake pedal, S4's inspection ramp, S5's order-rail conveyor, S6's overhead verdict machine, S7's counter, laminator, punch rail and gauge, S8's turntable, S9's exploded manual. S1's two-post lift appears as SET DRESSING only (empty, arms down, dim, deep), never as a hero object. The keys are not in this scene at all, so S7 still has everything to give away. No 0/20 rail seed, no digit anywhere except the one cast into the plate.

- **Story / metaphor:** Still S1's night strip-mall forecourt, sodium-orange, oil-stained, S1's burnt car still slumped where he stickered it. The hero is on his knees on the apron, **pleading straight down the lens.** Both nubs up, blank unlaminated provisional card held out to YOU, empty photo box, blank record strip, begging you to believe him. S1 already established direct address with the key toss, so the audience knows this address. He is not asking the villain for anything. He is asking you. And the villain, up on the burnt car's roof with one foot on the light bar, bored, answers for him: he licks the die, leans in over the hero's shoulder, and slaps a crooked sickly yellow-green DONE straight onto his forehead, then pats the cap. That is the VO's first clause as a physical event. You hope Claude is trustworthy. Claude's own grey copy answers the question for him, and the answer is a forgery on the hero's face.
  Then the lot dies. The two near sodium masts blow out on their filaments and for three frames the only lit thing on the forecourt is the villain's own yellow-green code-rain, in the air and in the puddle. Under it a sub-bass rumble comes up. It never leaves.
  Then **people build a system.** Not magic, not tarmac giving birth to itself: a night crew. A sparky throws breakers on a portable spider box and the lot's six dead flood masts strike on one at a time, marching away into the depth of the lot. A boom lift reverses four bolts out of the old JIFFY LOOB sign and it drops face-down on the concrete, and the frame's word count falls from a shopfront to one stencil. A crane jib swings a whole prefabricated gate section in from off-frame left at rail height, and the attendant, whose squeegee got swapped for a lit marshalling wand mid-swipe, walks it down like a guy landing a load at 3am. It drops into its sockets with a boom that shakes grit off the concrete, two hi-vis Claudes in ear defenders torque the foot plates, and four rivets fire ascending in pitch. The rack could hold five rules. It holds one: **NO SOLO WORK UNTIL PROVEN**, with a huge 1 cast into the same steel at the plate's left end and four empty slots beside it, bolt holes bare, nothing in them. Nobody argues with the villain. Nobody points at him. A crew simply welds a building around where he is standing, and nobody looks up at him once.
  Then he does the only thing in his character. He does not read it. He is standing above it on his own wreck, so he treats it like every other object in his life: a form to be stamped. He crouches, springs, and brings the die DOWN onto the plate's face. And the die-face **SHATTERS**. Yellow-green shards and a burst of his own counterfeit ink blow back across his white collar, and the recoil throws him. The plate does not move. He does. His own podium spits him out down its flank, he knocks the cracked lens off his own light bar on the way, and he lands flat on the apron at f200, and he is never higher than the hero again.
  **What the system takes off him, four things, his first loss in the reel:** (1) his die-face cracks open and proves it was ceramic, not brass; (2) his own ink marks HIM, the counterfeiter gets counterfeited, and it will not dab out; (3) his HIGH GROUND, which he does not step off, he is thrown off it, and by f206 the armco has come up through the wreck's downstage flank so the sill and the wing he climbed at f0 are inside a galvanized wall and the only face of the car left to him is a sheer burnt haunch, and he glances at the roof once at f204 and does not try; (4) his FREEDOM OF THE LOT, a latch-kicker walks a barrier up behind him segment by segment until the only direction left is depth. He is not destroyed. He calmly re-seats a spare die off the bandolier, because he learns nothing. He just no longer gets to choose where he stands.
  **And then the rule gets ENACTED, on camera, on the honest one.** A third Claude walks up to the left passage with no L-plate on his chest. The arm LIFTS. He walks under it and away into the dark, on his own, without a glance. Our hero steps up right behind him, L-plate square on his chest, and the arm **SLAMS DOWN** in front of his face. Same gate, same second, opposite answer, decided by one glyph on the chest. He backs against the post, slides a nub up the top of his own head, and looks: his nub is sitting a clear gap under the red band. Nobody asked him to. Nobody is watching except one rookie with his own plate on upside down. The system does not beat the villain with an argument and it does not console the hero with one either. It beats them both with a floor plan.

- **Set detail (Alex's #1 note):**
  **MASTER GEOMETRY, restated once, everything below hangs off it.** Panel-local **1012x792**. Camera at **KERB LEVEL**, roughly a standing Claude's knee, looking slightly up. **GROUND LINE / horizon: y=640.** Visible apron floor: **y=640 to 792**. **VANISHING POINT: (972, 640)**, on the ground line, right side. Every ground-plane line in this card converges on it. The gate's foot line is at **y=700**: everything downstage of it (y=700 to 792) is the old forecourt, everything upstage (y=700 up to the horizon at 640) is the deep lot, and the gate's two passages are the only ways through. Because a kerb-level camera compresses the whole floor into 152px, **depth is carried by explicit SCALE stamps per element, never by a y-band, and the depth stack below is keyed by named element, not by a y range.** 100% scale = a standing full-size Claude at 320px.
  1. **THE ONE-RULE GATE, the base object.** Two heavy uprights, LEFT x=8 to 58 and RIGHT x=954 to 1004, foot plates driven into the tarmac at y=700, tops at y=380. Between them a **chain-link skirt, y=590 to 700**, galvanized mesh, so the deep lot, the chute, the paint buggy and the bay glow all read THROUGH it, softly. Above the skirt a **cross-rail slot rack, y=400 to 590**, top chord y=400 to 415, bottom chord y=575 to 590, thin steel webs between bays. Rusted, six visible weld seams, a drip-run of orange rust off the bottom chord. It arrives as one prefabricated section and never moves again once planted.
  2. **THE PLATE.** Slot 4 only, **x=601 to 773, y=400 to 590**. The scene's ONE header, sandblasted and stencilled, not typed, in three stacked lines: NO SOLO WORK UNTIL PROVEN. Four rivet bosses at the rail joints. Its bottom lip sits at y=590, at the eye line of a body standing at the gate's depth, so it BLOCKS the road rather than hanging over it. It does the whole VO for free.
  3. **THE CAST 1.** x=610 to 668, y=412 to 578. A huge numeral cast into the plate's left end **as part of the same steel**, raised, chipped enamel in the counters, one rust weep down its stem. It is not a sign, not a marker and not a prop. It is the plate. The scene's only number.
  4. **THE FOUR EMPTY SLOTS.** Slot 1 x=64 to 236, slot 2 x=243 to 415, slot 3 x=422 to 594, slot 5 x=780 to 952, all y=400 to 590. Bare steel frames, bolt holes drilled and empty, a faint ghost of paint where plates used to sit in slot 2. A rack built for a rulebook with exactly one plate in it. That is "one rule" with no reading, no second digit, and it is why the frame stays see-through.
  5. **THE LEFT PEDESTRIAN PASSAGE.** A gap in the skirt, **x=150 to 260**. Its outboard edge is **THE HEIGHT POST**, x=250 to 272, y=440 to 700, with **THE RED PROVEN BAND at y=478**, one saturated red enamel band, unnumbered notches above and below it, 162px clear of the ground line so it can never be mistaken for a horizon. Scuffed, chipped, pre-owned. **THE PEDESTRIAN ARM** parks on the band at y=478, red and white candy stripe, counterweight can on the short arm, rubber up-stop and down-stop. Its rest height is exactly the hero's cap brim. The fairground bar rebuilt as a law.
  6. **THE RIGHT CHUTE PASSAGE.** A gap in the skirt where the chute crosses the gate's foot line, **x=758 to 900**. **THE CHUTE ARM** on the same shaft, post at (905, 700), pivot at (905, 645), arm 150px, scaled to that depth. It starts down at f236 and is still descending at f254. It never lands on screen. The scene ends on a door closing, not closed.
  7. **THE SHAFT.** One steel shaft runs the length of the cross-rail and drives BOTH arms. One law, two arms, two answers. Visible couplings at x=260 and x=758, and the shaft rotates a few degrees on every lift and every slam, so you can see that the same mechanism made both decisions.
  8. **THE WRECK, S1's car, matched to S1's shipped signature.** Body x=520 to 770, slumped on its rims, base y=700 to 748, nose down-left. **Both front wheels gone.** One door lying flat on the concrete beside it at x=490 to 560, y=735 to 760. **Scabbed with roughly nine overlapping sickly yellow-green DONE stickers** (match S1's final count), one curling off the windscreen, one stuck over the tape holding nothing on. Cowl still leaking a lazy smoke column. Roof plane **y=520**, roofline x=570 to 720. It is the villain's PODIUM at f0, his WALL at f200, and his EVIDENCE at f228. The same object indicts him three times.
  9. **THE LIGHT BAR.** A bent knockoff roof light bar across **x=600 to 690, y=506 to 520**, one lens cracked and hanging by its wire. The villain's raised foot rests on it at f0, so the prop earns its place. It rattles visibly on each of the four rivet hits, swings loose after the fourth, and the cracked lens is knocked clean off and smashes when he tumbles past it at f196.
  10. **THE GREEN TARP.** Bunched against the wreck's rear haunch at **(740, 706)**, inside the chute, on his side of the armco, one corner already lifting in the draught from f0 so your eye keeps catching it. At f228 he yanks it over the wreck as the barrier walks him past. This is the exact tarp S4's tin bot pulls off. Chekhov, loaded on screen, and nothing steel stands between him and it.
  11. **THE EMPTY TWO-POST LIFT, S1's.** x=690 to 830, base y=668 to 676, top y=520, deep, dim, arms down, empty, one arm pad missing. The wreck has been rolled off it onto the apron. It is upstage of the armco, so the old regime's machine ends the scene abandoned on the wrong side of a fence. With the JIFFY LOOB sign and the wreck it gives the same-place recognition three anchors instead of leaning on the sodium colour alone.
  12. **THE PUDDLE.** 180x38, centred **(330, 725)**, x=240 to 420, pooled at the low spot beside the drain grate. The best surface in the scene and the reason the left third is not a void. f0 to f60 it mirrors sodium orange and the near mast, rippling under the attendant's squeegee. f68 to f75 it goes dead black except one travelling band of the villain's yellow-green code-rain, which is the thing that carries the near-black. f88 to f140 it takes each flood shaft as that bank strikes, so the palette pivot lands on the floor as well as in the air, and it lands on the hero's reflection while his nubs are in it. **From f120 it carries the gate section's inverted reflection sliding in from the left, so the rule arrives in the puddle a beat before it arrives in the frame.** f170 the fourth rivet shatters the reflection into rings that have not settled at f254. The attendant never gets it dry.
  13. **THE APRON.** y=640 to 792: three oil stains in the shape of where cars used to sit; a **cast-iron drain grate at (440, 740)**; a loose **wheel-nut** rolling a slow arc from (560, 770) to (505, 790) across the whole scene because the impacts keep nudging it; grit that jumps a half-inch off the concrete on every rivet hit; and **THE SHARD**, one yellow-green die fragment kicked off at f188, spinning on the concrete at **(455, 748)** like a dropped coin, still spinning down at f254 at the grate's lip, never settling.
  14. **THE TWO NEAR SODIUM MASTS.** Mast L at x=205, base y=778, head y=185. Mast R at x=490, base y=758, head y=230, directly over the kneeling hero, so the old lawless orange is his key light for the whole plea. Buzzing amber, mast L already wearing a dead moth-shaped scorch on the diffuser. They blow f60 to f72 with filament pings and falling glass.
  15. **THE FOUR DEEP SODIUM MASTS.** x=560 / 700 / 810 / 885, bases y=690 / 672 / 658 / 650, heads y=470 / 512 / 540 / 556, scales 42% / 30% / 23% / 18%, receding to the back fence. They do NOT die with the near two: they blow in a wave at f92 / f112 / f132 / f150, so the palette pivot MARCHES AWAY from camera across the whole scene instead of completing at f72.
  16. **THE SIX FLOOD BANKS, the lot's dead hardware, energised not born.** Explicit scale and head per mast so the rank reads as depth and not as wallpaper:
      · Bank 1: x=110, head y=95, base y=790, **100%**, hard-edged, shaft blowing out the left of frame, flicker fills a third of the frame, THUNK is a slab.
      · Bank 2: x=340, head y=228, base y=742, **74%**.
      · Bank 3: x=545, head y=324, base y=706, **55%**.
      · Bank 4: x=730, head y=404, base y=682, **40%**.
      · Bank 5: x=870, head y=462, base y=664, **29%**.
      · Bank 6: x=975, head y=511, base y=650, **20%**, soft, sitting almost on the horizon, its strike a tiny distant blink and a tick.
      Heads and bases both converge on the vanishing point. Bank 6 is STILL striking on at f254 and its shaft has not reached the floor.
  17. **THE SPIDER BOX + THE SPARKY.** A portable distro board on a stand at (430, 762), fat rubber feeds snaking away across the apron and into the mast bases, one feed running right through the puddle's edge. **A crew Claude throws one breaker per bank, by hand, at f88 / f100 / f112 / f124 / f140 / f248.** Every light in this scene is caused by a Claude's nub on a lever. At f254 he is still leaning on the last one with bank 6 stuttering.
  18. **THE JIFFY LOOB SIGN.** x=620 to 880, y=250 to 345 on the neighbouring unit, reading JIFFY LOOB · CERTIFIED IN 5 MINUTES OR IT'S FREE, **identical spelling to S1**, the committed running gag. The boom-lift Claude reverses four bolts out of it f95 to f126 and it drops face-down on the concrete at the facade's foot, x=620 to 880, y=612 to 642, with a flat aluminium bang, and it is STILL rocking on its corner at f254 with a slow decaying wobble. The old regime's promise physically comes down and the frame's word count drops to one header. The scene deletes its own text on camera.
  19. **THE FESTOON.** Sagging knockoff triangular bunting from the sign's left corner (620, 268) across the void to a bent pole at (120, 300), sagging to y=400 at mid-span, filling the upper-centre from frame 0. Flapping f0 to f126. **Dragged down slack when the sign drops at f126** so it hangs in a dead loop. The gate section swings in THROUGH it at f132 and it drapes over the top chord for the rest of the scene, twitching in the draught at f254.
  20. **THE SHUTTERED UNIT.** Facade x=180 to 560, base at the ground line y=640, rising to y=250. Wire-caged windows, a **dead tube sign strobing on a broken ballast for the entire scene**, a fascia letter band at y=270 to 330, a skip at (500, 618) with a burst bin bag beside it whose shreds flap continuously f0 to f254. Explicitly **no roller shutter**: S0 owns that verb.
  21. **THE CHAIN-LINK BACK FENCE.** Base on the ground line y=640, top y=558, spanning x=0 to 950, the lot's boundary. A cable-tied banner at x=300 to 420 rattling in the wind f0 to f254.
  22. **THE TYRE STACK.** x=45 to 135, base y=752, top y=640, four part-worn tyres. The **fourth rivet's shake at f170** topples the top one off and it rolls a slow arc for the rest of the scene.
  23. **THE ARMCO, in two runs, kicked up by hand.** Corrugated, galvanized, one segment already dented and repainted a slightly wrong grey so the system reads USED. **RUN A, f196 to f206**, six segments along the chute's downstage edge from **(770, 697) walking toward camera to (430, 792)**, so the clangs get closer to the mic as it seals. It comes up THROUGH the wreck's crumpled downstage sill and the fallen door and interleaves with them, which is what takes the roof off him: the step, the wing and the door frame he climbed at f0 are now inside a wall. **RUN B, f224 to f250**, from (770, 697) up-right through the chute passage to **(950, 652)**, segments shrinking, clangs receding. Each new segment arriving at his back is what moves him. **THE LATCH-KICKER**, a crew Claude with a bar, walks each run and kicks each latch. The fence does not decide to rise. A guy kicks it.
  24. **THE LANE PAINT + THE PAINT BUGGY.** Two hard white lines inside the chute, converging on the vanishing point: near line from **(500, 792) to (952, 650)**, far line from **(735, 792) to (960, 646)**. A crew Claude walks a paint buggy up the near line f180 to f254, throwing a fine mist into flood shaft 5, and at f254 the head is at (895, 664), still travelling, still 60px short of the point. At f236 he steps aside for the herded villain without looking at him and keeps painting.
  25. **THE CRANE JIB.** Off-frame left, only the jib head, the block and the chains ever enter, at rail height. It swings the whole prefabricated gate section in HORIZONTALLY at f120, tracking right at y=400 to 590, and sets it DOWN into pre-cut sockets at f150. It never enters from the top of frame and never descends onto anybody.
  26. **THE CHERRY-PICKER + THE BOLT-GUN CLAUDE.** Basket at x=430 to 520, rising f138 to f150 to basket floor y=560, ear defenders, coiled air line trailing down to a compressor puttering on the apron. He fires the four rivets from the basket, and at f210 he calmly reaches out and rivets a small bracket onto the plate's underside for no visible reason. Working machinery needs a working guy.
  27. **THE TEARDOWN BOOM LIFT.** x=640 to 760, basket head y=300. Unbolts the JIFFY LOOB sign f95 to f126, then **tracks right-to-left along the facade from f130 unbolting the shuttered unit's individual fascia letters, one dropping every 30 frames at f130 / f160 / f190 / f220 / f250**, basket still tracking left with one letter still in his nub at f254. The teardown does not finish.
  28. **THE NIGHT CREW.** **THE ATTENDANT** at (395, 748), grubby hi-vis tabard, bent-handle bucket, idly squeegeeing the puddle at f0; at f112 a passing crew Claude plucks the squeegee out of his nub mid-swipe and plants a lit marshalling wand in it without breaking stride; f120 to f150 that wand is the thing GUIDING the gate section down, rhythmic, professional, and he is genuinely good at it; from f152 he goes straight back to squeegeeing the air with the wand and is still squeegeeing at f254. He has been conscripted into the OS and has not noticed. **TWO HI-VIS CLAUDES** in ear defenders, at the foot plates, x=30 and x=976, impact wrenches, torquing at f150. **HI-VIS CLAUDE A** then pulls a scuffed, chipped, one-bolt-short **RED PROVEN BAND out of his tool bag at f202** and rivets it onto the height post: it has clearly been on other gates, the institution keeps a stock of them, and its missing-bolt corner swings loose and is still swinging at f254.
  29. **THE PLATELESS ROOKIE.** Enters frame-left on the apron at f196, base y=730, 80%, no L-plate, no cap, no lanyard, hands in nothing, not queueing, just leaving. Reaches the left passage at f204. The arm lifts. He walks under it f210 to f216 and away into the dark of the deep lot f216 to f240, alone, never once looking back. Still a shrinking silhouette at f254.
  30. **THE LEFT-OUT ROOKIE.** ONE rookie, base **(120, 736)**, 72%, dim, on the free apron behind the hero, watching the villain get walked past. **L-plate on upside down.** He quietly rotates it at f244 while nobody is looking, and at f248 he quietly copies the hero and slides his own nub up his own head. He is not in a line, he is not holding a ticket, and there is no rope: the queue is S7's and this scene does not touch it.
  31. **THE S4 BAY GLOW, a bound plant.** A small cold-lit square sitting ON the vanishing point at **(972, 625)**, exactly **40x30px**, with a knee-high wind-up silhouette walking a lap inside it. **Bound limits:** under 2% of frame, never keyed, never sharp, never larger than 40x30, no cut or push ever moves toward it, its lap never syncs to any villain beat, and it never shares a sharp focal plane with him. He walks toward it. He never shares a read with it. It is the machine that will catch him and it is already running.
  32. **THE DUST LADDER.** Continuous by handoff, never a blanket claim. f0 to f60 warm amber motes drifting in the TWO sodium cones. f60 to f72 the blowout scatters glass dust through the dying cones. f72 to f75 near-black, and the only lit particles in the frame are the ones drifting through the villain's yellow-green code-rain, in the air and repeated in the puddle. From f88, dust enters each flood shaft AS IT STRIKES, at f88 / f100 / f112 / f124 / f140, and never leaves it, so the volumetric load builds with the building. **Bank 6 carries no dust, because at f254 it has no shaft yet.** Grit blasts into a hard swirl on each rivet CHA-CHUNK so the light itself reacts to the machinery.
  33. **THE FOREGROUND OCCLUDER.** Bottom and left edge, y=740 to 792, heavily defocused: a cracked concrete wheel-stop and a sagging bollard chain, plus drifting dust and, from f188, yellow-green shards tumbling THROUGH the near field past the lens.

- **CONTINUOUS ACTION (fills the whole line):** Eleven named layers, each on its own clock, none waiting for another.
  **L1 HERO, f0 to f254, and he owns the last quarter:** f0 he is ALREADY ACTING, mid-plead, kneeling base (505, 706), head crown y=520, 84%, both nubs up, blank provisional card held out **to the lens**, cap askew, shoulders working as he begs YOU. f42 the stamp lands on his forehead and his head rocks back. f50 to f58 he turns back to the lens with the yellow-green DONE across his face and the card still up: the thesis frame of the scene. f58 to f70 he scrubs with a nub and only smears it wider, wipes it on his sleeve at f66 and makes it worse. f72 to f75 near-black, he freezes with his nub on his face. f78 he tucks the card back onto the lanyard where it swings for the rest of the scene. f78 to f96 he crawls left to the puddle. **f96 to f148 he is WORKING, not watching: he is on his nubs over the water trying to wash the lie off his own face.** It does not come off, it spreads, and now his nubs are yellow-green too. Each flood strike at f100 / f112 / f124 / f140 re-colours his own reflection under his nubs, so the palette pivot happens ON HIS FACE. **f120 the gate section's inverted reflection slides into the water in front of him and he sees the rule before the frame does.** f126 he stops. f150 to f168 he stands, slow, backs a step, chin climbing. f172 he reads it. f180 to f188 he flinches as the villain springs. f196 the shatter blows past him and he ducks. **f204 to f216 he walks left to the passage**, 125px in 12 frames, four brisk steps, and steps up right behind the plateless rookie at f216. **f218 the arm SLAMS down in front of his face and he stops dead an inch off it.** f222 to f240 he backs against the height post and slides a nub up the top of his own head. **f240 he LOOKS: his nub is sitting a clear gap under the red band.** He looks at his nub, then at the band, then straight down the chute at the villain being walked away. f244 to f254 he does not slump. He steps back onto the mark, sets his shoulders and holds the nub where it is. At f254 the nub is still under the band, the arm is still down across his face, and his lanyard card is still swinging.
  **L2 VILLAIN, f0 to f254, and he loses the frame at f224:** f0 on the burnt car's roof, foot anchor **(640, 520)**, other foot up on the light bar at (650, 508), 84%, head at y=250, a full body and a half above the kneeling hero, stamp resting on his shoulder, bored. f30 he licks the die. **f42 he leans in over the hero's shoulder and SLAPS the forehead**, shoulder dropping to (600, 455), reach 120px, then two condescending pats on the cap. f60 to f130 he does not react to the lights at all, he examines his die and blows lint off it, which is colder and funnier than panic. f150 the section's shadow sweeps over him and he finally looks down, glint bar flaring on the shades. f176 he crouches. f180 he SPRINGS. **f188 CONTACT: he brings the die DOWN onto the plate's face at (690, 460) like it is a form on a counter, because that is the only verb he has.** f188 to f194 the die-face shatters, a ring of yellow-green shards and an ink burst blows back across his collar, **and the recoil ragdolls him BACKWARDS off the roof. The plate does not move. He does.** f194 to f200 he tumbles down the wreck's flank, his own podium spitting him out, knocking the cracked lens off the light bar on the way. f200 he lands flat on the apron at (700, 726), inside the chute, and he is never higher than the hero again. f204 he glances once at the roof: run A is already up through the wreck's flank and the way up is inside a wall, and he does not try. f206 to f224 he dabs the ink on his collar with a pocket square, and it does not come off, and he keeps dabbing. f222 he pulls a spare die off the bandolier and re-seats it with a click, learning nothing. **From f224 he is BACKGROUND: run B walks him up-chute, mid-depth and right, softening and shrinking as he goes.** f228 he yanks the tarp over the wreck as he passes it. f240 he crosses the chute passage. At f254 he is at x=890, 45%, feet y=688, head y=598, mid-step, the chute arm's tip hanging at (838, 604) across his head-line, still falling, and his nub still dabbing the collar.
  **L3 THE GATE, f120 to f254:** f120 the crane jib swings the prefabricated section in from off-frame left at rail height. f120 to f148 it tracks right and HUNTS, swinging laterally on its chains, overshooting twice, a slow pendulum in x, never a drop from top of frame. f126 to f150 the attendant walks it down on the wand. f132 the top chord catches the slack festoon and drags it. **f150 it DROPS into its sockets, a deep tarmac boom, and two hi-vis Claudes torque the foot plates.** f152 / f158 / f164 / f170 four rivets fire ascending in pitch, the fourth hardest with a screen shake. **Bolted and ringing by f170, eighteen frames before he swings at it.** f170 to f254 the plate micro-oscillates on its mounts and never fully settles. At f254 it is still ringing.
  **L4 THE ARM, f202 to f254, the scene's payoff:** f202 the pre-owned red band is riveted onto the height post, a fifth rivet, deliberately smaller and lower than the four. f204 the plateless rookie arrives. **f206 to f210 the arm LIFTS**, a clean pneumatic sigh, and the shaft rotates a few degrees along the whole rail so you see the mechanism decide. f210 to f216 he walks under and away into the dark, alone. f216 the hero steps up. **f218 the arm SLAMS**, one flat dry clack on the rubber stop, no shake, no sting, quieter than the rivets, because it is procedure and not a punchline. f218 to f254 it sits there ticking on the stop, and it is still ticking on the cut. f236 the same shaft starts the chute arm down over the villain, and it is still descending at f254.
  **L5 LIGHT ORDER, f0 to f254, no inert stretch:** sodium buzz f0 to f60. Near masts blow f60 to f72. **Sub-bass rumble in at f68, under the last ping.** Near-black f72 to f75, three frames only. **Deep sodium masts blow in a wave at f92 / f112 / f132 / f150**, so the pivot marches away from camera for 90 frames after the near two are already dead. Flood banks 1 to 6 struck by hand on the spider box at f88 / f100 / f112 / f124 / f140 / f248, each with a flicker-stutter, each scaled: bank 1 fills a third of the frame, bank 6 is a blink. **Bank 6 is STILL striking on at f254 and its shaft has not reached the floor.** The building is not finished when we cut.
  **L6 GROUND ORDER, f180 to f254:** the paint buggy walks the near line f180 to f254 and is still 60px short of the point. Armco run A, six kicked latches walking toward the mic, f196 to f206. Armco run B, kicked latches receding, f224 to f250. The chute arm starts down f236 and never lands.
  **L7 TEARDOWN, f95 to f254:** four bolts reverse out of the JIFFY LOOB sign f95 to f126, it drops at f126, and it is STILL rocking on its corner at f254. The boom lift then tracks right-to-left along the facade from f130, dropping one fascia letter every 30 frames at f130 / f160 / f190 / f220 / f250, basket still tracking and one letter still in his nub at f254.
  **L8 CODE-RAIN, f0 to f254:** four columns of sickly yellow-green glyphs fall over the villain continuously. f0 to f188 dense and confident. **f72 to f75 they are the only lit thing in the frame, in the air and in the puddle, and they are what carries the near-black.** f188 the shatter GLITCHES them, they stutter, tear, drop a frame. f188 to f254 they run thinner and keep hitching, never recovering, still hitching at f254. His aura is damaged and you can see it.
  **L9 NIGHT CREW + CAMEOS, f0 to f254:** attendant squeegees, gets swapped to the wand at f112, guides the section f120 to f150, squeegees air with the wand f152 to f254. The sparky throws six breakers and is still leaning on the last lever at f254. Two hi-vis Claudes torque at f150; hi-vis A rivets the pre-owned band at f202. The bolt-gun Claude rides the picker up at f138, fires four, rivets a pointless bracket at f210, still working at f254. The latch-kicker walks two runs. The paint buggy Claude steps aside for the villain at f236 without looking at him. The teardown Claude never finishes. The plateless rookie walks through and away. The left-out rookie rotates his upside-down plate at f244 and copies the hero's measure at f248.
  **L10 ATMOS, f0 to f254:** the dust ladder above, unbroken by handoff. The wreck's smoke column leaks continuously and **from f140 it climbs into flood shafts 4, 5 and 6 and lights up white as each strikes**, so it is a background layer, not a garnish. Grit jumps on every rivet. The toppled tyre rolls its arc from f170. The wheel-nut rolls its slow arc all scene. **The single shard** spins at the grate's lip from f188 and is still spinning down at f254. The banner rattles, the tube sign strobes, the bin shreds flap, the festoon twitches.
  **L11 CAMERA, f0 to f254:** unbroken push-in, never pauses, never arrives, plus a lazy 5 degree crane DOWN from f150 that settles the frame's weight onto the left passage, so the sharp near-left at f254 is the hero under the bar and the villain is a soft shrinking figure up the chute at the right.
  **AT FRAME 254, still in motion:** flood bank 6 striking on and the sparky still on the lever, the pedestrian arm ticking on its stop across the hero's face, the chute arm descending and not landed, the paint head 60px short, the plate ringing, the code-rain hitching, the hero's nub under the red band with the gap already seen, the villain mid-step still dabbing, the shard still spinning, the JIFFY LOOB sign still rocking, the attendant still squeegeeing air, the left-out rookie mid-measure, the teardown basket still tracking with a letter in his nub, the festoon twitching, the tyre rolling, the wheel-nut rolling, the smoke climbing white through shaft 5, the banner rattling, the tube sign strobing, dust in five shafts, the plateless rookie still shrinking into the dark, the tin silhouette still walking its lap, camera still travelling. Nothing has parked.

- **Escalation:** S2 ended in personal panic: one exhausted driver stomping a pedal in a cramped cabin, the only safety in the car, losing. S3 answers on the same axis, who does the watching, and escalates it from a person to an INSTITUTION, and the scale jumps from a footwell to a floodlit lot receding to a back fence. The curve: **DOWN first**, and hard. Beat 1 is the lowest the hero gets in the whole reel: on his knees, pleading at YOU, and getting forged on the forehead for it. Then a genuine floor, but not a void: the sodium dies ON his stamped face, the frame drops to near-black for three frames only, and the mix floor RISES as the light goes, the sub-bass in at f68 under the last filament ping. Absence is where the thumb moves, so the scene loses the light without ever losing the sound. Then the rise is not a spike, it is a crew building something and it is relentless: breaker, breaker, breaker, sign down, a jib swinging steel across the frame, a boom into the tarmac, four rivets ascending. **The peak is f188, the SHATTER**, the loudest hit in the reel to this point, the first time the villain loses anything, and it is placed at 74% of the scene, on a plate that has been bolted and ringing for eighteen frames, with eighteen frames of clear air after the fourth rivet's shake so the two impacts never mush. Then the register goes cold and the scene hands the last quarter to the hero. No cheering, no gloat, no sting. **The payoff is quieter than the peak on purpose:** a bar lifts for one Claude with a soft sigh and slams for ours with one dry clack, and the loudest thing in the last 40 frames is a hum. The energy does not fall at the end, it FLATTENS into dread, which is what makes S4's dumb little tin bot land as a joke and a threat at the same time.

- **Gag / pop-culture:** All geometric and iconic, zero organic, zero real marks.
  1. **PRIMARY: THE WEIGH STATION / COMPOUND GATE.** The universal image of a steel gate across the road with a lift arm, a rule board on it, and a chute that funnels you off the open lot into an inspection lane. Pure geometry, instantly readable at thumbnail, and it says "you do not get to just drive on" without a word. It replaces the v1 Fight Club typography non-gag, which was typography pretending to be a joke.
  2. **THE RACK BUILT FOR A RULEBOOK.** Five slots, four of them empty with the bolt holes showing. Every institution you have ever met has a wall of rules. This one has one, and the four empty frames are the joke and the number at the same time.
  3. **THE FAIRGROUND HEIGHT BAR**, folded in as the red band on the post: "you must be this proven to ride." The plateless one clears it and walks off alone; ours comes up short. It is funnier because our guy measures himself voluntarily, after nobody asked him to, and because a rookie behind him copies it.
  4. **THE NIGHT CREW.** A car park turns into a licence authority in eight seconds and the whole thing is done by six blokes in hi-vis at 3am who never once look up at the man in the shades. The joke is the indifference. He is a traffic cone to them.
  5. **THE RUNNING KNOCKOFF, JIFFY LOOB**, identical spelling to S1, committed as a real running gag: S1 promised CERTIFIED IN 5 MINUTES OR IT'S FREE, and S3 unbolts that promise and drops it face-down on the concrete. **It pays back at S5 with "WE DON'T CERTIFY OURSELVES ANYMORE"** (alt: "NOW WITH INDEPENDENT INSPECTION"). Same joke, funnier, on-thesis for the fresh-eyes scene, and it costs the number spine nothing.
  6. **THE IMMOVABLE OBJECT**, geometric version: the guy whose answer to every problem is to declare it done treats a law like a form, stamps it, and his tool explodes in his hand and throws him off his own car. He does not learn. He reloads.
  7. **MICRO-GAG, THE BANDOLIER.** He wears a bandolier of spare DONE dies across his chest, which tells you he has broken stamps before and has budgeted for it. Re-seating a fresh one with a click at f222 is the most in-character thing he does all reel.
  8. **MICRO-GAG, THE COLLAR.** His own counterfeit ink splatters his own white collar and will not dab out. The forger gets forged. It stays on him into S4.
  9. **MICRO-GAG, THE CONSCRIPTED ATTENDANT.** A crew Claude swaps his squeegee for a marshalling wand mid-swipe without breaking stride. He is brilliant at guiding the load down, and the second it is planted he goes straight back to squeegeeing the air with the wand for four straight seconds.
  10. **MICRO-GAG, THE PRE-OWNED BAND.** The red PROVEN line comes out of a tool bag scuffed, chipped and one bolt short. The institution keeps a stock of them. It has measured people before, and its loose corner never stops swinging.
  11. **MICRO-GAG, THE POINTLESS BRACKET.** The bolt-gun Claude rivets an unexplained bracket to the plate's underside after the rule is already up, because real institutions never stop adding hardware.
  12. **MICRO-GAG, UPSIDE-DOWN L.** The one rookie left on the free apron has his L-plate on backwards and quietly rotates it when he thinks nobody is looking, then copies the hero's self-measure four frames later.

- **Sprite costume:** Base rounded clay-pixel Claude mascot silhouette and face UNCHANGED for every body in frame. No generic humans anywhere.
  **HERO, the rookie:** warm clay `#D97757`, **eyes always visible**, that is the tell against the villain. Red-and-white L-PLATE worn square on the chest like a marathon bib. Driving-school cap one size too big, sitting flat and slightly askew, its brim at exactly the arm's rest height. Lanyard with a blank unlaminated provisional card, empty photo box, blank record strip, swinging on every move. Both nubs empty of authority all scene. New for S3: from f42 a crooked sickly yellow-green DONE smeared across his forehead, and the more he scrubs the wider it gets, and by f130 his nubs are yellow-green too, so he carries the villain's lie on his face and on his hands for the whole scene. Expression arc: pleading at the lens f0, rocked at f42, humiliated and still looking at YOU at f50, working at the puddle f96 to f148, chin up reading the plate at f172, ducking at f196, stopped dead an inch off the bar at f218, and at f240 quietly reading his own shortfall without flinching. He never gloats when the stamp breaks. He is not the one who beat the villain, the crew was, and the bar does not care either way.
  **VILLAIN, Agent Done, exactly per the bible, use `<Villain/>`, never hand-rolled:** identical canonical Claude mascot silhouette, he is a COPY. **Slate-grey `#5A5F6B`** clay. **Eyes NEVER visible**, opaque black wraparound shades with one hard white glint bar, and the glint flares at f150 when the section's shadow crosses him. Black jacket with sharp shoulder blocks, skinny black tie, white collar. Coiled earpiece wire down the back of the neck, two stacked rects and a curl. Mouth is a flat line and appears only at f188 on the shatter. **He never speaks, he only stamps.** Weapon: the fat counterfeit DONE stamp, ink sickly yellow-green `#A8B84A`, crooked and smeared. Aura: four thin columns of sickly yellow-green code-rain falling wherever he stands, on from f0, permanently glitched from f188. S3-only additions consistent with the bible: a bandolier of spare dies across the chest, and from f196 his own yellow-green ink across his own white collar.
  **CAMEOS, all Claude sprites:** the hi-vis forecourt ATTENDANT, grubby tabard, squeegee then wand, bent-handle bucket. THE SPARKY at the spider box. TWO HI-VIS CLAUDES in ear defenders with impact wrenches. THE BOLT-GUN CLAUDE in ear defenders with a trailing air line, in the picker. THE TEARDOWN CLAUDE in the boom-lift basket. THE LATCH-KICKER with a bar. THE PAINT-BUGGY CLAUDE. **THE PLATELESS ROOKIE**, no plate, no cap, no lanyard, walking out. **ONE LEFT-OUT ROOKIE**, dimmer and smaller, cap, upside-down L-plate, no lanyard. No instructor in this scene. The institution replaced him. That is the entire point of the beat.

- **Camera:** **Lens:** wide anamorphic, 24mm feel, slight barrel bend on the verticals so the uprights lean, long horizontal flares off the flood banks when they strike, shallow at the extremes so the foreground occluder blooms soft. Nothing load-bearing sits within 60px of either edge any more, so the barrel distortion now costs the scene nothing: the plate and the cast 1 are at x=601 to 773, dead inboard, and the height post is at x=250.
  **Height:** **KERB LEVEL, roughly a standing Claude's knee, looking slightly UP the whole scene, and the set agrees with it: ground line y=640, apron y=640 to 792, the lot receding into a shallow band at the top of the apron.** Everything is bigger than him, the lot goes away from him, and the two things that decide his night, the plate and the bar, are both above his eye line. Same low hero angle as S1's forecourt, which is what makes the transformation read as the SAME PLACE.
  **Movement:** one unbroken slow PUSH-IN across all 255 frames, never stopping, never arriving, plus a lazy **5 degree crane DOWN from f150 to f254 that settles the frame's weight onto the LEFT PASSAGE**, so the picture closes on the hero standing under a bar that will not lift, not on the villain's back. Two motivated shakes only, and they are 18 frames apart: the fourth rivet at f170 and the shatter at f188. The f218 slam gets no shake at all. At f254 the camera is still travelling, so even the frame itself has not parked.
  **Depth stack, keyed by named element because a kerb-level camera makes y useless as a depth key:**
  · **FOREGROUND OCCLUDER**, heavily defocused: the cracked wheel-stop and the sagging bollard chain bottom-left, drifting dust, and from f188 yellow-green shards tumbling through the near field past the lens. It gives the push-in real parallax and means the hero is always framed by something you have to look past.
  · **MIDGROUND, sharp, the only sharp band:** hero, villain, the wreck and its light bar, the puddle, the gate uprights and plate and both arms, the height post, the armco run A, the attendant, the drain grate and the shard.
  · **BACKGROUND, soft:** the facade and its dying tube sign, the festoon, the fallen JIFFY LOOB sign rocking, the teardown basket, the six flood masts firing away into depth, the four deep sodium masts dying in a wave, the empty two-post lift, the back fence and its banner, armco run B, the paint buggy, and **the S4 bay glow sitting on the vanishing point at (972, 625), 40x30, never sharp, never keyed.** The background is where the future is, and it is running four or more independent layers at every single frame of the scene, including f126 to f254.

- **Transition + SFX:** **IN:** hard smash-cut off S2's brake stomp straight into a LIVE forecourt: sodium buzz, distant traffic, a wet squeegee squeak and the faint hiss of code-rain from frame 0. **No headroom void.** The interrupt is a screaming cabin cutting to a wide orange car park where a small guy is begging the camera, and the mix never opens a hole for the thumb.
  **BEAT LIST:** f0 sodium buzz bed, distant traffic, one wet squeegee squeak, code-rain hiss. f30 a small tacky lick. f42 the flat wet SLAP of the stamp on the forehead, then two dry pats. f60 to f72 filament PINGS and falling glass as the two near masts blow. **f68 the sub-bass GROUND RUMBLE comes in UNDER the last ping and never leaves, so the floor of the mix rises as the light dies.** f72 to f75 near-black, three frames, carried by the rumble, the code-rain hiss and one dripping tap. Never silence. f88 to f140 five hand-thrown breakers, each a percussive electrical THUNK then a rising ballast whine, scaled by depth so bank 1 is a slab and the later ones recede and pan away. f92 / f112 / f132 / f150 four more distant filament pings as the deep masts die, panning away from you. f95 to f126 four bolts reversing out with a ratchet whir, then the flat aluminium BANG of the JIFFY LOOB sign hitting concrete and a decaying wobble that never stops. f112 a squeak cut short and a small servo click as the wand lights. f120 crane jib groan and chain rattle as the section swings in from the left. f120 to f148 one long wooden creak as it hunts. **f150 the section DROPS into its sockets: a deep tarmac BOOM, a settling shudder, then two impact wrenches rattling on the foot plates.** f152 / f158 / f164 / f170 four pneumatic rivets, CHA-CHUNK, CHA-CHUNK, CHA-CHUNK, CHA-CHUNK, ascending in pitch, the fourth landing hardest with a screen shake. f170 the tyre flops off the stack. **f188 THE SHATTER:** the biggest transient in the reel to this point, a fat ceramic CRACK plus a glass burst plus a wet ink splat, then the tiny bright skitter of shards across concrete into the drain grate. f188 a code-rain glitch stutter, digital, ugly, and it never cleans up. f194 to f200 three body thumps down the flank and the cracked lens popping off its wire and smashing. f196 one held beat of the villain saying nothing, because he never speaks. f200 the flat thud of him hitting the ground. f196 to f206 run A's six galvanized latch CLANGS, each closer to the mic than the last. **f202 one dry fifth rivet, smaller and lower than the four, as the pre-owned red band goes on.** f206 to f210 the arm LIFTS: a clean pneumatic sigh and a soft up-stop tap. **f218 the arm SLAMS: one flat dry CLACK on a rubber stop, no shake, no sting, quieter than the rivets, then it just sits there ticking.** f222 a small precise CLICK as the spare die seats. f224 to f250 run B's clangs, receding. f228 the heavy canvas WHUMP of the tarp. f236 to f254 the chute arm's slow servo whine that does NOT resolve. f248 bank 6's tiny distant blink and tick.
  **BED:** the ground rumble f68 to f254, and from f140 a low institutional hum fading UP under everything, so the scene ends on the sound of a building running rather than on a hit.
  **OUT into S4:** cut on nothing. The hum, the unresolved servo whine and the still-spinning shard carry across the cut into S4's dawn bay, so the machine you hear at the end of S3 is the one already working when S4 opens.

- **Mute check:** "A steel gate lands across the car park, the grey guy in shades smashes his stamp on it and a fence walks him off down a lane, and then the bar lifts for a Claude with no learner plate who walks off alone into the dark, and slams down in front of the one still wearing his, who backs against the post and comes up short of the red line." That sentence contains **alone** and it contains **proven**, and it needs zero VO and only one stencil to get there. Even a viewer who reads nothing still gets: a car park turns into an official facility around two characters, the one in shades treats the big sign like a form and his tool explodes in his hands, and then a bar makes a decision about two guys in one second based on the square of plastic on their chests. The L-plate does the work of "not proven," the shattered die does the work of "his approval is worthless," the lifting bar does the work of "alone," and the slam does the work of "not yet."

- **Hook into next:** **The object carried forward is the TARP.** His last act in frame is to flick a green tarp over the burnt car as the barrier walks him past it, so the final beat of S3 is the villain hiding evidence, and it is the last thing he will ever get to hide. **The question the viewer is now holding:** the machine has him in a lane, but that thing is enormous and expensive and slow, so what actually catches him? **S4's surprise is not that the tin bot exists, it is that the tin bot is what CATCHES him.** The plant sharpens that instead of spoiling it: S3 spends 8.5 seconds welding up a compound, and S4 opens on the cheapest, dumbest thing in it, a knee-high two-cent wind-up bot walking a lap at dawn, who pulls that exact tarp off. The gate did not catch him. The tin toy did. Also handed forward: the yellow-green ink on his collar (he wears his own lie into S4), the cracked-and-reloaded stamp (survives here so S7 can confiscate and crush it), the hero's forehead DONE (scrubbed off by S5 when he is promoted), **the L-plate (comes off once, at S7's 20th punch, S8 opens plateless)**, and the red PROVEN band (the physical ancestor of S7's 95 gauge, planted here with zero digits so it costs the number spine nothing). Per the binding continuity fix, no 0/20 count is seeded on the top rail: the rail stays dumb house furniture and S7's punch rail delivers the 20 unspoiled. **Flag to the S5 author: the JIFFY LOOB payoff line is "WE DON'T CERTIFY OURSELVES ANYMORE," NOT "NOW 20 MINUTES," because a 20 on a sign in S5 collides with the locked spine two scenes before S7 earns it.**

- **⛔ CRITIC FIXES (binding on the build):**
  - **1. Rule ENACTED, not asserted.** f204 to f218 contrast pass at the left passage: plateless rookie in, arm LIFTS, he walks under and away alone into the dark; hero steps up behind, arm SLAMS in his face. Same gate, same shaft, same second, opposite answer, decided by one glyph on the chest. Zero reading. The boom is now the scene's payoff, not set dressing, and the mute sentence contains alone and proven.
  - **2. Hero owns f190 to f254.** The villain's dab, reload, tarp and herd all continue but are staged mid-depth and right, softening as they recede. The sharp near-left is the hero. The crane-down settles on him under the bar, not on the villain's back. Also fixed the earlier spectator hole: f96 to f148 he is not watching lights, he is on his nubs in the puddle trying to wash the forgery off his own face while each flood strike re-colours his reflection.
  - **3. Plea aimed at the LENS.** Hero begs YOU, card held out to camera; the villain leans in over his shoulder and answers for him. He never asks the villain for anything, so the frame can never read as him wanting the fake DONE.
  - **4. The band is now the gate's gauge and it delivers a verdict.** Moved off the upright onto the height post at the passage, red band at **y=478**, 162px clear of the ground line so it reads as a mark and not a horizon. Head under the band = arm down; the plateless one clears it = arm lifts. **He LOOKS at f240** and sees his own nub sitting a clear gap under the red line, then holds it there to the cut.
  - **5. + 19 RECONCILED (they contradicted each other).** Fix 5 kills the leg shield and casts the 1 huge into the plate's left end on a five-slot rack with four empty bays: the number is now architecture, not decoration, which voids fix 19's premise that a decorative shield carries the scene's number. Fix 19's genuinely good beat is transplanted, not lost: **the pre-owned, chipped, one-bolt-short RED PROVEN BAND arrives on a fifth rivet at f202** out of a hi-vis Claude's tool bag, preserving "predates everyone / the institution keeps a stock," costing zero digits, and making the scene's verdict instrument arrive on camera instead of sitting there as furniture. The pointless bracket is kept too, at f210.
  - **6. Peak un-mushed.** Section in f120, hunts f120 to f148, planted and torqued f150, rivets **f152 / f158 / f164 / f170** (fourth = shake, hardest). Bolted and ringing by f170. Hero reads it f172. Villain crouches f176, springs f180, **CONTACT f188, 18 frames of clear air after the rivet shake.** The attendant's wand swap moved to **f112** so the wand can actually guide the load in, instead of a robot arm dropping off a structure that is still swinging.
  - **7. + 23 RESOLVED, one chute stated once.** Wreck and tarp moved INTO the chute corridor (wreck base y=700 to 748, tarp at (740, 706)), armco on the villain's downstage flank running the perspective line **(770, 697) to (430, 792)** so it seals his retreat and leaves only depth, lane paint drawn as two lines CONVERGING on the point, boom re-sited to the same chute. No steel ever stands between him and the last thing he touches. The story paragraph and the coordinates are now stated in the same terms.
  - **8. Camera and ground line agree, and everything is re-hung off it.** Committed to the low camera: **ground line y=640, apron y=640 to 792, hero's head near y=520, uprights driven in at y=700.** Every element previously hung off y=430 has been re-hung, per fix 8's own instruction that elements built on the old ground line inherit the error. **Where fix 8's plate line ("y=150 to 255, towering") collided with fix 22 (no overhead portal, bottom lip at eye level), fix 22 wins:** it is a board-level collision ruling and "towering overhead" is the exact read it forbids. Plate now y=400 to 590, bottom lip at the eye line of a body at the gate's depth.
  - **9. People built it, at 3am.** No self-assembly anywhere. Crane jib swings the section in; the conscripted wand guides it down; two hi-vis Claudes torque the foot plates; the bolt-gun Claude rides a cherry-picker instead of arriving on the load; a sparky throws every single flood bank by hand on a spider box; a latch-kicker kicks up every armco segment; a Claude walks the paint buggy. **The masts are the lot's pre-existing dead hardware, energised, not born out of the tarmac,** which also fills the sky from frame 0.
  - **10. Retention cliff protected.** Near-black cut from 8 frames to **3** (f72 to f75), the sub-bass rumble moved in to **f68** under the last filament ping so the mix floor rises as the light falls, and the 20-frame near-silence on the smash-cut in is deleted: the scene opens on a live bed. The palette pivot survives intact; the void does not.
  - **11. Background live from f126 to f254.** Added the teardown boom lift dropping a fascia letter every 30f from f130 with one still in his nub at f254; four deep sodium masts blowing in a wave at f92 / f112 / f132 / f150 so the pivot marches away from camera; the fallen JIFFY LOOB sign rocking to f254; the draped festoon; the strobing tube sign; the rattling banner; and the wreck's smoke re-filed as a background layer that lights up white in shafts 4, 5 and 6 from f140. L2's f0-to-f254 claim is now true rather than asserted.
  - **12. Upper-left and upper-centre specified from frame 0.** Festoon across the void to a bent pole at (120, 300); the shuttered unit x=180 to 560 with caged windows, a dead tube sign on a broken ballast, a skip and a burst bin bag; the tyre stack that loses its top tyre on the fourth rivet's shake (re-timed to **f170**); the chain-link back fence and its rattling banner; plus the six pre-existing dark flood masts, which are verticals in the sky at f0. **No roller shutter anywhere: S0 owns that verb.**
  - **13. Masts read as depth, with the y values re-hung to the new ground line.** Scales kept verbatim (100 / 74 / 55 / 40 / 29 / 20) and each mast now carries an explicit head AND base that converge on the point: 1 (110, head 95, base 790) · 2 (340, 228, 742) · 3 (545, 324, 706) · 4 (730, 404, 682) · 5 (870, 462, 664) · 6 (975, 511, 650). The strike is scaled too: bank 1's flicker fills a third of the frame, bank 6 is a blink and a tick.
  - **14. Vanishing point stated, everything built off it.** **VP (972, 640)**, on the ground line, per the committed camera. Converging lane lines replace the constant-y pair. **S4 bay glow moved onto the VP at (972, 625), 40x30**, and the depth stack is now keyed by named element instead of a y-band, because a kerb-level camera makes y useless as a depth key. Boom re-sited to the chute passage, scaled to that depth. **Herd starts f224**, so he is at x=890 under the descending arm on the cut frame.
  - **15. Villain on the roof, not inside it.** Roof plane set explicitly at **y=520** and he is given a FOOT anchor at **(640, 520)** with his raised foot on the light bar, head at y=250. **The light bar is now in the set list** (x=600 to 690, y=506 to 520, one cracked lens on a wire), it rattles on every rivet, swings loose after the fourth, and its lens is knocked off during his fall.
  - **16. The system takes his high ground, he does not surrender it.** The plate is architecture and does not move: **the recoil throws HIM.** f188 contact, f188 to f194 blowback ragdolls him backwards off the roof, f194 to f200 he tumbles down his own podium's flank, f200 he lands flat. Then the floor plan does the rest: armco run A comes up **through the wreck's downstage sill and door at f196 to f206**, so the step, the wing and the door frame he climbed at f0 are inside a galvanized wall and the only face left to him is a sheer burnt haunch. He glances at the roof at f204 and does not try. (This is the physically buildable version of fix 16's intent under fix 7's tarp requirement: the fence takes the way UP without ever standing between him and the tarp.)
  - **17. + 26 MERGED. The queue is CUT.** No rope, no tickets, no wickets, no bodies in the lane mouth: the queue is S7's and DMV dressing is banned outside S6 and S7. **One rookie survives**, moved to the free apron behind the hero at **(120, 736)**, watching the villain get walked past, upside-down L-plate rotated at f244, and copying the hero's self-measure at f248 so the measure has an audience and the empty left third gets a body. **The lane now contains exactly one occupant: the villain.**
  - **18. Dust rewritten as a handoff ladder.** Two sodium cones f0 to f60 (warm amber), glass dust through the dying cones f60 to f72, then **the villain's code-rain is the only lit particle field in the near-black and it carries it**, then dust enters each flood shaft AS IT STRIKES and never leaves. **Bank 6 carries no dust, because at f254 it has no shaft.**
  - **20. The puddle is promoted to a numbered set item and made load-bearing.** (330, 725), 180x38, beside the grate. It mirrors sodium, goes black except one code-rain band in the near-black, takes each flood shaft as that bank strikes, **carries the gate's inverted reflection from f120 so the rule arrives in the water a beat before it arrives in the frame**, and shatters into rings on the fourth rivet that have not settled at f254. It is also the hero's f96 to f148 workplace. The attendant never gets it dry.

The plant is kept and BOUND: under 2% of frame, 40x30 max, on the VP, never keyed, never sharp, no cut ever moves toward it, never in the same read as the villain. Hook bullet corrected: S4's surprise is that the tin bot is what CATCHES him, not that it exists.
  - **22. S3 does not get an overhead portal, and the word for S6's object appears nowhere on this card.** Re-blocked as **THE ONE-RULE GATE**, ground-anchored, driven into the tarmac from below, blocking at eye level. The plate's arrival is lateral (a crane jib swings it in from off-frame left at rail height and plants it), never a descend-and-hunt from top of frame onto a subject: that move and that silhouette are reserved for S6 per the board's Collision-1 ruling. Board reads S3 = a door, S6 = a machine, S7 = a counter, and this is a door. **Note for the board: the villain bible's arc line ("THE RULE lands as a steel gantry") is superseded on the word only; the substance it locks (the rule lands, his stamp shatters on it, he is herded into the lane, first loss) is delivered in full.**

  - **27. The wreck matches S1's shipped signature** (roughly nine overlapping yellow-green DONE stickers, both front wheels gone, one door on the concrete, cowl smoking), and **S1's two-post lift stands in frame empty, arms down, dim, deep, as SET dressing only**, with the wreck rolled off it onto the apron. Same-place recognition now rests on three anchors (lift, JIFFY LOOB sign, wreck) instead of the sodium colour alone, and the tarp beat hides legible evidence rather than one curling sticker.

---

### S4 TIN BOT

- **Takeaway:** The thing that catches him is not smart, it is just there every single morning, it cannot be talked to, and its only output is a red flag it puts up itself.

- **Base object:** **THE TARP.** A heavy grey canvas dust sheet DRAPED over a wreck-shaped lump and weighted at the corners with what was to nub (three brake discs and a coil of his own rope laid loosely over the sunk floor rings, never tied through them), still fluttering from the heat underneath it. Its verb is **UNCOVER**, and that verb exists nowhere else in the reel. Collision audit: S0 owns the roller shutter and the padlock, S1 owns the lift and the sticker gun, S2 owns the dual-control brake pedal, S3 owns the rule gantry, S5 owns the order rail and the sealed pod, S6 owns the press and PASS/FAIL, S7 owns the keys and the gauge, S8 owns the open road and the revolve. Explicit kills carried from the v1 critic: **no turntable** (S8 owns the revolve, this bay uses a fixed inspection pit and ramp), **no tick/cross paddle** (S6 owns PASS/FAIL, this bot triages), **no pen** (S5 owns writing), **no door cycle** (S0 owns the shutter, so the roller door here is dead, shut and static for all 105 frames and never moves once), **no tyre-man decal** (trademark, cut with no replacement). New kills this pass: **no mushroom button** (cut outright, a prop whose only job was to not be pressed), **no separate klaxon pillar** (the beacon now lives on the mast head), **no anchored rings** (nothing was ever tied, he was sloppy).

- **Story / metaphor:** The villain worked all night. After his stamp shattered on the rule in S3, Agent Done did the only thing a self-grader can still do: he **hid the evidence**. He dragged the S1 wreck into the bay before dawn, put the fire out, threw a tarp over it, weighted the corners with three brake discs and a coil of rope because he could not be bothered to tie anything, smoothed the canvas flat with his own nubs, and stood back. Between checks, he is safe. That is his entire remaining business model, and he is running it with a **dead gun**: S3 split the barrel, the spring is hanging out of the crack, the ink pad is dried and cracked, and it is slung useless in his off nub. He cannot stamp any more. He can only peel.
  Then the cheapest object in the building finishes winding itself and starts its lap. A knee-high tin toy with a wind-up key, one googly eye and a 2c price tag trundles along a groove it has polished into the concrete over a thousand identical mornings, with **one hard torch bulb in its chest throwing a low beam that rakes the floor ahead of it**. That beam is the whole character: it is a check, it costs no words, and it points at the FLOOR, which is exactly why the con cannot reach it.
  The villain runs the con he has run on every human in this reel. He steps into the lane, crouches, hand-peels a single loose counterfeit sticker off the roll and offers it down at the bot's eye level, sickly yellow-green, crooked, smeared. **The bot drives into his shin, backs up, turns ninety degrees, and goes around him.** It never looked at the sticker. It cannot look at the sticker. Its beam is on the concrete and its one googly eye is loose.
  What beats him is not luck, it is procedure. The beam finds the dead wheel jutting from the hem, the bot noses in to look at what its beam found, and the loose corner rope takes its axle. It keeps driving, because it is still wound, and the cover walks off the lie in one long slither. The beam crosses the flank, finds the **one red-crossed panel**, and a dumb photocell linkage pops the flag arm up out of the clip on its back. Red light in, flag out. No judgment to corrupt, which is why there is nothing here to corrupt. It rams the staff into the mast and the red goes up because the bot put it there.
  What the villain loses here is **concealment**, and only concealment. His roll goes back in his jacket and he walks out of this scene still armed, because S5 is where his power actually dies. What escapes is exactly one thing: the single counterfeit he peeled for the bot, refused, dropped, and skittering away downhill out of his reach, still going when we cut. That is the sticker S6 bins.
  The cruelty is that the bot never notices it won. It does not gasp at the wreck. It does not point. It just keeps driving, because it has a lap to finish and a crank to get back to.

- **Proof / real UI:** **The project must read as a real codebase, not a cartoon car, or the VO's "checks your project" has no project in it.** The wreck's body panels are believable stencilled repo tiles laid out like a file tree wrapped onto the chassis: `src/`, `api/`, `db/`, `tests/`, `.env`, each carrying a small check, and **ONE panel carrying a red cross with a thin red test-runner bar burning across it**, the exact visual a real red build gives you. They are rendered at texture scale so they read as a rash of tiles and never as sentences, the same way the nine DONE stickers read as a rash and never as words. Scabbed crooked over those tiles are his nine counterfeit yellow-green DONE stickers from S1, and one of them is slapped **half over the failing panel with the red bar burning out from under its edge**: his pass does not even cover the failure it is claiming. That single object is the reel's thesis. It is the panel the torch beam finds at f60, it is why the flag goes up, and it converts the reveal from "someone hid a broken car" into "your build is red and someone put a sticker on it." No invented count, no new number, so the locked spine (a week in ONE night, ONE rule, 20 passes, 95%, below 90) stays clean. The bot reads as a real cheap tier without naming a model or a price: tin, matte, knee-high, wind-up, one torch bulb, plastic vest, a 2c tag, against the rookie's soft premium clay. **This field is binding and the build may not quietly skip it again.**

- **Set detail (Alex's #1 note):** Panel-local, 1012 x 792, high angle, cold blue-grey dawn, one hard latticed shaft.
  1. **THE TARP** (hero prop), x 400-720, y 355-585. Heavy grey canvas, **draped, not tied**: three brake discs and a loose coil of his own rope laid ON TOP of the sunk floor rings, never threaded through them. A wreck-shaped lump under it. It **breathes**: residual engine heat lifts and drops the centre on a 22f cycle from f0, and one thin wisp of smoke escapes a fold at (610, 400) for the whole scene. Rope-lays and canvas creases catch the shaft as raised specular ridges.
  2. **THE WORN CIRCUIT** (the "every morning" prop, and it is legible at f0 with zero reading). A shallow wheel-width groove buffed **mirror-bright** into the concrete, circling the bay in a closed loop from the charge post at wall-left, along the front edge, out past the pit rail and back. Thousands of identical mornings, visible as polish. The bot is already on it at f0. The shaft crosses it and it **glints**. A painted lane runs inside it with **the paint worn clean away in two parallel wheel stripes**. At the end the dragging tarp scuffs dust across the polish, which is the one morning the routine broke.
  3. **THE DAILY CHECK STENCIL** (the scene's ONE header), floor stencil inside the worn lane, x 250-620, y 655-700, reading up at the camera. Worn, repainted, and **scrubbed thin down its middle by a thousand laps**. It is up from f0 to f104, so unlike a flag word it is legible for the entire scene, and it buys the VO's "every morning" clause outright. It is the same label S9's recap module carries, so the callback is already paid for.
  4. **THE CHECK-CARD SPIKE**, nail on the pit rail at (760, 500). A **fat thumbable stack of punched daily check-cards**, hundreds deep, each one a morning. The bot's return path passes it. Nobody reads it, it just has to be FAT.
  5. **THE WIND-UP CHARGE POST**, wall crank at (80, 470), and it **fires at frame 0 instead of claiming**: the crank is **still ratcheting to a stop** f0 to f10, brass krrk decaying, because the bot has just wound itself and left, as it does every morning. Behind it, a **bot-height crescent hammered into the wall plaster** and the handle **worn through to bare brass**. The post is the thing the bot's loop returns to, which is the only maintenance this thing will ever get.
  6. **THE ESCAPED WHEEL**, (440, 592). S1's runaway wheel, jutting flat out from under the tarp's near hem. It is **DEAD and lying flat on frame 0**, no wobble, no spin. It is not a runaway any more, it is a clue: the beam finds it, and the reveal pays it off when you see the empty hub it came out of.
  7. **THE INSPECTION PIT AND RAMP**, pit trench x 330-790, y 295-635, steel grating ramp over it.
  8. **THE OVERHEAD CATWALK** (new, and it is what makes the signature texture physically possible), a grated steel catwalk slung between the clerestory and the floor at y 120-180. **The shaft arrives already latticed** because it passes through this grating, so the lattice genuinely crawls across the tarp as dawn climbs. It earns its own layer: **rust flakes and dust sift down through it continuously**, f0 to f104.
  9. **THE PIT LAMP**, caged work-lamp on a coiled cord hanging into the pit at (360, 430), **repointed to where it is physically true**: it throws its moving lattice UP THE BACK WALL through the floor grating, on a slow 40f swing cycle for all 105 frames. That puts a live moving layer directly behind the sleeping rookie's bench, where the frame previously had nothing but blur.
  10. **THE CLERESTORY AND THE WALKING SHAFT**, window x 40-250, y 15-105. One hard blue-white shaft rakes down-right through the catwalk grating. **The widen is load-bearing, not decoration:** at f0 its pool sits low-right on empty concrete (x 470-800, y 560-660), and as dawn climbs the pool **walks UP-LEFT onto the wreck's flank and the villain's mark**, arriving at x 430-700, y 380-560 by f60, exactly as the canvas leaves. The light reaching what he hid, at the moment it is uncovered, is the shot. Dust motes drift up through it continuously.
  11. **THE PEGBOARD**, x 150-340, y 165-310. Painted tool-shadow outlines with **three tools missing from their outlines** (he took them last night). **One torque wrench swings gently on its hook** in a draught, f0 to f104, and it is load-bearing: it is the object the rookie's nub is reaching for at f104, and S5 opens by slapping his nub off it.
  12. **THE VILLAIN'S CODE-RAIN**, 4 thin columns of sickly yellow-green glyphs, falling wherever he stands, always on per the bible. They **spatter and pool on the tarp canvas**, which is how you find him in the frame. They **desync at f42** and stay broken through the cut.
  13. **THE DEAD STICKER GUN**, slung in his off nub. A **hairline split across the barrel, the spring hanging out of the crack, the ink pad dried and cracked**. S3's wound, carried on his body. This is why he is reduced to hand-peeling.
  14. **THE ONE ESCAPED STICKER**, fires at f58, not pre-placed. Until then it is in his nub.
  15. **THE BENCH AND THE ROOKIE**, bench x 830-1000, y 400-490, in the bay's shadow. Rookie Claude asleep, hard hat tipped over his eyes, L-plate square on his chest, one nub trailing on the concrete. Hard hat **rises and falls with breathing** f0 to f100. **No ON CALL lettering**: a stencilled bunk-and-moon pictogram on the bench back instead, which costs zero header budget. The system is not spending him today, and that is the point of the scene.
  16. **THE FLAG MAST**, spring-loaded mast on the pit rail at (800, 500-590), with an **empty socket at exactly bot height** and **the caged beacon mounted ON ITS HEAD**. Flag-up and red-on are now one object, one event, one place in frame.
  17. **THE OIL DRIP AND PAN**, pan moved onto **solid concrete at the ramp's edge (430, 655)**, so the drip has somewhere to land instead of falling on a grating over a pit. One drip every 14 frames, surface rippling out each time. It is the metronome of the room and it works under a voice.
  18. **THE FLOOR DRAIN**, grating at (300, 620), directly on the worn lane. **Its edge is polished bare on the exact spot the wheel catches**, and the bot's shoulder dent matches that spot. Bump 1 is therefore not a fresh accident, it is **the same accident it has every morning and never learns from**, which is the takeaway rendered as wear.
  19. **THE ROLLER DOOR AND THE SCUFF TRAIL**, door at the **back upper-right (x 860-1012, y 150-330)**, shut, dead, never cycles. Sickly yellow-green heel scuffs run from its threshold **diagonally down-left, receding, to the tarp at (700, 585)**. Evidence he left himself, and the trail does the exact job the high angle is there to do. The bot's wheels track straight over them without registering a thing.
  20. **THE 2c PRICE TAG**, cardboard tag on a string tied to the wind-up key, **scaled to price-gun-tag size, not realistic size**. Flapping f0 to f104 but **staged for one clean read at f17 to f27**.
  21. **THE DAWN RIME**, along the pit rail y 300, x 330-790. **Static texture, not a timed beat.** It says cold. It does not perform.
  22. **THE MOTH**, circling the swinging pit lamp from f0 to f104, never flung, never paid off. Living world, no payoff required.
  **EXACT RENDERABLE TEXT, and the build holds this line:** ONE header, the **DAILY CHECK** floor stencil (f0 to f104). ONE number, the **2c** tag (lit read f17 to f27). **The flag is PURE RED with no word on it.** No ON CALL. The repo tiles and the nine crooked DONE stickers are overlapping, crooked and ink-smeared so they read as a rash of tiles and a rash of stickers, never as sentences, at thumbnail size. The maker's-mark pictogram and the coin slot are **flat texture only**, never a timed detail, because nothing on a knee-high sprite's back plate is visible from 4m up.

- **CONTINUOUS ACTION (fills the whole line):** 105 frames, eight named layers running independently, three bumps, and a hero acting on frame 0. **Every anchor below is bound to a word in `words_os.json`, not to a round number.** Local word map (S4 starts at global f692, 30fps): "Every morning," f1-13 · "a cheap model" f15-27 · "checks your project" f27-48 · "and asks one thing," f48-76 · **VO breath gap f76-81** · "does anything need attention?" f81-105, with "attention" at f98 running past the cut.
  **L1 TRUNDLE (the bot, the subject, and the largest highest-contrast most-moving thing in frame at every single frame).** f0 it is already mid-lap on the polished groove at (120, 610), wheels turning, key wound TIGHT (it just wound itself, see L6), **torch beam already raking the floor ahead of it**. It animates on 2s while the room animates on 1s, **except through the drag window, see L11**. f0-f14 it trundles right along the worn lane. **BUMP 1, f15**, THE SAME ACCIDENT AS EVERY MORNING: its wheel catches the drain's polished rim at (300, 620), the body wobbles, the googly eye rattles and lags a beat behind, **and the wobble flips the 2c tag up into the shaft's low pool**. f17-f27 the tag **holds flat and lit**, under the exact words "a cheap model." f19-f21 it frees itself and drives on. **f27-f34, THE CHECK ("checks"):** the torch beam rakes ahead across the concrete and up onto the tarp's near hem. **f34-f38, THE FIND:** the beam catches the dead escaped wheel jutting flat from the hem at (440, 592), and **the bot noses in toward what its beam found.** This is procedure, not luck: it drove at the thing because its beam lit the thing. **f40, THE SNAG:** the loose corner rope coil at (470, 596) wraps into its wheel axle. It does not notice. It just keeps driving. f40-f62 it drives right, dragging. **BUMP 2, f52**: it drives straight into the villain's shin at (560, 580), boink, backs up f54-f56, turns ninety f56-f58, goes around him f58-f62, **still dragging**. It did not look at him. **f60, THE OUTPUT:** the beam sweeps the uncovered flank and lands on the **red-crossed panel**, and a dumb photocell linkage **pops the red flag arm UP out of the clip on its back**. Red light in, flag out, no judgment in the loop. **BUMP 3, f66**: it hits the mast base at (800, 545). **f68-f74, THE ASK: it rams the flag staff into the mast socket at bot height. The flag leaves its body.** f74-f104 it is still driving, wheels still turning, key running down honestly, dragging the rope out through the bottom-right foreground plane, heading for the far side of its loop and the crank it comes back to tomorrow.
  **L2 THE CON (the villain, ONE con, given room, and everything else he does is REACTION).** f0-f38 crouched at (560, 430) ON the tarp, smoothing the canvas flat with both nubs, patting it, glancing at the back door, **dead gun slung in his off nub**. f38 he stands, head snapping to the tin noise. **f42 he lunges into the lane at (560, 580) and plants himself.** **f44-f52 THE ONE CON, 14 frames of room:** he crouches, **hand-peels a single loose counterfeit sticker off the roll** because his gun is dead, and offers it down at the bot's eye level, crooked and smeared. f52 the bot bonks his shin. **No reaction sound, he never speaks.** f54-f58 he tracks it backing up and turning and going around him, and his shoulders go rigid: **that single beat IS "it cannot be talked to," complete.** **f58 the sticker falls from his nub.** f62 he stops dead, upright, arms at his sides, **in the open, in the light** (the walked-up pool has arrived on his mark at (520, 520)), mouth a flat line for the first and only time. f62-f104 he does not move again. He is caught, and he is watching.
  **L3 THE TARP.** f0-f38 breathing on a 22f heat cycle, code-rain spattering on it, the catwalk lattice crawling across it. **f40-f62 THE DRAG, 22 frames, landing its start exactly on the word "project":** it peels off the wreck in one slow slither, **the loose weights just slide because nothing was ever tied**, one brake disc scraping off the ring and onto concrete at f44, f50 and f56 (the three-beat rhythm survives, it is discs now, not anchors), the canvas sliding right and down and **wiping through the sharp foreground plane at camera-RIGHT**, which is the physical wipe of the scene. **At f104 it is still sliding, still settling, not landed.**
