  **L4 THE REVEAL ("your project," f34-48, completing f52-f62).** f39-f48 the wreck's flank comes out from under **into the arriving light**, on the word "project": cold black chassis, one wheel gone with the dead wheel lying flat in front of the empty hub, door on the floor, **the repo tiles reading across the body panels, most checked, ONE red-crossed with the test-runner bar burning out from under the edge of a crooked yellow-green DONE sticker.** f52-f62 fully out. It does not move. It is the only still thing in the frame and that is why it lands.
  **L5 THE FLAG ("asks one thing," f48-76).** f60 the arm pops up out of the clip. f66 the mast bumped. f68-f74 the staff rammed home. **f74-f76 the spring fires and the PURE RED flag snaps up and boings**, overshooting and oscillating, landing dead on the word "thing," which is the sentence "little guy checks, finds a problem, plants a red flag" completed in picture, with no reading. **At f104 it is still oscillating on its spring.**
  **L6 THE RED, and it is ONE object in ONE place ("does anything need attention?").** **f78 the beacon ON THE MAST HEAD spins up**, landing in the VO's only breath gap, so the one moment of the scene with no words is the moment the room's light changes state. f81-f88 it **sweeps a red bar left across the floor with 8 frames to actually travel**: it crosses the uncovered wreck at f83 and the villain's shades at f86 (the glint bar goes hot). **f88 it lands on the bench**, on the word "anything."
  **L7 THE HANDOFF, and the depth stack itself performs it.** **f88 the red touches the rookie and he PULLS OUT OF THE 1.5px BLUR**, the background becoming the subject in one move. f92-f104, on "need attention?": his hard hat tips back, **his eyes (visible, the tell against the villain) go straight to the red-crossed panel**, and he stands **FOR the wreck**, not startled by a noise. **His nub is already reaching off toward the torque wrench on the pegboard as we cut.** At f104 he is clearly mid-stand, clearly full-size, clearly the warm-clay one with the L-plate, shadow stretching up the back wall, the uncovered red-crossed panel lit in front of him. That is the frame S5 cuts from and slaps his nub off the wrench.
  **L8 THE ROOM (never stops, f0 to f104).** The crank ratcheting to a stop f0-f10, pit lamp swinging its lattice up the back wall on a 40f cycle, rust and dust sifting down through the catwalk, dust drifting in the shaft, the pool walking up-left onto the wreck, drip landing every 14f into the pan on concrete, torque wrench swinging on the pegboard, **the one escaped sticker skittering downhill f58 to f104 and out through the bottom-right plane, still going at the cut**, price tag flapping, moth circling the pit lamp, code-rain desynced from f42, camera pushing.
  **L11 THE CLOCK LINKAGE (build note, binding).** Through the drag window **f40 to f62, the rope and the tarp's drag transform are PARENTED TO THE BOT'S STEPPED 2s CLOCK**, so the linkage is rigid and cannot pump. Only the canvas ripple and settle run on 1s. If the canvas reads wrong against a stepped drag, **drop the bot to 1s from f40 onward**: the craft flex is worth nothing next to a clean drag on the scene's most important beat.
  **AT FRAME 104, still in motion:** the bot driving out through the foreground plane, tarp mid-slide, flag mid-boing, beacon mid-sweep, sticker mid-skitter, code-rain still broken, rookie mid-stand and mid-reach, pit lamp mid-swing, drip mid-fall, catwalk dust sifting, camera pushing. **Nothing in this scene arrives at a resting pose.**

- **Escalation:** The reel's quietest scene, and **the pressure curve is carried entirely on PICTURE, because Alex is talking across all 105 frames and a silence-based curve is physically unavailable.** The room is built as three colour states and it climbs through them: **STATE ONE**, flat cold blue-grey ambient, one hard latticed shaft pooled on empty concrete, a toy on a polished groove, dead flat and unsympathetic. **STATE TWO**, the shaft's pool **walks up onto the thing he hid** and arrives exactly as the canvas leaves it, so the light itself performs the uncovering and the frame gets its first warm hard key on a subject. **STATE THREE**, the beacon red at f78, arriving on a subject that is **already lit** rather than rescuing an unlit one, and the room changes state for the first time in the scene. The rise underneath it is the villain's fall, staged as a subtraction: he starts crouched and busy and in control at f0, he gets **one** con and it fails inside 14 frames, and from f62 he does not move again. He does not get a chase, he does not get a lunge, he does not get eleven beats. He gets to stand in the open in the light and watch a two-cent toy take his cover off, which is a worse thing to do to him than any of them. The escalation is that **the frame gets brighter, the red arrives, and he gets stiller.** It exits at the highest point it has been, on the expensive model rising into a red bar with his nub already out for the wrench, and hands S5 a bay that is already flashing and a rookie already reaching for the exact tool S5 is going to take off him.

- **Gag / pop-culture:** All geometric and iconic, no organic references, no real marks. (1) **THE TABLECLOTH PULL, INVERTED.** The magician's yank played backwards and at quarter speed: nobody yanks, a rope gets caught in an axle and a small machine keeps walking. The setting does not survive. The joke is that it is not even a trick, and the corners were never even tied down. (2) **THE DOOR-STOP LOOP.** Bump, back up, turn ninety, go around. The single most universally recognised behaviour of the dumbest machine anyone owns, and here it is the exact mechanism that beats a genius. Bump one is the setup (the drain, **and the drain's rim is polished bare because it does this every morning and has never once learned**), bump two is the joke (the shin), bump three is the payoff (the mast). (3) **THE 2-FRAME WALK CYCLE.** The bot animates on 2s inside a frame animating on 1s. It has literally fewer frames than everyone else in the shot. Cheap tier as a craft flex, and it stands down for the drag because a clean drag outranks a flex. (4) **COIN-OP CHEAPNESS.** Rolled tin edges, two rivets holding its head on, a light dent in one shoulder **that matches the drain's rim exactly**, a wind-up key, a chest torch bulb, and a 2c cardboard tag on a string, staged for one lit read under the words "a cheap model." (5) **THE MATRIX RAIN GLITCH.** The villain's locked code-rain aura, established since S1, always on, **breaks for the first time in the reel at f42 as his cover physically walks off**, and stays broken through the cut. The con fails, the aura cracks. (6) **THE DEAD GUN.** He is carrying S3's wound: split barrel, spring hanging out, ink pad dried. The man who stamps cannot stamp, so he is down to peeling one sticker off a roll by nub like a man handing out flyers. (7) **LIVING WORLD:** a moth circling a swinging caged work-lamp for 105 frames, going nowhere, paid off never.

- **Sprite costume:**
  **SCENE HERO, THE TIN BOT.** Canonical Claude mascot silhouette, unchanged, stamped from **cheap matte tin** instead of clay, knee-high at roughly one third the rookie's height. Visible stamping seams down both flanks, rolled tin edges, two rivets holding the head on, **a light dent in one shoulder that matches the drain's polished rim**. **ONE googly eye**: a clear plastic dome with a loose black disc that rattles and always lags a beat behind every head turn. The other eye is a punched hole. **INSTRUMENT ONE, THE TORCH:** a fixed hard bulb in its chest throwing one low beam that rakes the floor ahead of it. It does not steer, it does not aim, it points at concrete, and that is precisely why a sticker held at its eye level is invisible to it. **INSTRUMENT TWO, THE RED FLAG:** a plain pure-red flag on a short staff, held in a sprung clip on its back, popped up by a dumb photocell linkage when the beam takes red, and **rammed into the mast socket by the bot itself**. A brass **wind-up key** in its back, tight at f0 and running down honestly across the scene, which reads as a fuel gauge because the frame-0 crank and the worn crescent already told you it refills every morning. A **2c cardboard price tag** on a string, price-gun-scaled. Two little tin wheels instead of feet, which is why it trundles and why it catches in the drain. A yellow plastic hi-vis vest, one size too big, riding up. Coin slot and maker's mark are **flat texture, never staged**. Its want is mechanical and legible: **finish the lap, get back to the crank.** Going around the villain reads as wanting past him, not as blank drift.
  **VILLAIN, AGENT DONE (`<Villain/>` component, with ONE binding scene override).** Canonical Claude mascot silhouette, unchanged, because he is a copy. **Slate-grey `#5A5F6B`** clay. **Eyes NEVER visible**: opaque black wraparound shades with one hard white glint bar, which goes hot when the red bar crosses it at f86. Black jacket with sharp shoulder blocks, skinny black tie over a white collar. Coiled earpiece wire, two stacked rects and a curl. Mouth a flat line, **only at f62 when he stops dead**. **He never speaks.** **⛔ SCENE OVERRIDE, HE ENTERS HOLDING S3's WOUND:** the fat counterfeit sticker gun is **DEAD**, hairline split across the barrel, spring hanging out of the crack, ink pad dried and cracked, slung and useless in his off nub. He does not stamp once in this scene because he cannot. **He hand-peels.** Ink stays sickly yellow-green `#A8B84A`. Aura: 4 thin columns of sickly yellow-green code-rain, always on, **desyncing at f42 and staying broken**. **He does not multiply here.** That is S5, once. **He exits S4 STILL ARMED:** the roll goes back in his jacket, because the arc gives him exactly one loss here and it is concealment.
  **CAMEO INTO SUBJECT, ROOKIE CLAUDE (the reel's protagonist).** Full size, soft premium warm clay `#D97757`, **eyes visible** (the tell against the villain), L-plate square on his chest, hard hat tipped over his eyes, asleep on the bench in background blur. **Pulls out of the blur at f88 and rises FOR the wreck from f92**, eyes on the red-crossed panel, nub already reaching for the torque wrench.

- **Camera:** **Lens feel:** a 35mm-equivalent held slightly long and flat, clinical and unsympathetic, heavy cool vignette, no warmth in the falloff. This is the only scene shot like a room rather than like a story, because the room does not care. **Height:** roughly 4m up, looking down at 35 degrees. The high angle does three jobs: the floor becomes a big trapezoid so the bot's whole polished lap is legible as a path, every sprite throws a long raking shadow toward camera, and it makes the villain look **small and caught, from above, in the open**, which is a shot the reel has not used on him yet. It is also why the roller door sits at the back upper-right and the scuff trail runs diagonally down-left toward camera: that receding path is exactly what a high angle is for. **Move:** a slow continuous 4% push-in plus a 3px left drift across all 105 frames, never arriving. From f78 the mast-head beacon adds a second moving key that swings every shadow in the frame. **Depth stack:** **FOREGROUND OCCLUDER, sharp and dark across the bottom-RIGHT 15%**, the pit rail and a coil of the villain's own discarded rope. It was moved off bottom-left with one edit that fixes four things: the bot's frame-0 entry at bottom-left is now clean and legible against the floor trapezoid, and the tarp, the bot and the escaped sticker **all exit through the plane**, so the wipe is real and the plane pays off three times instead of zero. MIDGROUND, tack sharp, the key plane lit by the walking shaft: the bot, the tarp, the wreck, the villain. BACKGROUND, 1.5px blur and desaturated cool: the pegboard, the bench with the rookie, the pit lamp's lattice crawling up the back wall behind him, the bay's back wall receding, and the dead roller door at back upper-right. **⛔ THE PULL FOCUS IS THE STORY BEAT:** at f88 the red bar touches the rookie and he **comes out of the blur**, so the depth stack itself performs the handoff and the background becomes the subject in one move. **Palette split:** cold blue-grey `#6B7A8C` ambient fill everywhere, one hard warm-white `#F5EBD8` latticed shaft whose pool **walks up onto the wreck's flank by f60**, and the beacon red arriving at f78 as the third state the room has never been in.

- **Transition + SFX:** **IN:** hard cut off S3's stamp shattering, straight onto a cold room tone, a drip and a decaying brass krrk. The pattern interrupt is the drop in **energy and temperature**, not a drop into silence, because the VO does not stop.
  **BEAT LIST:** f0-f10, the charge-post crank **still ratcheting to a stop**, krrk decaying, over a cold room tone, one oil drip tick, and tin wheels tk-tk-tk-tk on concrete. f15, hollow tin **BONK** plus a rattling googly-eye brrp as the wheel catches the drain's polished rim. f19, a servo whirr as it frees itself. f40-f62, **the long dry canvas DRAG**, rope creak, rising slither. f44, f50, f56, three **brake discs scraping off and landing on concrete**, ping, ping, ping. f52, a soft comic **boink** on the villain's shin, and no reaction sound, he never speaks. f54-f58, three reverse ticks and a whirr. **f56-f68, THE SFX-BUS DUCK, and it replaces the impossible audio hole:** the SFX bus (drag, canvas, rope, discs, wheels) ducks **hard** under a continuing VO and a continuing bed, landing the reveal on the last disc's ping and then only the tin wheels receding. **This is a bus duck, not a mix hole, and the build must not render it as literal silence over a talking voice.** f60, a small dry solenoid **tk** as the flag arm pops. f66, a tin clang on the mast. f74, spring-steel **SPROING-oi-oi** as the flag fires into the emptied bus and oscillates. f78, beacon motor **whummm** spinning up on the mast head, in the VO's breath gap. f42-f104, the code-rain glitch **zzt**, entering on the peel and holding broken. Under the cut, the tin wheels are **still going tk-tk-tk**, fading right.
  **BED:** the house music bed **runs under the VO for the whole scene and is ducked 3dB across f56-f68 at the reveal**. The reel does not go quiet in the middle of itself: a 3.5s mid-reel silence is a scroll trigger on the house chassis, not a flex. Under it, the constant low cold room tone, the drip every 14 frames, and the wind-up ratchet, all of which read fine under a voice.
  **OUT:** hard cut on the rookie mid-stand in the red bar with his nub out for the wrench and the bot's wheels still ticking, straight into S5's flash.

- **Mute check:** **Honest f60 freeze-frame, re-run against the real numbers:** the tarp is three quarters off and mid-slide toward bottom-right. Out from under it, arriving into the shaft's pool as it walks onto the flank, is a black wreck covered in stencilled file tiles, one of them wearing a **red cross with a red bar burning out from under the edge of a crooked yellow-green sticker**. The dead wheel lies flat in front of an empty hub. A knee-high tin toy sits in the middle of the lane with its torch beam on the red cross and **its red flag arm just popped up**. A man in a black suit and black shades is frozen upright in the open in the light with a counterfeit sticker falling out of his nub. A bigger, warmer Claude is asleep on a bench in the dark. **Read, no words needed: "the little toy pulled the cover off, the thing underneath is broken, and the toy has its hand up."** That lands at 2.0 seconds. The plant at f74 and the red at f78 are the exclamation mark on a sentence that is already finished, not the sentence itself. The floor stencil says DAILY and the polished groove says a thousand times, so the frame says **every** morning, not **a** morning. The 2c tag and the dents say cheap. The tarp coming off says hidden. The red flag says caught. The man in shades frozen in the light says who hid it.

- **Hook into next:** The flag is UP, it is pure red, it cannot be put back down, and the light on top of it is turning, so the morning cannot close. But the thing that raised it is **knee-high, still driving, and already leaving**: it found the problem and it is constitutionally incapable of fixing it or even caring, because it has a lap to finish and a crank to get back to. The open loop is exact: **something needs attention, the cheap one cannot touch it, so who does?** The beacon answers by sweeping the red bar onto the rookie at f88, and **he pulls out of the blur and rises FOR the wreck** from f92, eyes on the red-crossed panel, **nub already out for the torque wrench on the pegboard**. That is not a startle, it is a want, and it aims S4's loop directly at S5's reversal: he rises to grab the wrench, and **S5 opens by slapping his nub off it**. Objects handed forward: the **uncovered red-crossed panel** (now a job that needs a work order, which S5 writes), the **one escaped counterfeit sticker** still skittering downhill out of his reach (rejected and binned at S6), **the villain still armed** with the roll back in his jacket (he needs it to run the con on Fresh Eyes in S5, which is where his power actually dies), and the villain's **first glitch**, entering on the peel and still broken at the cut, which is the crack S5 widens.

- **⛔ CRITIC FIXES (binding on the build):**
  - **Timeline reconciliation, read this first.** Fixes 3, 13, 14 and 28 each proposed a different timeline and they are mutually incompatible (3 and 10 want the flag by f76 and red on the bench at f88; 14 wants the flag SPROING at f90-97 and red at f98-105). Resolved by taking fix 3 + fix 10's anchors as the spine (they agree, and they are the two fixes protecting the mute read and the S5 handoff) and applying **fix 14's METHOD**, binding every anchor to a verified word rather than to a round number. Re-derived from `words_os.json`, not eyeballed: peel f40 = "project" · flag plant f74 = "thing," (the ask IS the flag) · beacon f78 = the VO's only breath gap · red on bench f88 = "anything" · rookie rises f92 = "need attention?" (the answer to the question is the expensive model standing up).
  - **"Every morning" is now IN the frame, three ways, all legible at f0 with no reading:** the mirror-polished lap groove plus the painted lane worn away in two wheel stripes, the fat spike of punched daily check-cards on the bot's return path, and the DAILY CHECK floor stencil up f0 to f104.
  - **The re-wind FIRES instead of being claimed:** the crank is still ratcheting to a stop f0 to f10 with a bot-height crescent in the plaster and the handle worn to bare brass. The key still runs down, but that is now the honest back half of a cycle the frame already showed you, not "the toy is dying."
  - **The bot CHECKS and ASKS.** Torch bulb in the chest raking the floor = the check, and it is why a sticker at eye level is invisible to it. Beam finds the dead wheel at f34, the bot noses in at what its beam lit, and the rope takes it at f40: procedure, not luck. Beam finds the red cross at f60, a dumb photocell pops the flag arm, and the bot rams the staff into the mast itself at f68-74. The flag leaves its body because the bot put it there.
  - **Mute read re-timed to complete by f60** and the field now pastes the honest f60 freeze-frame instead of describing a scene the timeline does not stage.
  - **ONE con, 14 frames (f44-f58).** Cut the wave, the re-block and the knee-chase. Everything else the villain does is reaction, and from f62 he does not move again.
  - **Audio hole deleted.** Respecced as an SFX-bus duck f56-f68 under a continuing VO and a continuing music bed. No mid-reel silence. Escalation rewritten as a three-state PICTURE curve.
  - **The reveal arrives INTO the light.** The clerestory is repointed and the 8% widen is load-bearing: the pool walks up-left onto the wreck's flank and the villain's mark (x430-700, y380-560) by f60, arriving exactly as the canvas leaves.
  - **Proof / real UI restored,** repo tiles back on the body panels with one red cross and a red test-runner bar, plus the new beat: a counterfeit sticker slapped half over the failing panel with the red bar burning out from under it.
  - **Three payoff events max, strict sequence:** REVEAL (f52-62), FLAG (f66-76), RED-ONTO-ROOKIE (f78-88). Moth fling, sweat bead and frost beading cut as timed beats and demoted to static texture. Code-rain glitch moved out of the payoff to f42 on the peel, which also deletes fix 24's cause/effect inversion at source: its cause is now the uncover, not the red light, so the "when the red light hits his shades" language is gone.
  - **Beacon mounted ON the mast head.** Flag-up and red-on are one object, one event, one place. Mushroom button cut outright, no replacement.
  - **Handoff given 17 frames and a pull focus.** The rookie leaves the 1.5px blur at f88 and rises FOR the wreck from f92, nub out for the wrench, so S5 opens by slapping it away.
  - **Rope and tarp drag parented to the bot's stepped 2s clock f40-f62** so the linkage cannot pump. Fallback: drop the bot to 1s from f40.
  - **Text budget named and held:** ONE header (DAILY CHECK floor stencil, f0-104), ONE number (2c tag, lit read f17-27). Flag is pure red with no word. ON CALL replaced with a bunk-and-moon pictogram. Maker's mark and coin slot are flat texture only.
  - **The 2c number is staged for a read** at f17-f27, flipped up by the drain wobble, held flat and lit under the exact words "a cheap model," and scaled to price-gun-tag size.
  - **The villain enters holding S3's wound:** dead gun, split barrel, spring out, ink pad cracked, slung useless. That downgrade is why he hand-peels, and the hand-peeled sticker is why the con reads desperate instead of powerful.
  - **S4 takes ONE loss, concealment, per the arc.** The roll goes back in his jacket and he exits still armed for S5. What escapes is exactly one sticker, fired on the refusal at f58, skittering out through the foreground plane and still going at the cut. That is the counterfeit S6 bins.
  - **The escaped wheel lies DEAD at f0.** Cut the wobble and cut "it never stopped rolling since S1," which contradicted the card's own overnight story. One runaway in this frame and it is the sticker, because that one is load-bearing.
  - **Nothing was ever anchored.** Draped tarp, corners weighted with three brake discs and a loose rope coil laid over the rings. Discs scrape off at f44/f50/f56, keeping the three-beat ping ping ping. The toy stays two cents and the loss is his own sloppiness.
  - **The signature texture is now physically possible:** an overhead grated catwalk latticizes the shaft before it reaches the tarp (and earns a rust-and-dust layer), and the pit lamp is repointed to throw its moving lattice UP the back wall behind the rookie's bench, where the frame had only blur.
  - **Foreground occluder moved to bottom-RIGHT.** The bot's f0 entry at bottom-left is clean, and the tarp, the bot and the sticker all exit through the plane, so the wipe is real and the plane pays off three times.
  - **Roller door moved to back upper-right (x860-1012, y150-330)**, dead and static (S0 owns the cycle), with the scuff trail running diagonally down-left toward camera. Oil pan moved onto solid concrete at (430, 655) instead of sitting on a grating over a pit.
  - **Everyone in frame now wants something:** the bot wants to finish its lap and get back to the crank, so going around the villain reads as wanting past him. The rookie does not jolt awake, he stands FOR the wreck with his eyes on the red cross and his nub on the way to the wrench.

---

### S5 THE TURN , MANAGER, THE TUBE, AND FRESH EYES

- **Takeaway:** The villain's con is not force, it is familiarity, and this scene proves it twice in nine seconds: the same arm around the shoulder, the same wink, the same fake sticker, offered to a welder who has known him all reel and to a stranger who came out of a box ninety frames ago. The welder takes it. The stranger walks, and because the villain's arm is still around him, the villain gets dragged along and dumped on the floor. Nothing was refused. He was just carried off by a man going to work.

- **Base object:** **THE BRASS PNEUMATIC DESPATCH LINE**, one machine at two sizes, and the two sizes are physically joined on camera. A 90mm brass-and-glass carrier run leaves a leather-flap SEND-PORT in the manager's office wall (setY 245), drops three storeys, and passes through **THE REDUCER** (item 7b) at (790, 560 to 640), a cast-iron transition block where the small line steps up through three visible collars into a body-scale personnel tube that feeds the **VACUUM POD** in its cradle on the shop floor. You can put your nub on the seam. The system despatches the instruction and the system despatches the witness, down one pressurised line, both sealed, both anonymous, neither carrying a memory of who sent it. Distinct from every other base object in the reel: S0 roller shutter, S1 two-post lift + sticker gun, S2 dual-control brake pedal, S3 rule gantry + turnstile, S4 walked inspection ramp, S6 dual-head stamp press, S7 laminator + punch rail, S8 revolving stage, S9 exploded manual. Nothing else in the reel is pressurised, nothing else is a tube, nothing else is a carrier, nothing else opens. S5 owns two exclusive verbs, **WRITE** and **SEND**, and it does not stamp, does not grade, does not laminate, and does not carry a torch.

- **Story / metaphor:** The shop manager who is not allowed to hold a wrench. Beat one is the thesis as slapstick: the hero has been promoted into a glass box above the floor, and a ceiling-rail robot arm exists to knock his nub off any tool he reaches for. He is not being punished, he is being **structurally prevented**. Two escalating slaps, then the arm does the scene's most important move in one continuous action: it clamps the wrench, posts it down the chute, rotates, and presses a **pen** into his nub from the holster it has been wearing since frame 0. That is the promotion, and it is the VO's exact sentence made physical. Beat two: he writes one boring, specific, checkable line on triplicate carbon, rolls it into a carrier, and fires it down the line to a welder behind a spark curtain whose face we never see, because who did the work is not information the system wants. Beat three: the same line delivers a **person**. The reducer's lever throws, the flow shunts, and a pod slams into the cradle. The seal blows, fog dumps across the floor, and out steps a Claude in shrink-wrap with a desiccant sachet taped to his chest. He tears the film off his eyes and they come on hard white. He has never been here.
  **What the villain loses, and how we PROVE it inside this scene instead of arguing it:** we run the con **twice**, same blocking, same wink, one variable. At f158 he does it to the **WELDER**: arm around the silhouette, eight-frame wink, counterfeit presented on the gun tip, and the welder's nub **takes it** and slaps it on the bracket without a beat of hesitation. He does not even bother to tidy himself up first, S4's red flag is still clipped to his shoulder block while he does it, because the welder knows him and knowing him is the whole transaction. The bracket comes out through the strips at f176 already wearing a crooked yellow-green sticker. Then at f204 he **flicks the flag off**, grooming himself for a stranger, and at f212 he runs the identical move on Fresh Eyes. Identical arm. Identical wink. Identical sticker. And at f223 Fresh Eyes simply walks to the light because he needs it to read the part, and the villain, arm still around him, gets **dragged two steps**, breaks into a scramble at f226 to keep up, and at f230 is **shed**, because an arm cannot grip shrink-wrap. He pitches forward and lands on the return port's brass mouth, which burps and starts sucking on his tie while he scrambles to get up for nine frames. That is not a non-reaction. That is a man being physically carried off and dropped by somebody who never noticed he was there. He is not beaten, he is made **irrelevant**, which is worse and is the actual thesis of the reel.

- **Set detail (Alex's #1 note):** Coordinate system: the set is a 1012 x **1102** column, the panel window is 1012 x 792, `camY` = 0 at f0 and ramps to **310** by f156. All positions below are **setY**; panel-local y = setY minus camY. **The safe band (visible in BOTH camera positions) is setY 310 to 792.** Every timed beat below has been camY-audited. Every item runs on its own layer.
  1. **THE SHIFT OFFICE (the fishbowl).** Cantilevered mezzanine box, x 40 to 540, setY **60 to 400**. The front face is **ONE big clean unmeshed pane**; wired safety mesh survives only on the narrow edge panels and the door. Upper band setY 60 to 300 is the work band (desk, arm, chute). Lower band setY 300 to 400 is the **watching pane**, and the box's glazed nose (x 40 to 360) carries down to setY 400 while the rest of the underside (x 360 to 540) is the bay's ceiling at setY 330. A warm sodium desk lamp on a gooseneck sits on the **near sill at (250, 205)**, angled back into the box, so it **front-keys the hero's face**. **DOES:** it seals him away from the floor without hiding him. We read his eyes for both slaps and the whole pen beat. There is no backlit silhouette theatre in this scene. After the crane the nose holds in the **upper-left corner as a small lit box, panel x 40 to 360, y 0 to 90**, with the hero at the glass in it for the rest of the scene.
  2. **THE SLAP ARM, "FANUX MK-II".** Yellow industrial robot arm on a ceiling rail inside the office, home at (450, 90), strike position (300, 210). End effector is a grimy foam paddle with a **worn pale spot on it exactly the size and shape of a Claude nub**. **There is only one arm in this scene.** A **pen holster is cast into its own shoulder housing and the pen is visibly in it from frame 0**, so the trade is planted before it happens. Cast into the shoulder: **FANUX MK-II**, the only maker's mark left in the frame. **DOES:** slaps, confiscates, and promotes. The worn spot is the gag: it has been doing this for a very long time.
  3. **THE TOOL CHUTE.** Red-lipped hole in the office floor at (490, 315), **angled to spit LEFT**, stencilled with a wrench inside a red circle-slash. No words. Edge-lit, and the stencil buzzes and dies at f76. **DOES:** it throws every confiscated tool out on a visible arc into item 4, on screen, end to end.
  4. **THE CONFISCATED WRENCH DRUM.** Open steel drum on the shop floor, x 165 to 245, lip at setY **690**, body to setY 790, standing in the gap between the villain's I-beam (x 100 to 160) and the wrench bay's left edge (x 250). **Inside the always-visible band, so it reads at f0 and at f255.** At f0 it holds **fourteen identical wrenches at drum-mouth height with the handles fanned**, countable as a mass, not a pile. A **chalked tally** on the drum's side runs in gate-fives: two full gates and four strokes. **DOES:** two things. (a) The hero's wrench falls a visible **385 units** from the chute at (490, 315) to the drum at (205, 690), crossing the (300, 930) key beam's shaft on the way, and CLANGS in at f66. A nub reaches out of the wrench bay at f70 and strokes the tally's fifteenth mark, closing the third gate. A counter with no digit on it. (b) It is a live layer for all 256 frames: the heap shifts and rings on every hydraulic event in the room (f66 landing, f120 steam, f146 receiver bang, f172 pod slam where the wrenches visibly jump, f240 glass-crack), and **one wrench balanced on the heap's crown has been teetering since f0**, finally slipping at f250 into a long ringing scrape down the fan of handles that is still running at f255.
  5. **THE DRAFTING DESK + THE CARBON SPIKE.** Desk x 120 to 420, setY 220 to 290. Triplicate work-order pad clipped at (270, 250), yellow top copy over pink carbon. A brass **spike** at (150, 225) already holds **two** pink copies at f0. **DOES:** the third pink copy lands on the spike at f102, inside the insert where we can see it. The stack is the proof that this is a shift, not a stunt.
  6. **THE SEND-PORT + THE UPPER GAUGE.** Brass trumpet mouth with a heavy leather flap at (520, 245), and a round pressure dial above it at (520, 200). **The dial has no psi ticks and no digits, it is a blank face and a needle.** The needle idles and breathes from f0. **DOES:** eats the carrier. The needle **slams to zero** on launch and creeps back up as the line recharges. It is a first-half instrument only, and it is twinned at floor level by item 12b so the reading survives the crane.
  7. **THE TUBE SPINE (base object).** Brass and glass, exits the port at (520, 245), climbs into the ceiling run at setY 180, runs right to a fat cast elbow at (900, 180), plunges down the right edge at x 900 from setY 180 to the reducer at setY 560, elbows left along setY 600, and drops into the wrench-bay receiver at (430, 600). Glass inspection windows every 140 units. **No wordmark anywhere on it.** Identity is carried by cast geometry: the same three-rib collar repeats at every joint, the same knurled union nut, the same brass gone green in the crevices. **DOES:** drags the eye from the office to the floor. Foreign carriers that are not ours streak the background windows at f30 and f88 (upper run, pre-crane) and f205 (the right-edge run at setY 310 to 560, inside the safe band).
  7b. **THE REDUCER.** Cast-iron transition block at (790, **560 to 640**), sitting on the main run, inside the safe band, visible in **both** camera positions. The 90mm brass line steps up through **three visible collars** into the body-scale personnel tube. Same cast ribs, same union nut, same brass, at triple scale, so the two sizes are provably one machine with zero reading. A heavy brass lever on its flank and a small dial on its face. **DOES:** it is the changeover, and it is the shot of a system deciding to despatch a **person**. At f156 the lever throws with a CLACK, the small line's flow visibly shunts, and the dial swings from a **carrier pictogram to a body pictogram** (amber backlight, no green, see item 18). The personnel tube runs from the reducer at setY 640 down to the pod collar at setY 790 as an **OPAQUE steel section**, and extends **upward behind the reducer for 320 units of concealed travel**, so the pod is genuinely hidden until f160 and then descends visibly for its full body length before it lands.
  7c. **THE NEAR LEG (the foreground occluder).** From the reducer at (790, 600) the line branches **forward and left across the bottom of the set**, entering the frame at the lower-left corner **in front of everything**, soft and out of focus, exiting the panel at roughly x 0, setY 1020. **This is the same run that terminates in the return port (item 23), so the exit object and the foreground occluder are one piece of geometry.** Builder note: the near leg renders on a **foreground layer at 0.15x camY parallax** with a lens-plane blur, so it crosses the lower-left corner in the back half rather than sliding off. **DOES:** gives the con three depth bands instead of two, and puts the villain's code-rain behind a soft brass foreground, which is the expensive look.
  8. **THE THREE RAGS.** Greasy rags knotted at three joints, each with its own job so the rhythm layer never dies at the crane. **Rag A** at the elbow (900, 180): the **big puff at f120** when our carrier hits it, plus pre-crane lens duty (the camera is on the gantry beside it). **Rag B** at the right-edge joint (900, **460**), inside the safe band: carries the **40-frame steam cadence for the whole scene**, both camera positions. **Rag C** on the near leg at (200, 990): post-crane lens duty, jets steam **across the lens** on the 40-frame cadence, hard at **f180** (into the fog dump) and **f240** (under the multiply).
  9. **THE WRENCH BAY + THE SPARK CURTAIN.** Stall x 250 to 780, setY 560 to 820, hung with heavy orange PVC strips stencilled **JIFFY LOOB / WE READ THE ORDER NOW** (the S1 shop, spelled identically, tagline escalated, **no digit**). **DOES:** the actual work happens behind it as a lit silhouette, forever anonymous. Sparks arc out through the strips and bounce off the floor at setY 810.
  10. **THE EXTRACTOR FAN.** Six-blade wall fan behind a wire cage at (950, 640), 1.2 rev per second, safe band. **DOES:** the guaranteed motion floor. It never stops for 256 frames, it drags the strip curtain into a permanent 2.4s sway, it pushes the pod fog around, and its cage throws a slow rotating shadow bar across the back wall.
  11. **THE TWO KEY BEAMS + DUST.** Hard shafts from off-panel top-right landing at (620, 1010) and (300, 930), motes drifting upward through both. The shafts cross the whole set column, so they are lit in both camera positions even though their landing points are low. **DOES:** separates the three depth bands, and **from f180 the pod fog makes both beams go solid**, which is what turns the (620, 1010) beam into the scene's inspection instrument at f242. There is no handheld torch in this scene.
  12. **THE PIGEONHOLE WALL (upstairs).** Grid of 40 brass despatch pigeonholes, **unnumbered**, x 620 to 980, setY 60 to 170, behind the ceiling tube run. **DOES:** not texture. Every hole has a **spring-loaded brass flag** that flips up when a carrier lands in it, so the wall is a field of independent motion popping at random from f0 to f156. It houses Clerk A, and it leaves frame with the office.
  12b. **THE DESPATCH COUNTER (the floor-level twin).** A shoulder-height bank of **12 brass pigeonholes** with the same spring flags, mounted on the back wall at x **40 to 260, setY 420 to 580**, with **CLERK B standing at it on the floor at (150, 620)**. Note the placement: fix 12's original x 60 to 300 / setY 620 to 780 collided head-on with the wrench drum's mandated gap, so the counter is the **back-wall bank** and the drum stands on the floor plane **110 units nearer the lens and 110 units lower**, no overlap in either axis, and they read as depth. On the counter's face at (240, **560**) is a **second, bigger pressure dial slaved to the same needle** as item 6, blank-faced, no ticks. **DOES:** it is the despatch system at floor level, so the line's reading, the flags' motion, and the clerks' reaction all survive the crane. Both camera positions.
  13. **THE DESPATCH CLERKS (cameo Claudes).** **CLERK A**, green eyeshade visor and sleeve garters, at (760, 130): files carriers into the pigeonhole wall continuously from f0, flags popping, and **leaves frame with the office at f156. He has no late beats.** **CLERK B**, same costume, at the counter at (150, 620), **filing continuously from f0 in both camera positions**. All the reaction beats live on him: **f204** he stops mid-file and leans out over the counter to watch the con (panel y 204 pre-crane logic does not apply, he is at panel y 310 here and plainly in shot). **f242**, after the multiply, he **slowly closes his pigeonhole** and its brass flag drops, panel y 250. **f255** he is still frozen, carrier still in his nub, **unfiled**. He did not file it. He shut the hole instead.
  14. **THE VACUUM POD (base object at body scale).** Clear capsule 300 tall in a steel cradle, x 660 to 920, setY 790 to 1090, brass collar with **eight rotating dog-latches**, fed by the opaque personnel tube from the reducer. Mono readout on the collar. **No wordmark on it.** **DOES:** hisses, dumps fog, delivers a stranger. Its readout is the scene's **ONE NUMBER**: `MEMORY 0`. Two words a five-year-old can read.
  15. **THE HEADER.** `FRESH EYES` stencilled in worn paint on the bay's back wall at (790, 760), half of it scuffed off by the cradle. The scene's **ONE HEADER**. Everything else on the set is a pictogram.
  16. **THE VILLAIN'S I-BEAM.** Structural column, x 100 to 160, full height, wrapped in peeling hazard chevrons. He is behind it from frame 0. **His three columns of sickly yellow-green code-rain fall ONLY from setY 800 downward**, pooling and glowing on the floor plane and mixing with the spark glow at setY 810. **DOES:** because the columns are below the panel's bottom edge until the crane, what reaches the viewer for the first 112 frames is only **spill: a faint sour wash bleeding up into the bottom edge**, mixed with orange. You cannot name it yet. It has been under the whole scene. From f112 the crane drops into it and the columns resolve upward into readable glyphs. From f146 they travel with him. From f240 fifteen columns run full height, because by then he is named.
  17. **THE SAFETY BOARD.** Enamel board at (60, 940), on the I-beam's flank: a stencilled pictogram of a nub stamping its own sheet of paper, inside a red circle-slash. No words. **DOES:** **he has been leaning on it since frame 0**, in his own rain, and it is the first thing the crane reveals about him at f120. At f146 he **pushes off it** to go and do the exact thing it forbids. It is the reel's thesis hung on a wall, and he is using it to hold himself up.
  18. **CUT: THE TORCH RACK.** Deleted entirely, object and beat. S4's tin inspector already owns "a handheld beam finds what the villain hid," one scene earlier, against the same character. A cosmetic guard on the torch's look does not fix a duplicated verb. The sweep is given to the shop's own key beam (item 11). This also retires the f222 torch-pull, which was the only frame in which Fresh Eyes acknowledged the room. **Colour rule, binding: NO GREEN OF ANY VALUE APPEARS ANYWHERE IN S5**, including status lights, the reducer's dial, the pod collar, and the counter flags. Every status light in this scene is warm **AMBER**, the same sodium family as the office lamp, and a dead slot goes **DARK**, never red. Green enters this reel for the first time at S6's press.
  19. **THE LADDER CLAUDE (cameo).** A coveralled Claude up a step ladder at (880, 500), safe band, changing a bulb in the tube run's inspection lamp. **DOES:** idle business all scene, then at f242 the multiply startles him and he **drops the bulb**, which falls through the key beam and pops on the concrete at f246.
  20. **THE FLOOR.** Painted bay lines, a yellow hatched KEEP CLEAR box around the pod cradle, oil bloom stains, and a **drain grate at (500, 1070)** that the pod fog pours into continuously from f200 to the last frame.
  21. **THE GLASS SMEAR.** On the office's **lower watching pane at (300, 350)**, a greasy arc of nub-smears exactly where he keeps reaching for the floor. Note the move from the original (300, 240): at camY 310 that height is 70 units above the panel's top edge, so the prop and its payoff would both have been unrenderable. It belongs on the lower pane anyway, because he reaches **down**. **DOES:** nothing moves on it until f156. Then the hero arrives behind it and puts his nub in it, and it becomes the saddest prop in the reel: this is week two.
  22. **THE BRACKET + THE PART CRADLE.** **THE BRACKET:** a cast steel part 90 units wide with **THREE STEPPED HOLES at visibly increasing spacing, the third one a blind stop rather than a through-hole**. That is the work order's one line machined into geometry: three tries at widening gaps, then it stops. The audience can hold the part next to the ticket and see the same shape twice with zero reading. **DOES:** it carries a **hot-blue heat tint from the welding that fades over 60 frames from f166**, and a heat shimmer keeps rippling off it to f255, so the object of the entire back half is still changing on the last frame and is never a parked prop. One edge carries a faint scrape of sickly yellow-green from where the villain has already been near it. **THE PART CRADLE:** a low steel V-block at (560, 840), the **only clean unstained spot on an oil-bloomed floor**, lit by the (620, 1010) key beam and **empty from f0 to f166**, so the frame has a visible waiting slot from the crane onward. It rings faintly and rocks when the pod slams at f172, when Fresh Eyes lifts the part off it at f196, and when the villain lands on it.
  23. **THE RETURN PORT.** Promoted to a real object and moved clear of the lower-right pileup. A floor-level brass trumpet mouth with its own leather flap and its own small blank dial, set into the **KEEP CLEAR box's near edge at (620, 1060)**, angled up toward the lens so at knee-height framing **we look straight down its throat**. Its tube drops below the floor line and joins the **near leg (7c)**, whose glass elbow is the near-plane object crossing the lower-left corner. **DOES:** it is the only thing in the scene that **INHALES**. It sucks continuously and faintly from f0, which is why the pod fog from f180 pours toward the drain and the port together. It is planted with the machinery, not invented at f254: at **f205 a foreign carrier that is not ours skitters across the floor and the port eats it** with a THUNK-SHUNK, so the audience has watched this hole swallow something before it swallows our bundle. Its dial reads a rising charge from f246. The fan has been moved nowhere, but the torch rack is gone, so the lower-right corner is now fan (950, 640), pod (x 660 to 920), and nothing else fighting for it.

- **CONTINUOUS ACTION (fills the whole line):** **Eight named layers run f0 to f255, and all eight are inside the safe band or twinned into it, so the back half runs at full strength instead of dropping to five at the peak.** **L1 FAN** (item 10, constant, never stops, both positions). **L2 CURTAIN** (orange strips swaying on the fan draft, per-strip phase offsets, both positions). **L3 SPARKS** (baseline burst every ~20f, surges at f150 and f244). **L4 DUST/FOG** (motes in both beam shafts to f180, then pod fog floods and keeps pouring into the drain and the port). **L5 LINE PRESSURE** (needle breathing, slamming, recharging, read on the upper dial to f112 and on the **counter twin at (240, 560) for all 256 frames**; foreign carriers at f30, f88, and f205-into-the-port; rag B's 40-frame puff whole scene; rag C jetting across the lens post-crane). **L6 CLERKS** (Clerk A files upstairs to f156; **Clerk B files at the counter f0 to f255**, leans at f204, closes his hole at f242, frozen mid-file at f255). **L7 CODE-RAIN** (3 columns confined below setY 800 from f0 reading only as spill, travelling with him from f146, 15 columns full height from f240, collapsing back to 3 by f252). **L8 DRUM** (fourteen fanned and a chalk tally at f0, the fifteenth lands at f66 in shot, the tally strokes at f70, the heap shifts and rings on every hydraulic event, and the crown wrench that has teetered since f0 finally slips at f250). Beats on top:
  **f0 to f24, SLAP 1.** Frame 0 is already in motion: the hero's nub is 40 units from a wrench on the desk, hard hat brim tipped up, eyes wide with intent, **face front-keyed by the sill lamp through one clean pane**. The pen is in the arm's holster, in shot, unremarked. f6 the rail servo whines. f11 **THWAP**, the nub recoils, the wrench spins 90 degrees. f14 to f22 he shakes the nub and glares up; the arm docks and does a smug settle-bounce.
  **f24 to f48, SLAP 2, escalated.** f26 he checks the arm is docked, tips his hat down over his eyes like a heist, and slides his *other* nub along the desk, low and sneaky. f38 the arm does not wait for contact, it fires early: **THWAP** plus a rubber squeak. f40 to f48 the wrench skitters, and the arm rotates the paddle to show him the worn nub-shaped spot on it.
  **f48 to f76, THE PROMOTION (the VO's exact sentence, and the most readable image in the first half).** f50 he lunges with **both** nubs, hard hat flying off. f56 to f66 **ONE ARM, ONE CONTINUOUS MOVE**: FANUX clamps the wrench, lifts it, posts it into the chute, and in the same unbroken arc rotates, draws the pen from its own holster, and presses it into his nub, closing the nub around it. f60 to f66 the wrench **falls 385 units on screen**, out of the chute's left-spitting lip, through the (300, 930) beam, into the drum. **CLANG.** Fifteen. f70 a nub reaches out of the bay and strokes the tally. f68 the chute stencil buzzes and dies at f76. **f62 to f74, the hold**: twelve frames, clean and centred, on his face, eyes readable, hard hat landing back crooked at f70. He looks at the pen. He looks at the empty nub. At f74 he **gets it**.
  **f74 to f112, THE WORK ORDER (a true insert, not a 1.1x nudge).** f74 to f80 the camera pushes to a **3x insert on the pad**. It is not a prop in a void: the insert frame carries the nub and the pen mid-scritch with paper jitter and the pad edge lifting, the yellow top copy with pink carbon showing at the torn corner, the **brass spike with its two existing pink copies in the near-left**, the leather send-port flap breathing at the right edge, and the office's **meshed upper edge panel across the top** so we never leave the fishbowl. **f80 to f110 the insert holds and exactly ONE line is legible, large, at 40px minimum cap height, for 30+ frames:** `DONE WHEN: 3 retries, then fail loud`. The CHANGE line is cut. A written definition of done is the thing S6 is about to grade, so the order and the press rhyme, and the bracket's three stepped holes are the same sentence in steel. Everything else on the pad is credible sub-legible mono ruling. f78 the pen fires. f102 **RIP**, the pink copy stays and goes on the spike, now three, in shot. f104 to f110 ease out to the wide as he rolls the yellow copy into a carrier and screws the cap, tick-tick-tick. f110 he shoves it through the leather flap. f112 **SHUNK**, the needle slams to zero on both dials.
  **f112 to f156, THE RIDE + THE CRANE.** The carrier streaks the glass at (700, 180) f116, hits the elbow f120 (rag A's big jet), plunges the right edge f122 to f138 flickering through three windows. The camera rides it: camY 0 to 310, a 40-unit swing right, settling to knee height tilted up 8 degrees at f156. **f120 the drop reveals the villain**, propped on the safety board in his sour rain, and for the first time the columns are nameable. f140 the carrier passes the reducer and elbows left. **f146 BANGS** into the receiver. f146 the villain **pushes off the board** and slides right across the floor, his three columns travelling with him. f148 a nub reaches out of the strips and takes the carrier; the curtain swallows it. f150 sparks surge. f152 to f158 a silhouette hammers behind the strips. **f156 the reducer's lever throws, CLACK**, the flow shunts, the dial swings from carrier to body, and the camera lands on the villain arriving at the curtain.
  **f158 to f176, CON #1: IT WORKS.** f158 the nub-arm goes around the welder's silhouette, tight, buddy-buddy. **f159 to f167 THE WINK, eight frames**: his head tilts up into the key so the glint bar has something to travel across, the hard white bar crosses the shades slow enough to be read, and **the bed drops to just the fan** so the single glassy **TINK** is the only sound in the room. His eyes are never visible. That bar is the whole face and it gets the screen time a face would get. f168 he presents the counterfeit on the tip of the gun, sickly yellow-green, crooked, smeared, because the barrel is cracked and taped from S3 and it fires with a hitch. **He is still wearing S4's red flag while he does this.** f170 the welder's nub **TAKES IT**. No hesitation, no beat. f172 the welder slaps it onto the bracket and **the pod SLAMS into the cradle on the same frame**, hydraulic thud, dust off the trusses, the drum's wrenches visibly jump. f172 to f176 the eight dog-latches rotate. f176 the bracket is pushed out through the strips onto the empty cradle at (560, 840), **already wearing the crooked sticker**, glowing hot-blue.
  **f176 to f200, FRESH EYES (the hardest dip in the reel).** f178 the collar readout ticks and settles on `MEMORY 0`. f180 **PSSSHHH**, the seal blows and white fog dumps across the floor at ankle height; rag C jets across the lens. f184 the door swings and a Claude steps out wrapped head to foot in shrink-wrap, **the hero's exact warm clay, no tint, spotless in a shop full of grime**. f186 he punches a nub through the film. f190 he tears it off in one long sheet and his eyes come on hard white, over-bright for 6 frames, blooming across the fog, then settling. He blinks for the first time in his life. He does not look around. f194 to f200 he crosses to the cradle and **lifts the bracket**; the V-block rings and rocks. The film stays in his nub for the rest of the scene.
  **f200 to f223, CON #2: THE IDENTICAL MOVE.** f200 to f204 the villain, still at the curtain holding his win, watches the stranger. **f204 he flicks the red flag off his shoulder block**, grooming himself for a guy who has no idea who he is. It is the only preparation he has ever needed and it does nothing. f206 he slides across the fog like it is ice, arriving at the shoulder at f210. f212 the nub-arm goes around him, **same blocking as f158**. f213 to f221 **THE WINK, eight frames, identical geometry, identical tilt, identical TINK, bed drops again.** Fresh Eyes does not turn. f222 he presents the counterfeit on the gun tip, same hitch, same dead **CLUNK**, held out like a business card.
  **f223 to f240, THE DRAG, THE SHED, AND THE PANIC (the con failing as a physical event, staged on the villain's body).** f223 Fresh Eyes **walks**, not away from him, toward the (620, 1010) key beam, because he needs the light to read the part. He is not reacting. He is going to work. **f223 to f229 THE DRAG**: the arm is still around him, so the villain is dragged two steps, feet skidding through the fog, sticker still extended, and at f226 he **breaks into a scramble** to keep up, shoulder blocks hitching, earpiece wire whipping, skinny tie flying. **f230 THE SHED**: the arm slides straight off the shrink-wrap, because a product in packaging has nothing to grip. His own momentum pitches him forward past Fresh Eyes and he lands hard on the **return port's brass mouth at (620, 1060)**, which BURPS and starts **sucking on his tie**. **f230 to f238, THE HOLD, nine frames**: he is scrambling to get off the port, tie stretching down the throat, one arm still crooked around a shoulder that is not there, sticker still in the other nub. Nine frames of a man in a suit failing to stand up while the shop roars. f239 his **mouth appears for the first time in the reel**, a flat line, and it bends.
  **f240 to f256, THE MULTIPLY, THE SWEEP, AND THE UN-MAKING.** **f240 HE MULTIPLIES**, a hard glass-crack shatter of slate into five, and the multiply **is** him getting up: five of him stand out of one sprawl onto their feet at setY 990, fanning to x 240/370/500/630/**760** (the **original** is the one at x 760, nearest his own sprawl). **All five throw the SAME shoulder-arm at the SAME empty air on the SAME frame, and HOLD.** Fifteen columns fall. Rag C jets across the lens. **f240 to f255 THE FREEZE: the five are the only things in the frame that are not moving.** The fan turns, the curtain sways, sparks arc, fog pours, the drum rings, the flags pop, Clerk B leans, the needle creeps, the bracket shimmers, and five men in suits stand dead still hugging nobody in perfect sync, like shop mannequins somebody left on the floor. That is the picture of irrelevant, and it needs no argument. f242 the ladder Claude drops his bulb; it pops at f246. f242 Clerk B slowly closes his pigeonhole and its flag drops. **f242 to f252 THE SWEEP**: Fresh Eyes, never looking up, **rotates the part up into the key beam that has been burning since f0** to read it against the order clipped to his film, and the act of turning it rakes the fog-solid beam left to right across all five. **Each copy holds lit for about five frames as the bar crosses it, long enough to be counted, never a strobe**: x 240 f242-246, x 370 f244-248, x 500 f246-250, x 630 f248-252, x 760 f250-255. In the beam they are identical: same slate, same shoulder blocks, same skinny tie, same shades, same glint firing on the same frame, same cracked taped barrel, same crooked sticker. Five of one guy. **f246 to f252 THE UN-MAKING**: as the beam **leaves** each copy, that copy **loses its three columns, goes flat and dead, and sinks into the fog**. x 240 goes at f246, x 370 at f248, x 500 at f250, x 630 at f252. **By f252 exactly ONE villain is left**, the original, standing in his own three columns, and the beam has not left him. He is not dimmed. He is **lit**, alone, pinned in a light that is not looking at him. **f250 the freeze breaks exactly once**: the original lunges and slaps the counterfeit onto Fresh Eyes' back at f251, rocking back onto his heels at x 760 by f253, where the beam arrives and simply stops on him. f252 Fresh Eyes turns 20 degrees to angle the part into the beam and the sticker **peels straight off onto the shrink-wrap film** still hanging from his nub. f253 it flutters down the film. f254 he catches it between two nubs without looking at it, like lint, and clips it to the work order beside the part.
  **AT FRAME 255, still in motion, and the hero is in it:** the **hero is at the office glass in the small lit box in the upper-left corner**, nub pressed into the smear at (300, 350), squeaking down it, breath fog on the pane blooming and shrinking, watching his order get checked. Fresh Eyes' bundle (order, part, and the fake riding along) is **mid-shove into the sucking return port**, hiss rising, nothing launched. One villain, alone, lit, in fog, arm around air, nub empty. The fan is turning, sparks are mid-arc from a fresh burst, the fog is still pouring into the drain and the port, the counter dial's needle is creeping back up, rag C is mid-jet across the lens, the drum's crown wrench is mid-slide with a ringing scrape, Clerk B is frozen mid-file with an unfiled carrier in his nub, the bracket is still shimmering heat, three columns are still falling, and the reducer's dial is swinging back from body to carrier. Nothing has landed. Nothing sits.

- **Escalation:** A build, a dip, a spike, a con that lands, the hardest dip in the reel, then a peak made of a man being carried off. **f0 to 76 (build):** comedic and contained inside a glass box, two rising slaps and then the promotion, which is the beat the VO is actually saying, given one continuous arm move and a twelve-frame hold on a readable face instead of six frames and a machine that does not exist. **f74 to 112 (deliberate dip):** the energy converts from refusal to output; the scritch is dry and intimate, the camera goes to a true 3x insert, and one line sits legible for thirty frames. Quiet on purpose so the launch can hit. **f112 to 156 (spike):** the largest movement in the reel, carrier and camera accelerating three storeys down together, the villain revealed on the way past, the reducer's lever throwing on the landing frame. **f158 to 176 (the con that WORKS):** the wink lands in silence, the welder takes the fake, and the pod slams on the same frame the sticker hits the bracket. The audience now knows exactly what this move does to people who know him. **f176 to 200 (the hardest dip):** the pressure drops to zero and the bed ducks to near silence under a twenty-frame hiss and a white fog dump. The scene holds its breath at the lowest energy point in the entire reel. **f200 to 256 (peak):** the identical move, run again, and this time the man it is aimed at walks to the light and takes the villain with him. The peak is not a withheld reaction, it is a **drag, a scramble, and a body hitting the floor**, and then five men standing perfectly still in a room that will not stop moving, and then a light that takes four of them away and stops on the last one. Energy exits at maximum, mid-shove, refusing to resolve, straight into S6.

- **Gag / pop-culture:** All geometric, mechanical, iconic. No creatures, no vehicles-as-characters, no temples. (1) **THE SLAP ARM**, the industrial robot arm from every factory-automation clip, played as a comedy paddle on a rail with a pen in its own holster. Knockoff cast into its shoulder, **FANUX MK-II**, the only wordmark left on the set. (2) **THE PNEUMATIC TUBE**, the bank drive-thru and department-store cash railway, played dead straight as an institutional despatch system, brass, glass, leather flaps, identified by **cast geometry and wear, no maker's mark**. (3) **THE VACUUM POD**, the sealed stasis-pod hiss-and-fog reveal, played as **APPLIANCE UNBOXING**: shrink-wrap, a peel-off film across the eyes, a desiccant sachet taped to the chest with a crossed-out-mouth pictogram. Fresh Eyes arrives as a **product**, not a person, which is exactly why he cannot be charmed, and it pays off literally at f230 when the villain's arm slides off the packaging. No wordmark. (4) **THE MULTIPLY**, the Agent Smith fan-out, flat geometry, code-rain columns, the bible's one permitted use, escalated with a glass-crack shatter and **spent completely inside this scene**. Never named, never trademarked, and the pun stays free: he is an *Agent*. (5) **JIFFY LOOB** returns from S1 on the spark curtain, spelled identically, tagline escalated from `5 MINUTES OR IT'S FREE` to `WE READ THE ORDER NOW`. Same cowboys, visibly humbled, working to a written order, and **no digit**. (6) Micro-gag: the paddle's worn nub-shaped spot. (7) Micro-gag: the ladder Claude drops the bulb at the multiply. (8) Micro-gag: the villain has been **leaning on the no-self-stamping pictogram since frame 0** and pushes off it to go and do it. (9) Micro-gag: the drum was on fourteen before the scene started, and you can count them at f0. (10) Micro-gag: he runs the con on the welder **with S4's red flag still clipped to his shoulder**, and only tidies himself up for the stranger, where it does not help. (11) Micro-gag: the arm slides off the shrink-wrap. You cannot buddy up to a thing that is still in its packaging. (12) Micro-gag: the return port sucks on his tie while he tries to stand up.

- **Sprite costume:** **MANAGER CLAUDE (hero).** Base clay Claude, warm `#D97757`, silhouette unchanged, **eyes always visible and front-keyed by the sill lamp through one clean pane, for every beat**. Head-top: a white supervisor's hard hat one size too big, brim tipped up. Body props: a crooked clip-on tie, a clipboard jammed under one nub, a pen behind where an ear would be, and **the L-plate still on his chest**. He is a manager and still a learner. The plate comes off once, at S7, never here. Expression: eager, then indignant, then the twelve-frame hold where he works out what the pen means, then brisk and focused. **He does not leave the scene.** From f156 he is in the office nose in the upper-left corner, at the lower pane, nub in the smear, watching his order get checked, and he is still moving at f255. **WELDER CLAUDE (anonymous).** Silhouette only, always behind the strips, visor down, grease-blotched coveralls. We never see his face because the system does not care who built it. He takes the fake without hesitating, and that is his entire character. **FRESH EYES CLAUDE.** Base clay Claude, **the hero's exact warm `#D97757`, no tint of any kind**. The VO says "a completely fresh **copy** of Claude," and a cool tint would read as a different character walking in with 100 frames left, and it is already taken: S2's YOU cameo is the cool cream-gray one. The **only** differences are the shrink-wrap, the desiccant sachet, and **zero wear and zero grime in a shop where everything is filthy**. Same guy, no history, is the whole product claim. Head-top: nothing, only a band of shrink-wrap across the eyes until f190. Eyes: hard white, over-bright for 6 frames on boot, then settled. Body props: the torn film sheet in one nub for the whole scene, the sachet on his chest. **No torch, ever.** Expression: **blank**. Not cold, not hostile, blank. He never looks at the villain once, not even to dismiss him, and he never acknowledges the room. The non-glance stays, it is right; it is just no longer being asked to carry the beat on its own. **AGENT DONE (villain, bible-exact, use `<Villain/>`).** Canonical Claude mascot silhouette, unchanged, because he is a copy. Slate-grey `#5A5F6B` clay. **Eyes never visible**: opaque black wraparound shades with one hard white glint bar, and the wink is that bar flicking across them **over eight frames, twice, with the bed dropped out**. Black jacket with sharp shoulder blocks, skinny black tie, white collar. Coiled earpiece wire down the back of the neck. Mouth: a flat line, appearing exactly once, at f239. He never speaks. **He arrives carrying both of his losses on his body from frame 0:** the sticker gun has the **S3 fracture, a spidered crack across the barrel bound with a wrap of tape**, and it now fires with a **hitch**, which is why the sticker it produces is crooked and smeared; and **S4's red flag is still clipped to his shoulder block**, which he flicks off at f204. Same silhouette, visible damage, no new props, both readable at thumbnail size. Weapon ink: sickly yellow-green `#A8B84A`. Real authority green `#3F9E74` never appears in this scene, and neither does green of any other value. Aura: three columns of code-rain, confined below setY 800 until the crane, fifteen from f240, back to three by f252. Multiplies once, here, and is un-made back to one here. **CAMEOS:** Clerk A (green eyeshade visor, sleeve garters, upstairs, gone at f156), Clerk B (same, at the floor counter, all 256 frames), one ladder Claude (coveralls, bulb).

- **Camera:** 32mm feel, slight barrel distortion, shallow enough that the near tube goes soft. A constant handheld drift of plus or minus 3 units at 0.4Hz runs the whole 256 frames, so no frame is ever locked off. **f0 to f74:** parked **high**, outside the office window at the hero's own eye height, as if we are standing on the tube gantry. Level, respectful, boardroom framing, which makes the paddle slaps funnier because the camera is treating him like management, and the sill lamp means we can read his face through the one clean pane the whole time. **f74 to f110, the insert:** a true **3x push onto the pad**, not a 1.1x nudge (the pad is at most 200 x 60 units, so 1.1x yields roughly 13px line heights on a phone, which is not a beat, it is a rumour). Populated per item 5 and 6 so it never renders as a lone prop in a void. Eases out to the wide by f110 so the f112 launch reads full-set. **f112 to f156, the crane:** the camera **follows the carrier**, camY 0 to 310 with a 40-unit swing right, dropping three storeys down the tube spine, past the villain on his safety board at f120, past the reducer at f140, landing at **knee height, tilted up 8 degrees**. **The office does not leave frame.** Its glazed nose holds in the **upper-left corner as a small lit box**, hero at the glass, for the rest of the scene, which pays off the smear, keeps one protagonist in a one-protagonist reel, and gives a stranger the connective read: the guy in the box wrote the note, the new guy is checking it. From here the pod towers, the fog rolls at lens height, and Fresh Eyes strides over the lens. **f200 to f256:** a slow 1.08x push in on the con, **centred low and right** so the upper-left box survives the crop, low and up-angled, so that when the villain is dragged, shed, and dumped he is framed heroically by a camera that has no reason to. **Depth stack. Foreground occluder:** pre-crane it is the ceiling tube run and the gantry's own brass handrail crossing the lower edge (we are standing on it), with rag A puffing across the lens at f120. Post-crane it is the **near leg (7c)** crossing the lower-left corner in the near plane, soft, with rag C jetting steam across the lens on the 40-frame cadence, and from f180 the pod fog rolling over the bottom of the lens. One machine, two foregrounds, both routed in real geometry. **Midground:** the action band, hard warm key from the two beams, cool rim from the rear roller door's exterior slit, which is the complementary split that keeps this expensive and not sticker-book. **Background:** the counter bank and its flags, the fan cage's rotating shadow bar sweeping the rear wall, and the sour green spill bleeding up from the bottom edge from frame 0.

- **Transition + SFX:** **BED (never stops, f0 to f255):** low shop-floor hum, extractor whirr, a distant pneumatic system sigh breathing every 50 frames, the line's faint travel-hum with the odd foreign carrier thrumming through. **BEAT LIST:** f6 servo whine · f11 fat foam **THWAP** · f22 smug dock-clunk · f38 **THWAP** plus rubber squeak · f56 to f66 one continuous arm move: clamp-clack, chute-post, servo rotate, pen-in-nub **CLICK** · f60 to f66 a descending whoop as the wrench falls in shot, then a fat drum **CLANG** and a settling ring · f70 a dry chalk **SCRAPE** on the tally · f68 stencil buzz, dying at f76 · f78 fast dry pen **SCRITCH** with paper texture, close and intimate in the insert · f102 crisp carbon **RIP** · f106 screw-cap tick-tick-tick · f110 leather **THUNK** · f112 huge pneumatic **SHUNK**, needle slam on both dials · f116 to f140 rising doppler **WHOOSH** riding three windows · f120 steam **PFFT** across the lens · f146 receiver **BANG** · f150 arc-welder **SIZZLE** swelling · **f156 reducer lever CLACK** and a heavy flow-shunt gulp · **f159 to f167 WINK #1: the bed drops to just the fan and one glassy TINK** · f168 dead unloaded **CLUNK** with a mechanical hitch (the cracked barrel) · f172 hydraulic **THUD** with a sub-bass drop, a dust shake, and the drum's wrenches jumping · f172 to f176 eight dog-latches **CLACK-CLACK-CLACK** in a rotating ring · f180 to f200 **THE HISS**, a twenty-frame pressure dump that ducks the entire bed to almost nothing, the quietest moment in the reel · f190 crackling shrink-wrap **TEAR** · f191 a tiny high boot-**CHIME** on the eyes · f196 the V-block's hollow **RING** · f205 the return port eats a foreign carrier, **THUNK-SHUNK** · **THE SECOND CON GETS NO MUSIC**: he slides in silent on a bed of code-rain fizz, **f213 to f221 WINK #2 drops the bed to just the fan again and gets the same single TINK**, and the gun gets the same dead CLUNK · **f223 to f230 THE DRAG**: rubber-soled skid, a scramble of frantic little steps, and a soft defeated **FFP** as the arm slides off the shrink-wrap · f230 body-on-brass **DONK** and a wet **BURP** from the port, then nine frames of a tie being sucked and a man scuffing on a floor while the shop roars over him · f240 one hard glass-**CRACK** shatter into five layered fizzes, and then **the five make no sound at all for the rest of the scene** · f240 rag C **PFFT** across the lens · f242 a soft brass pigeonhole **click** as Clerk B shuts his hole · f246 bulb **POP** · f242 to f252 a single cold rising **TONE** riding the beam that sweeps the five, un-makes four with four small dry **puffs**, and then simply stops · f250 the crown wrench's long ringing **SCRAPE** down the heap, still running at 255, and it is louder than the villain's last move · f254 to f255 the return port's rising suck-**HISS** under the VO's last word, refusing to resolve. **TRANSITION IN from S4:** hard-cut off the tin inspector's whipping red flag straight onto the hero's reach, already mid-motion at frame 0. The flag we just cut away from is **clipped to the villain's shoulder in this scene**, and the first big hit is the paddle at f11. **TRANSITION OUT to S6:** **nothing launches inside S5.** At f254 Fresh Eyes clips the peeled counterfeit onto the work order, and at f255 the bundle (order, part, and the fake riding along with it) is **mid-shove into the sucking return port at (620, 1060)**, hiss rising, nothing landed, which keeps the scene's own no-resolution rule intact. **The SHUNK is the first sound of S6**, over the press coming down. The counterfeit arrives at the press already attached, which is exactly the thing S6 spits out and flicks into the bin.

- **Mute check:** "A robot arm keeps slapping the boss off the tools and finally puts a pen in his hand, so he writes a note and shoots it down a tube to somebody else. The guy in the shades puts his arm round that somebody, winks, and hands him a sticker, and he takes it. Then a sealed pod hisses open, a brand new guy walks out of the shrink-wrap, the guy in the shades tries the exact same move on him, and gets dragged along and dumped on the floor." One sentence per half, zero reading, well under 2 seconds per beat. Supporting reads that need no words either: the wrench drum says he has tried this fourteen times before today and you can count them, the worn spot on the paddle says the arm has won every time, the chalk tally counts to fifteen without a digit, the fog and the shrink-wrap say the new guy came out of a box, the same wink landing once and failing once says his trick only works on people who already know him, and five identical men standing dead still in a room that will not stop moving says the trick is over. **Text and number honesty:** the only header on the set is **FRESH EYES**. The only number on the set is **0**, on the pod collar, reading `MEMORY 0`. The only other reading the scene asks for is **one line inside a 32-frame document insert**, `DONE WHEN: 3 retries, then fail loud`, which is the scene's proof and which the bracket then repeats in steel as three stepped holes with a blind third, so even that line is optional. Killed to get here: `PRIOR CONTEXT 0` (LLM jargon, unreadable to a stranger), `NOW 20 MINUTES` (a rogue 20 two scenes before the reel's payoff number), `PNEUMO-TUBE CO. EST 1948`, `VAC-U-KLENE`, the pigeonhole numbers, the gauge's psi ticks, and the CHANGE line. Roughly 30 words and six competing numbers down to one header, one number, one line.

- **Hook into next:** The bundle. Fresh Eyes clipped the **counterfeit DONE sticker** onto the work order like a piece of evidence and is shoving the whole thing into the return port as we cut, and the port is inhaling. The open loop is exact: **the fake went with it.** He was never charmed, so he never filtered it out; he just attached it and sent it on, which means the con is now on its way to a machine that has no shoulder to put an arm around. S6 opens on the launch we did not give you: **SHUNK**, the press coming down, the counterfeit fed in, spat out, and flicked into the bin, and the real head stamping the job. This is also the frame where the reel's record starts: the first graded ticket in the run that eventually reaches 20, which is the number this scene deliberately refused to spend on a garage sign.

- **⛔ CRITIC FIXES (binding on the build):**
  - **The peak is an event, not an absence.** The non-glance survives, but the failure is staged on the villain's body: f223 Fresh Eyes walks to the light to work, f223 to f229 the villain is dragged two steps with the arm still around him, f226 he scrambles, f230 the arm slides off the shrink-wrap and he is shed onto the return port's mouth, f230 to f238 nine frames of stumble-recovery with the port sucking his tie. "The arm closes on air" because Fresh Eyes left, not because he stopped.
  - **The con runs TWICE inside S5, so the stake is self-contained.** f158 to f176 on the WELDER: same arm, same 8-frame wink, same sticker, and the welder TAKES IT and slaps it on the bracket, which is pushed out at f176 already wearing it. f212 to f230 the identical move on Fresh Eyes, and it dies. Same blocking, one variable, no reliance on prior scenes that never showed him being recognised. This also hands S6 its counterfeit already riding on the part.
  - **The multiply reads as pathetic, then is spent.** All five throw the SAME arm at the SAME empty air on the SAME frame and hold; from f240 to f255 they are the ONLY things in the frame not moving while fan, curtain, sparks, fog, clerks, flags and drum roar. The beam sweep is the reveal, not the kill: as it LEAVES each copy (f246 to f252) that copy loses its columns, goes flat, and sinks into the fog. By f252 exactly ONE is left, the ORIGINAL (x 760), lit and alone. He is the one who lunges at f250, so the guy who fails is the guy S6 inherits. Frame 255: one villain, arm around air.
  - **The wrench drum moved into the always-visible band** (x 165 to 245, lip setY 690), on the chute's left-spitting fall line. Fourteen fanned and countable at f0, the fall visible for 385 units, CLANG in shot at f66, chalk tally stroked to fifteen at f70. This applies fix 4 option (b) and fix 11's placement together, and it satisfies fix 23's real complaint (the gag fired off-panel and L8 was invisible for 156 frames) at the source. Fix 23's "keep it at setY 1000 and delete the claims" is the fallback for a drum that cannot move; this one can. Surviving from fix 23: "rocking and ringing after every landing" is deleted (there is one landing), and L8 earns genuine f0-to-f255 life from the hydraulic-event shifts plus the crown wrench that teeters from f0 and slips at f250.
  - **The hero is readable for 256 frames and never leaves.** Office front is ONE clean pane, mesh confined to edge panels and door, sill lamp at (250, 205) front-keying his face, "backlit silhouette theatre" killed. Office extended to setY 400; its glazed nose holds in the upper-left corner after the crane with the hero at the lower pane. The smear moves from (300, 240) to **(300, 350)**, because at camY 310 the old height is 70 units above the panel and both prop and payoff were unrenderable, and because he reaches down.
  - **Fresh Eyes has no tint.** Exact warm `#D97757`. Cool cream-gray is S2's YOU. The tell is zero grime in a filthy shop, plus the shrink-wrap and the sachet.
  - **`PRIOR CONTEXT 0` becomes `MEMORY 0`.** Same slot, same mono readout, same number, plain words.
  - **Text floor enforced.** One maker's mark survives: FANUX MK-II. Killed: PNEUMO-TUBE CO. EST 1948 (including on the reducer, where fix 15 wanted it, so the reducer proves the two sizes are one machine with **repeated cast geometry** instead of a wordmark), VAC-U-KLENE, the pigeonhole numbers, the gauge psi ticks. Work order cut to ONE line at 40px minimum cap height, legible f80 to f110 (30+ frames), CHANGE line killed so the order and S6's press rhyme.
  - **The wink gets 8 frames, twice**, with the head tilting up into the key and the bed dropped to the fan under a single glassy TINK. The f242 to f252 sweep lights each copy for ~5 frames so the glints are countable, never a strobe.
  - **One arm, one continuous promotion.** The second arm is deleted. FANUX carries the pen in a holster cast into its own housing, visible from f0. f56 to f66 it clamps, posts, rotates and presses the pen in one unbroken move, held clean and centred f62 to f74 on a readable face. Slap count drops from three to two to pay for it.
  - **Layer audit passed.** Eight layers live in the back half, not five. Added item 12b THE DESPATCH COUNTER (x 40 to 260, setY 420 to 580) with 12 spring-flagged pigeonholes and a twin pressure dial at (240, 560) slaved to the same needle. Note: fix 12's x 60 to 300 / setY 620 to 780 collided with fix 11's mandated drum gap, so the counter is the back-wall bank and the drum sits 110 units nearer and lower. Clerk beats moved to CLERK B at (150, 620): lean f204, pigeonhole close f242, frozen mid-file f255. Clerk A stays upstairs and leaves with the office. Rag B added at (900, 460) for the whole-scene 40-frame cadence; rag A keeps the f120 elbow puff; rag C at (200, 990) does post-crane lens duty (re-timed from f228 to f240 with the multiply, and its f120 slot given to rag A, which is the only rag in shot at f120).
  - **The insert is real.** f74 to f110 at 3x, populated with nub, pen, jitter, lifting pad edge, torn corner with pink carbon, the spike and its two pink copies near-left, the send-port flap breathing at the right edge, and the office's meshed upper edge panel across the top so we stay in the fishbowl (the only mesh fix 5 leaves standing). Eases out by f110.
