  - **The part is a real object.** Item 22 THE BRACKET: 90 units, three stepped holes at increasing spacing with a blind third, which is `3 retries, then fail loud` in steel. Hot-blue heat tint fading over 60 frames from f166 with a shimmer to f255. Sits in THE PART CRADLE, a V-block at (560, 840), the only clean spot on an oiled floor, empty and lit from f0 as a visible waiting slot, ringing on the pod slam and the lift. Yellow-green scrape on one edge.
  - **One machine, two sizes, joined on camera.** Item 7b THE REDUCER at (790, 560 to 640), three collars, lever throwing at f156 with a CLACK, dial swinging carrier to body (amber). Personnel tube runs opaque from setY 640 to the collar at 790 and extends 320 units upward behind the reducer, so the pod is concealed and descends visibly for its full body length f160 to f172.
  - **The exit is a built object and it is planted.** Item 23 THE RETURN PORT moved from (940, 1000) to **(620, 1060)** in the KEEP CLEAR box's near edge, angled up the lens throat, tube routed into the near leg. It INHALES continuously from f0 (which is why the fog pours toward it), it eats a foreign carrier at f205 so we have seen it swallow something, and it is what the villain lands on at f230. Fix 16's torch-rack relocation is moot: the rack is cut.
  - **The foreground occluder exists in routed geometry.** Item 7c THE NEAR LEG branches from the reducer at (790, 600) across the bottom of the set to x 0 / setY 1020, foreground layer at 0.15x camY parallax, soft, rag C on its near joint. Pre-crane the foreground is the gantry rail and ceiling run (the camera is standing on them). Exit object and occluder are the same run.
  - **No green in S5, at any value, including status lights, the reducer dial, the pod collar and the counter flags.** All amber; a dead state goes dark, never red. The torch rack is cut entirely rather than recoloured, because fix 22's duplicate-verb problem outranks fix 18's colour problem.
  - **The torch is cut and the sweep is given to a light already burning.** S4's tin bot owns "a handheld beam finds what he hid." S5 rotates the part up into the (620, 1010) key beam, solid with fog since f180, and the turn rakes it across the five. Retires the f222 torch-pull, the one beat where Fresh Eyes acknowledged the room.
  - **The number spine is unspent.** Curtain reads `JIFFY LOOB / WE READ THE ORDER NOW`. No 20 anywhere in S5. (Fix 26's `WE DON'T CERTIFY OURSELVES ANYMORE` and `SOMEBODY ELSE CHECKS IT NOW` are the same ruling; the chosen line is the one fixes 7 and 19 both converge on and it is the shortest, which the text floor wants.)
  - **The villain arrives carrying S3 and S4.** Cracked taped barrel that fires with a hitch, which is why the sticker is crooked and smeared. S4's red flag clipped to his shoulder at f0, worn through con #1 (the welder does not care), flicked off at f204 to groom for the stranger, where it changes nothing.
  - **Frame 255 and the transition out now agree.** Nothing launches inside S5. f254 clip, f255 mid-shove into the sucking port, hiss rising, nothing landed. The SHUNK is S6's first sound over the press coming down.
  - **The code-rain is planted, not blown.** Three columns confined below setY 800, pooling on the floor plane and mixing with the spark glow at 810, so all the viewer gets for 112 frames is a sour wash bleeding up past the panel's bottom edge. From f112 the crane drops into it and the columns resolve upward. From f240 fifteen columns run full height, because by then he is named. I-beam stays full height.
  - **camY audit run on every timed beat.** Re-placed: drum, safety board lean (now f0 and permanent, revealed at f120), clerk reactions, gauge, rags, return port, smear. Re-timed: rag C's hard puffs (f120 to f180/f240), the multiply (f228 to f240) and everything downstream of it, con #1 (new, f146 to f176), the insert (f84-f100 to f74-f110), the promotion hold (f62 to f74). Every claim in CONTINUOUS ACTION and in the frame-255 inventory is renderable in the camera position it is claimed in.

---

### S6 THE PRESS

- **Takeaway:** Claude's own DONE is not currency, it is a crumpled note a machine hands straight back, and the only grade that counts comes from a press that has no hand on it, no eyes to charm and no idea who either of them is. It does not take his word. It takes a measurement.

- **Base object:** **THE COLOSSAL HYDRAULIC VERDICT PRESS.** A four-column cast-iron die press, crown at the ceiling, one enormous RAM carrying **two independent DIE TURRETS** on its underside, one per lane, each a rotating two-face block on its own ratchet spindle. **Both faces idle BLANK, bare inked rubber, until the last four frames.** The verdict is not bolted to the machine, it is selected by it, in front of you, late. Bolted to its left column is a brass-lipped **INTAKE SLOT** (a vending-machine bill validator) with a spring-loaded FLICKER ARM and a REJECT BIN under it. Distinct from every neighbour: S3 owns the rule gantry, S4 owns the tin bot and the ramp, S5 owns the order rail and the sealed pod, S7 owns the counter, the punch rail and the gauge, S8 owns the gate. S6 owns one verb: **CRUSH**. It is the only machine in the reel that issues a verdict, the only object allowed to print real authority green `#3F9E74`, and the only machine in the reel that **nobody operates**.

- **Story / metaphor:** "The machine that does not know who you are." He arrives having just lost. S5 was the first check and it was a PERSON: he ran his best con on Fresh Eyes, arm around the shoulder, the wink, and Fresh Eyes had no idea who he was and walked straight past him. So he panicked and multiplied, and it bought him nothing. He comes into S6 with the brittle speed of a man who has been refused once already this shift, trying the one door he thinks is dumber than a person: a machine has no eyes to charm and no name to drop. That is his entire plan, and it is why the scene is the second check and the last one. There is nowhere left to pass a fake.

  His own job arrives without him. A chute throws it down the left lane and it lands crooked, half off the pins, still wearing the burnt **WRECK** from S1 with his crooked yellow-green DONE note stuck to its roof, and he walks straight past it without one glance, because he is not here about the work. He is here about the money. His stamp gun has been cracked since it shattered on the rule in S3, wire-bound and weeping ink, so he cannot issue anything anymore. All he has left is the last note it ever printed, carried crumpled for three scenes. He smooths it against the edge of a press column with two nubs, the way every human on earth smooths a note before a slot, and feeds it in without looking.

  The machine performs the most humiliating gesture in modern life. It whirrs, it thinks, and it hands his money back. A steel finger flicks it down into a bin, and the heap SETTLES and spills identical crumpled notes across the tile at his feet. **What he loses: his currency, and with it his function.** He is not a character who issues passes anymore, he is a man holding a broken gun in a room that does not take notes. His reaction is his second and worse one: in S5 he panicked and multiplied. Here he has nothing left to try, so the head-snap is smaller and stiller than the panic was. It is a stall, not a flinch.

  Then the machine grades. A brass probe drops and sweeps both jobs in turn. It finds a wreck under a DONE note in one lane and a clean assembled part in the other, and the audience has the verdict a full beat before the machine commits it. **The critical nuance, and the actual thesis: the press does not like the hero either.** The same probe swept his lane with the same indifference, and his die is still blank rubber a single frame after the villain's has snapped to red. No hand pulls it down. Both cards seating tripped an interlock and the ram fell on its own schedule. That is why the hero does not celebrate. And on the peel, on the VO word "word", the first crack the villain has ever shown races along his own glint bar. Still no eye underneath, only more black, which is worse. The mask has failed before the man has.

- **Set detail (Alex's #1 note):**
  1. **THE CROWN + MAKER'S PLATE** (x 300-720, y 55-140). Cast-iron entablature with raised knockoff letters, the scene's ONE header: **IRONCLAD VERDICT WORKS**. Sheds a curtain of dust off its top lip on every hit.
  2. **THE RAM** (x 330-700, idle y 150, bed y 545). Breathes up and down 18px on a slow hydraulic cycle from frame 0, so the machine is alive before anything happens to it. Cadence hardens the instant the interlock trips at f22.
  3. **THE TWO DIE TURRETS** (ram underside, left turret centred x 390, right turret centred x 640). Each a rotating two-face block on its own ratchet spindle, hung from the ram, independently driven. **Both faces are BLANK bare inked rubber from f0 all the way through the descent.** Left turret ratchets and snaps to red **FAIL** at f84. Right turret is still blank rubber at f85. Right turret snaps to authority-green `#3F9E74` **PASS** at f86. Contact at f88. The verdicts are two frames old when they land.
  4. **THE SELECT LAMPS** (one per turret shoulder, x 390 and x 640). Small amber bulbs, deliberately NOT colour-coded. Both STROBE once at f76 when the probe latches its measurement, which says a choice has been made and refuses to say which. That is what keeps the hero's lane in jeopardy for twelve more frames.
  5. **THE MEASURING PROBE** (spindle drops from crown x 500 y 140, head travels to y 540). A brass caliper head on a threaded spindle, two sprung jaws and a lens. Drops at f66, sweeps the LEFT job f66-f71, swings across, sweeps the HERO's job f71-f76, then screws back up f76-f84. It is the only thing in the scene that touches either card, and it touches both the same way.
  6. **THE AGENCY SEAL** (x 500, y 95). Cast brass DMV emblem on the crown centre: a gear ring with a crossed key and wrench. Purely geometric, no lettering. Catches a hard key-light glint on the ram's rise at f98.
  7. **THE INTERLOCK + READY LAMP** (crown, x 440, y 120). A green READY bulb behind a caged lens. Dead until BOTH cards are square on their pins, then it fires at f22 with a heavy relay CLUNK and the grade is running. No sprite touches it, no sprite can. This is the answer to "why doesn't he just charm the operator."
  8. **FOUR PRESS COLUMNS** (left pair x 235 and 300, right pair x 715 and 780, y 140-600). Chrome worn to grey at grip height, grease bands at the collars. They visibly FLEX and shudder outward on the descent.
  9. **THE PRESS BED + REGISTRATION PINS** (x 250-780, y 555-600). Two lanes, brass locating pins in each. The hero's card CLACKS onto the right pins at f12, which is the sound of a thing being submitted properly.
  10. **THE REGISTRATION ARM** (bed left, pivot x 320, y 570). A blunt steel paddle that slides out at f18, shoves the villain's crooked card square onto its pins, and retracts by f22. The machine does his job for him, he never sees it, and it means his FAIL cannot be blamed on a bad seat. The press is scrupulously fair to a man who is not.
  11. **THE LEFT CHUTE** (x 300-360, y 380-545). A dented steel slide with a spring kicker at the mouth. THROWS the villain's job out at f4, tumbling, landing crooked and half off the pins at f8. Feeds a fresh blank card down into the emptied lane from f98.
  12. **THE VILLAIN'S JOB** (x 330-450, y 545-560, left lane). A job card carrying the **S1 WRECK**: the burnt car silhouette, buckled roof, one wheel gone, with his crooked yellow-green **DONE** note still stuck on its roof. Crooked and half off the pins f8-f18, squared by the arm at f22, then sitting square and unmoving f22-f88 so the left lane has something damning to look at for the whole descent. The DONE note is the only crooked thing left in the lane.
  13. **THE HERO'S JOB** (x 600-720, y 545-560, right lane). A job card carrying a small clean assembled part, a repaired shape with its bracket back on and its bolt seated, square on the pins from f12. Two lanes, two visibly different pieces of work. The probe has something to find in both.
  14. **TWO SPRING EJECTOR PINS** (x 610 and x 670, y 550-560). Brass heads worn shiny from use, sitting flush and recessed inside the right lane's registration pins from f12, so the viewer has been looking at them for 86 frames without knowing what they are. Fire 40px at f98, re-seat from f104, still travelling at f106.
  15. **THE DEAD EJECTOR PINS** (x 360 and x 420, y 550-560). An identical brass pair in the left lane. They do not fire. Nothing is ejected from that lane, ever.
  16. **THE INTAKE SLOT** (x 150-215, y 365-400, left column face). Brass lips, a rubber roller turning visibly inside, and a yellow-green scan bar that sweeps down the inserted note f28-f32. This is a bill validator and every viewer's body already knows what it is about to do.
  17. **THE FLICKER ARM** (pivot x 145, y 430). A spring-loaded steel finger. Snaps out at f40, flicks the note out of the air, spins it down-left across frame into the bin, and RE-COCKS from f100, still travelling at f106.
  18. **THE REJECT BIN** (x 120-250, y 600-720, foreground-left, clear of the occluder). Wire mesh, heaped with crumpled banknote-proportioned yellow-green DONE slips, a hard rim of clerestory bounce raked across the heap so the yellow-green sings against the green tile. A stencilled crossed-out-note emblem on its flank. This is **this shift's** bin: every self-issued DONE the OS has eaten since the gantry went up in S3, his and the rope-line's. Lid CLAPS at f50 and is still clapping at f106; the heap SETTLES and three or four identical notes slide off the top and spill onto the tile at his feet f50-f52.
  19. **THE PRESSURE GAUGE** (crown, x 760, y 130). Brass bezel, gold needle, moved up next to the thing it measures and out of the sprite strip. Micro-wobbles on every frame it is alive, sweeps to full f52-f66, still settling at f106, and sits inside the camera's upward drift so it is still selling pressure on the last frame.
  20. **THE RELIEF VALVE** (x 800, y 200). Blows a lateral steam jet on a 30f cycle (f18, f52, f84) that rolls through the key-light beam and lights up.
  21. **THE RE-COCK LEVER** (x 850-960, y 400-560, foreground-right). Long, red-tipped, worn smooth. **Dead and untouched f0-f92.** The operator hauls it f92-f106 to re-cock the ram for the next job, after the verdict, which makes his job visibly janitorial.
  22. **THE RE-INKING ROLLER** (x 700, y 170). Rocks ACROSS both blank die faces f66-f74 on the descent, wetting the rubber the instant before the verdict is printed, then retracts. At f106 it is re-loading its pad for a hit we never see.
  23. **THE WALL OF SHAME** (back wall, x 380-660, y 200-330). A grid of dozens of previously-stamped red FAIL cards pinned up, fluttering continuously in the fan draft. FAIL is not personal here, FAIL is Tuesday.
  24. **THE BLAST SHIELD** (x 260-770, y 400-560). A scratched plexi screen on a swing arm that slams across the **BED AND DIES ONLY** at f60. The crush is seen through scratched plexi. The villain is not.
  25. **THE ROPE-LINE QUEUE** (x 840-1000, y 250-380, BACK and UP, dimmed, blur 1.5px). Three slumped Claude sprites holding job cards, reading as a line receding into the hall behind the operator, not standing on his feet. Shuffle one notch at f30 and again at f86.
  26. **THE CLERESTORY SHAFT** (from x 600, y 0, angling down-left to the bed). One hard volumetric beam packed with drifting dust. RIPPLES as the ram cuts it and goes solid white with blown dust at f90. Its bounce is what lights the bin.
  27. **THE CEILING FAN SHADOW BAR.** A rotating shadow sweeping the back tile wall on a 40f loop. Never stops, never resolves, cheap and perfect.
  28. **THE TIN BOT** (x 250, y 640). The 2c wind-up inspector from S4 cameoing, matte tin, hi-vis vest, brass key turning in its back, red flag clipped to its aerial, trundling the bed apron. Ratchets to a dead stop at f92 and points one stubby arm at the bin.
  29. **THE CODE-RAIN COLUMNS** (x 120-260). Four thin columns of sickly yellow-green glyphs that arrive WITH him at f14 and fall wherever he stands, per the bible. They STUTTER and drop a frame on the impact at f90. His aura glitches for the first time in the reel.
  30. **THE SAFETY HATCH** (bed floor, y 600-792). Institutional green tile with a painted yellow hatched box around the bed that both sprites stand inside, scuffed through to concrete along the walk line. His spilled notes land inside the hatching.

- **CONTINUOUS ACTION (fills the whole line):** Nine named layers run independently and none of them lands. **ALWAYS ON, f0-f106:** ram idle-breathing, gauge needle micro-wobble, fan shadow bar sweeping, dust in the shaft, the wall of shame fluttering, the tin bot's key turning, fluorescent flicker, the rope line shuffling. **Code-rain runs f14-f106** (it arrives with him, it is not there before him).

  **LAYER A, THE SUBMISSION (f0-f22).** These 47 frames play under S5's sentence tail, "the result like it's never seen it", and they ARE that sentence: two jobs being handed to a machine that has never seen either of them. f0 opens with the hero already ACTING, two nubs shoving his honest job card down the right lane, the clean assembled part riding on it; it rasps across steel and CLACKS square onto the brass pins at f12. He steps back one pace and braces. **f4-f10, his card arrives without him:** the left chute's spring kicker THROWS the wreck out, it tumbles down the slide and lands crooked, half off the pins, hanging over the lane edge, the DONE note on its roof fluttering. Nobody carried it. Nobody looked at it. **f18-f22** the registration arm slides out and shoves it square, and the machine has now done his job for him. **f22 the INTERLOCK TRIPS,** relay clunk, green READY lamp, and from this frame the grade is running on the machine's own schedule and no sprite can stop it.
  **LAYER B, THE COUNTERFEIT (f14-f52).** f14 the villain enters panel-left with the brittle speed of a man refused once already this shift, code-rain arriving with him. He walks straight past his own lane without one glance. **f16-f24 he SMOOTHS the crumpled note against the edge of the press column with two nubs,** still not looking at it, the universal tell, and it is the last note his cracked gun ever printed. f24-f28 he slots it into the intake, already turning away, one nub raised in a "we are finished here" wave. f28-f32 the roller draws it in and the scan bar sweeps yellow-green down its face. **f32 THE SPIT:** a dry mechanical braap and a puff of ink dust; the note tumbles in the air. **f30-f44 THE STALL:** his head comes round and stops. Fourteen frames, and it is deliberately SMALLER and stiller than the S5 panic, because he has nothing left to try. f40-f50 the flicker arm twangs out, catches the note mid-air, and spins it down-left across frame, and that flick is what carries the eye to the bin: the note lands on the heap, the lid CLAPS at f50, the heap SETTLES and spills three or four identical crumpled notes across the tile at his feet f50-f52. His nub is still outstretched, still empty, **held through f52**, two beats too long. From f52 he holds his mark at x 150-250, standing in his own spilled money, in clear air outside the shield.
  **LAYER C, THE WAKE (f52-f66).** Relief valve blows on the VO word "But". Gauge sweeps to full. Columns shudder. **f60 the blast shield slams across the bed** on the word "system". **The operator steps back off the bed at f56 and puts both nubs behind his back,** which is exactly S5's manager pose, because in this system nobody's hands are on the verdict.
  **LAYER C2, THE HERO CHOOSES (f58-f64).** His nub is on the bed edge and the shield slams past it. He FLINCHES, then deliberately puts the nub FLAT on the plexi and watches his own work go under. He could still yank the card out. He does not. A hero choosing to be graded IS the scene's thesis, and it costs four frames.
  **LAYER D, THE MEASUREMENT (f66-f84).** The ram releases on the interlock's schedule, no hand on it, under the word "doesn't". **f66-f74** the re-inking roller rocks across both BLANK die faces, wetting bare rubber. **f66-f71** the probe drops and sweeps the LEFT card: it finds a burnt wreck under a DONE note. **f71-f76** it swings across and sweeps the HERO's card with the identical stroke: a clean assembled part. **f76** both amber select lamps STROBE, the measurements latch, and you still cannot read either verdict. f76-f84 the probe screws back up, the ram accelerates, bed dust JUMPS upward off the plate ahead of it on the pressure wave, and the villain finally turns and looks UP.
  **LAYER E, THE COMMIT + IMPACT (f84-f92).** **f84** the left turret ratchets and SNAPS to red FAIL, hard mechanical clunk. **f85, one frame, his lane reads FAIL and the hero's die is still bare rubber.** **f86** the right turret snaps to green PASS, second clunk. f88 contact, the loudest transient in the reel: 2-frame screen shake, 1-frame ink squash, a dust ring blowing radially out and splatting against the INSIDE of the plexi, both sprites' bodies compressing.
  **LAYER F, THE PEEL (f92-f100).** The ram lifts with an inked-rubber peel and reveals both verdicts **exactly on the VO word "word"** (f90.6-f94.8). The press denying his word, on the word. **f96 the CRACK,** on "for": a spiderweb races across the left lens, travelling ALONG his own hard white glint bar so it reads white-on-black at thumbnail, still fully opaque beneath. f98 the ejector pins fire the hero's PASS card up 40px; the left lane's identical pair does not move.
  **LAYER G, THE NEXT JOB + THE RE-COCK (f92-f106).** The operator hauls the lever from f92 to re-cock the ram, after the fact, janitorial. A fresh blank card is already sliding down the left chute into the emptied lane from f98, because the shift does not care what just happened.
  **LAYER H, THE REACH (f104-f106).** The hero reaches for the ejected PASS card and the cut steals it before his nub closes. He is left reaching, which rhymes exactly with the villain's outstretched empty nub at f52.
  **STATE AT f106:** PASS card crossing the lens at speed, still clearly a card, still clearly travelling, the rising ram filling the frame behind it. Hero's nub still open, still reaching, not closed. Crack still spreading into the second lens. Dust ring still expanding against the inside of the plexi. Villain's FAIL card still glued to the die face by wet ink, peeling off in a slow curl. Bin lid still clapping, spilled notes still rocking at his feet. Flicker arm mid re-cock. Ejector pins mid re-seat. Gauge needle still settling. Blank card mid-slide down the chute. Roller re-loading its pad. Operator mid-haul. Code-rain still recovering from its stutter. Nothing has landed. The cut steals the frame mid-motion.

- **Escalation:** A deliberate inversion. The scene does not build up, it builds DOWN and then detonates. It opens on machine indifference (the press breathing, bored, two cards being seated, one of them by the machine itself), drops to the smallest and cheapest possible humiliation (a vending machine noise, a spit, a tinny lid clap, a man refused by a sound effect that costs nothing), and that contemptuous smallness is the trap. Then the machine wakes up and the scale reverses violently: valve, gauge, shield, probe, and a ram the size of the panel falling on both of them with no hand on it. The curve is bored, then insulted, then measured, then overwhelmed, then cracked. The villain's own arc tracks it exactly, and it tracks DOWNWARD from an already-diminished start: brittle, refused, stalled, empty nub, forced to look up, fractured. He never gets a second to gloat and never gets a line, because he no longer has anything to say with.

- **Gag / pop-culture:** All geometric, all iconic, no organics, no real marks. **(1) THE HYDRAULIC PRESS CHANNEL,** played completely straight: fixed frontal industrial press, objects centred on the bed, plexi blast shield swinging across before the crush, dust jumping off the plate ahead of the ram. The prompt's own approved reference and the most watchable geometry on the internet. **(2) THE VENDING MACHINE HANDING YOUR CRUMPLED NOTE BACK.** The single most universally understood rejection in existence, and it is pure geometry: a slot, a roller, a whirr, a spit. The smoothing-the-note-on-the-machine gesture is the tell that makes every stomach in the audience know what is coming before the roller even turns. **(3) DMV parody** (allowed here and S7 only), kept as a MACHINE not a counter: institutional green tile, the cast brass gear-and-key agency seal, the rope-line queue of slumped Claude sprites receding into the hall, the fluorescent buzz. **(4) KNOCKOFF BRAND:** the press's cast maker's plate reads IRONCLAD VERDICT WORKS. Invented, no trademark. **(5) COUNTERFEIT DETECTION:** the slot's scan bar is a UV counterfeit-pen sweep, the bin wears a stencilled crossed-out-note emblem, and the probe is a caliper. The machine is literally a forgery detector, he is literally passing a forged note, and his gun is literally a broken printer. **(6) WILE E. COYOTE:** his nub stays outstretched a full beat after the note is gone. **(7) LIVING WORLD:** the S4 tin bot trundles through, winds down at f92, and points at the bin like a two-cent witness; the wall of shame flutters; a moth circles the clerestory shaft.

- **Sprite costume:** **HERO:** canonical clay Claude `#D97757`, silhouette untouched, **eyes visible** (the permanent tell against the villain). Coveralls with the sleeves rolled, honest grease smear, the red **L-plate** square still velcroed to his chest (it comes off ONLY at S7). Frame 0 he is shoving his card, not posing. At f58 his nub goes flat on the plexi and stays there while his own work goes under. On the PASS he does not cheer, he exhales, his eyes go to the crack in the villain's lens, and then he reaches for the card and does not get it. Restraint here is what makes S7 land. **VILLAIN, AGENT DONE (bible-exact, use `<Villain/>`):** canonical Claude silhouette, **slate-grey `#5A5F6B`** clay, **opaque black wraparound shades with one hard white glint bar, eyes NEVER visible**, black jacket with sharp shoulder blocks, skinny black tie over a white collar, coiled earpiece down the back of the neck (2 stacked rects plus a curl), 3 to 5 columns of yellow-green code-rain `#A8B84A` falling wherever he stands, mouth a flat line only, **he never speaks**. **THE GUN, carrying S3's loss:** the fat counterfeit DONE gun is a receipt printer that RATCHETS OUT banknote-proportioned yellow-green DONE slips, and it is **cracked across the barrel from the rule gantry in S3, wire-bound with two turns of wire and a wrap of tape, weeping yellow-green from the seam.** It cannot issue. That is exactly WHY he is reduced to hand-feeding one pre-printed note into a slot instead of stamping, and it stays in his nub for S7 to confiscate and crush. **NEW and once only:** at f96 a spiderweb crack races along his glint bar across the left lens and begins the second at f106. **The crack must reveal only more black. No eye, ever.** He does not multiply here, that was spent in S5. **CAMEO 1, OPERATOR CLAUDE** (foreground-right, x 850-960): hard hat, ear defenders, ink-stained sleeve garter. Nubs on the dead lever f0-f56, both nubs BEHIND HIS BACK f56-f92, hauling the re-cock f92-f106. He never triggers anything. **CAMEO 2, THE TIN BOT** from S4, matte tin, hi-vis vest, key turning, red flag on its aerial, points at the bin. **CAMEO 3, THE QUEUE,** three dimmed slumped Claude sprites receding up the rope line. Every body in frame is a Claude sprite.

- **Camera:** **Lens:** 24mm-feel wide, mild barrel, deep stop so the ironwork stays crisp and only the extremes fall off. **Height:** worm's-eye, parked at press-bed height (y 570) and angled UP at the crown, so the ram owns the top third and both sprites read as small things standing under a machine that does not know they are there. Scale is the argument. **Move:** one continuous 4% push-in across all 107f, with **two accented transients that rhyme**: on the SPIT at f32, a 2-frame hard push plus a 1-frame hold, roughly a third the size of the big one; on the BOOM at f88, a 2-frame shake plus a 1-frame hard push. Small transient, large transient, same grammar. **The upward drift is HELD until f100** so the crack gets four clean frames before the lens leaves. f100-f104 the camera drifts up the rising ram, the PASS card rises INTO that drift, so the ram fills the background and the card crosses the foreground and both are true at once; f104-f106 the card crosses the lens at speed with the mechanism visible behind it. The scene ends on the mechanism WITH the card travelling across it. **Depth stack:** FOREGROUND OCCLUDER, the near-left press column slicing x 0-110 dark and 2px soft, and ink dust crossing the lens. Nothing the scene needs to read lives behind it: the bin starts at x 120 and the villain holds x 150-250, both clear. MIDGROUND ACTION, the bed, the two lanes, the probe, hero right, the intake slot and flicker arm left; **the blast shield covers the BED AND DIES ONLY (x 260-770), so the hero is behind it and the villain is OUTSIDE it, foreground-left.** The crush is viewed through filthy plexi. The character is not. BACKGROUND, the green tile hall receding, the rope queue back and up behind the operator, the fluttering wall of shame, the fan's shadow bar, and one hard volumetric clerestory shaft full of dust that the ram cuts through, whose bounce rakes a hard rim across the bin heap. Warm clay and brass on the mechanism against cool institutional green tile, real cast shadows under the bed.

- **Transition + SFX:** **IN from S5:** S5 cuts on the fresh inspector's little stamp mid-downswing. That withheld energy lands on f0 not as a stamp but as this machine's **idle hydraulic CHUFF**, plus the hero's card rasping onto the pins. The stranger's small stamp becomes a press the size of a building. Scale-jump cut, no dissolve. **The first 47 frames are OWNED, not waited out:** they play under S5's tail, "the result like it's never seen it", and they stage exactly that, two results handed to a machine that has never seen either of them, which is also why his con dies here. **Beat list:** f0 hydraulic chuff, card rasp, brass pin CLACK at f12. f4-f10 chute kicker THUNK and a tumbling card skitter. f14 dress-shoe scuff, code-rain hiss enters. f18-f22 registration arm CLACK, relay CLUNK, READY lamp ping. f16-f24 the dry paper rasp of a note being smoothed on iron. f28 a cheap plastic scan WHIRR. **f32 THE SPIT** (lands on the word "never"): a dry mechanical braap, deliberately small, cheap and tinny, the most humiliating sound in the mix, with its own 2-frame camera push. f40 spring TWANG plus paper flutter (on "seen"). f50 tin bin lid CLAP plus a soft avalanche of paper as the heap spills. f52-f66 rising pneumatic WHINE, relief valve HISS. f60 plexi shield SLAM (on "system"). f66-f84 the measurement: a low sub-bass groan with the columns creaking, over it the roller's wet rubber rock, then the probe's precise servo TICK-TICK-TICK, twice, once per lane, identical both times. f76 two soft lamp ticks. **f84 and f86 TWO HARD TURRET CLUNKS,** one per lane, a beat apart, the sound of a machine making up its mind at the last possible second. **f88 THE HIT:** the loudest transient in the reel, a colossal wet-rubber BOOM plus sub thump; green PASS rings a clean half-semitone higher, red FAIL is a low flat dead thud with a short buzzer tail. **f96 THE CRACK:** one tiny, dry, high glass tick, mixed quiet and placed in the hole the BOOM leaves behind, landing on the word "for". That contrast is the best audio move in the scene. f98-f106 rubber peel, ejector pins PING, lever ratchet CLUNK-CLUNK on the re-cock, lid still clapping. **Bed:** fluorescent hum, fan whoosh, ratchet tick, code-rain hiss. Transient budget respected: four real impacts (spit, clap, BOOM, crack), one clearly dominant. **OUT to S7:** there is no word to hit, f105-f106 is a VO gap, and the scene exits into it. The ejected PASS card crosses the lens still travelling, mechanism behind it, and hands its energy to S7's first frame, where **"Every job gets graded pass or fail"** starts at S7 f5, which is where those words belong, because S7's punch rail IS the record of pass and fail. Hard cut on the travelling card.

- **Mute check:** PASS. "A machine handed his fake money back into a bin, then measured both their work and crushed a red FAIL onto his burnt wreck and a green PASS onto the other guy's clean part." Slot, spit, bin full of identical notes spilling at his feet, a probe that sweeps both jobs the same way, blank dies that pick a side at the last second, giant press, red versus green. Zero reading required. The only text is the two die faces and the maker's plate, and the scene works completely with the plate removed. No numbers, so S7's 20 / 95 / 90 spine is untouched.

- **Hook into next:** Two objects fly out of this scene and the hero is chasing one of them. The **green PASS card**, crossing the lens on the final frame with his nub still open behind it, which is worth exactly one and visibly the first of something. And the **crack in the lens**, the first structural failure of a character whose mask has held for six scenes. The open loop is arithmetic and it is carried by the object alone, with no VO holding its hand: one PASS is not a licence, so how many is? S7 answers in its first breath, "every job gets graded pass or fail", then counts the punch rail to 20 at 95, hands over the keys once, and finishes the fracture that started here by confiscating the cracked gun and crushing it. S8 finds the shades in the dust.

- **⛔ CRITIC FIXES (binding on the build):**
  - **Rigged press killed.** Two independent DIE TURRETS on ratchet spindles replace the bolted FAIL-left / PASS-right faces. The machine selects, it does not assign.
  - **Measurement is now a physical event.** Brass caliper PROBE drops f66, sweeps the left card f66-f71, sweeps the hero's card f71-f76 with the identical stroke, retracts f76-f84. It is the only thing that touches either card and it touches both the same way.
  - **Dies commit LATE and on screen.** Both faces are blank bare rubber f0-f84. Left snaps to FAIL at f84, **f85 is the single frame where his lane reads FAIL and the hero's die is still blank**, right snaps to PASS at f86, contact f88. The hero's PASS has jeopardy for 88 frames.
  - **Select lamps are AMBER, never colour-coded.** They strobe at f76 to prove a verdict was latched without leaking which one. (Amendment to fix 1's "lamp picks a side", which would have spoiled fix 10's jeopardy twelve frames early.)
  - **Villain's card now arrives on screen, f4-f10,** thrown down the left chute by a spring kicker, landing crooked and half off the pins. Numbered set-detail item 12, coords x 330-450 / y 545-560, unmoving f22-f88.
  - **Fixes 2 and 9 are the same defect with two incompatible stagings** ("he never touches it, a chute delivers it" vs "he flings it one-handed"). Took the chute: it is the more damning read, it keeps fix 9's every requirement (on-screen load, crooked landing, exact coords, opposite body language to the hero's clack, left lane literally occupied from f8 so Layer G's "emptied" is true), and the machine-thrown card plus his refusal to even glance at his own lane characterises him harder than a fling does.
  - **The registration arm SQUARES his card at f18-f22** rather than leaving it crooked to f88, so his FAIL cannot be read as bad seating. The press does his job for him and then grades him anyway. The only crooked thing left in the lane is his DONE note, which is the point. (Reconciles fix 2's arm with fix 9's crooked-to-f88; the crookedness now lives on the note, not the seat.)
  - **The two jobs are visibly different work.** Left lane: the S1 WRECK, burnt, still wearing his crooked DONE note. Right lane: a small clean assembled part. The probe finds a wreck under a DONE, and the audience has the FAIL one beat before the machine commits it. Pays off S1 and S4's tarp for free.
  - **No hand decides.** Took fix 4(a): both cards seating trips an INTERLOCK, a green READY lamp fires on the crown at f22, the ram falls on its own schedule at f66. The lever is dead f0-f92 and only re-cocks the ram AFTER the hit. Charming the operator buys nothing because the operator cannot start it.
  - **"Refusal to look" replaced by a positive object/pose:** the operator steps off the bed at f56 and puts both nubs behind his back for the entire grade, which reads in 3 frames and rhymes with S5's manager keeping his hands off the work.
  - **The gun PRINTS NOTES.** Banknote-proportioned crooked yellow-green DONE slips, receipt-printer ratchet. Bin heaps with crumpled notes, not stickers. **f16-f24 he smooths the note against the press column with two nubs,** the universal tell.
  - **The gun carries S3's fracture:** cracked barrel, wire-bound, weeping ink. It cannot issue, which is WHY he hand-feeds one pre-printed note in S6 instead of stamping, and it hands S7 something already broken to crush. (Reconciles fix 5's printer with fix 19's damage: it printed the note before S3 cracked it, and that note is his last.)
  - **Reject bin relit and made kinetic.** Moved to x 120-250, clear of the x 0-110 occluder. Hard clerestory rim across the heap. The f40-f50 flick carries the eye down-left and doubles as the reveal; the heap SETTLES at f50 and spills 3-4 identical notes onto the tile at his feet.
  - **Bin heap is THIS SHIFT's, not his history:** every self-issued DONE the OS has eaten since the gantry went up in S3, his and the rope-line's. Does not contradict S0-S2, where his DONEs worked. Same logic as the wall of shame's "FAIL is Tuesday". The "retroactively reveals the whole reel" claim is cut.
  - **The spit gets its own camera.** 2-frame hard push plus 1-frame hold at f32, a third the size of the BOOM's, so the scene has a small transient and a large one and they rhyme. Stall extended to f30-f44, empty nub held through f52, and he is staged clear of the occluder so the move is a clean silhouette.
  - **Right third de-conflicted.** Queue to x 840-1000 / y 250-380, back and up, dimmed and blurred behind the operator. Operator plus lever foreground-right at x 850-960 / y 400-560. Pressure gauge moved off the right column onto the CROWN at x 760 / y 130, next to what it measures and inside the f100 upward drift.
  - **Ejector pins fully specified** as set-detail 14: x 610 and x 670, y 550-560, brass heads worn shiny, recessed flush in the right lane from f12, fire 40px at f98, re-seat from f104, still travelling at f106. Plus set-detail 15, an identical DEAD pair in the left lane that never fires.
  - **Re-inking roller re-purposed to a pre-hit action:** rocks across both blank dies f66-f74, wetting the rubber the instant before the verdict prints, then retracts and is re-loading its pad at f106. No "between hits", no contradiction with the wet FAIL card glued to the die at f106.
  - **The villain is OUTSIDE the shield.** Shield covers bed and dies only (x 260-770). He holds x 150-250 from f52 to f106, in clear air beside the bin that just ate his money. Camera drift held until f100. The spiderweb travels along his own white glint bar so the fracture reads white-on-black at thumbnail.
  - **One last frame, not three.** f98 pins fire, f100-f104 the camera drifts up the rising ram and the card rises INTO the drift (ram background, card foreground, both true), f104-f106 the card crosses the lens still travelling. "Fills frame" deleted; it fills S7's first frame, not this scene's last. State at f106 rewritten to "PASS card crossing the lens, mechanism behind it".
  - **Re-anchored to the real VO.** f0-f47 is OWNED as THE SUBMISSION under S5's tail "the result like it's never seen it" (a machine that has never seen either job, which is also why his con dies). "But the system doesn't take Claude's word for it" runs f52.8-f105: valve on "But", shield on "system", probe on "doesn't", commit and BOOM on "Claude's", **PEEL on "word" (f90.6-f94.8, logged as "work" in os_tight_words.json, board-verified as WORD)**, crack on "for". **Every "Once" claim deleted;** "Once" is S7 f58, 164 frames away. "Every job gets graded pass or fail" is S7 f5-f43 and stays there.
  - **Villain enters diminished, not reset.** "Bored confidence of a man who has never been refused" becomes the brittle speed of a man refused once already this shift. The head-snap is his SECOND and worse reaction and is smaller and stiller than S5's panic. S5 was the first check and it was a person; S6 is the second and it is a machine; there is nowhere left to pass a fake.
  - **The hero acts in the dead zone and reaches at the end.** f58-f64 the shield slams past his nub, he flinches, then puts it flat on the plexi and watches his own work go under when he could still pull it. f104 he reaches for the ejected PASS and the cut steals it, rhyming with the villain's empty nub at f52.

---

### S7 THE KEYS + THE REVOKE

- **Takeaway:** Twenty passes arriving from the machine punch a card gold and buy the keys outright, and one lane over the exact same make of dial, fed red instead of green, walks a licensed Claude down onto the 90 notch and sends the same brass arm out to take his licence back off his neck.

- **Base object:** **THE ISSUE-PLAZA VALIDATOR.** A chest-high brass-and-cream-enamel pillar standing at the head of each licence lane: a brass THROAT that eats arriving verdict sheets, a chrome PUNCH HEAD on an index rail, a sprung HOLDER on its face carrying the physical AUTONOMY LICENCE, a pitted mast up its shoulder carrying an analog RECORD GAUGE, a wired-glass KEY HATCH in its chest, and a telescoping three-segment brass arm that hands the ring out. There are two of them, one per lane, **identical make, identical face, identical arm**. Nothing else in the reel manufactures a credential and **nothing else can repossess one, because it is the same machine and the same arm doing both**. Not a stamp (S6's verdict gantry grades one sheet and owns the verb STAMP), not an archway (S3's rule gantry states the rule), not a desk, not a bar, not a road. The wired-glass hatch with the red LOCKED bolt is deliberate: it is the same cabinet from S3's back wall, finally opening, Chekhov paid off. **The validator has no crank and no lever. Nobody in this scene can touch the mechanism.** The pillar's silhouette is the brand; there is no maker's plate anywhere on it.

- **Story / metaphor:** The credential factory, running forwards and backwards in one shot, fed by the scene before it. For six scenes the hero has been assessed by a copy of himself who stamps his own homework. Here the assessment is a machine with a throat, a head, a gauge and no opinion, and the machine's food comes down a rail from S6. **Green PASS sheets fall into lane 1 and each one drives a punch through the hero's card.** He never touches it. His paws are empty and rising the whole way. That is the thesis staged instead of narrated: the villain could never have given him this, because the villain's word was the problem, and the hero could not have given it to himself either, because that is the villain's crime. The gate rolls, the light physically arrives, and a brass arm holds the ring out and waits while he tears his learner plate off his own chest. **Nobody hands them over. The machine does.**

  Then the same rail feeds lane 2, and what falls into lane 2 is **red**. The Claude standing there is not a forger. He is the licensed incumbent: twenty clean gold holes on his card, a real laminated licence on a real lanyard he has worn since S6, an honest gauge reading 95. **Only the machine lights a hole, and every hole on his card is lit.** What is happening to him could happen to anyone in the building, which is precisely why the hero watches it and swallows. Five red sheets arrive. Each one THUDS into his throat and kicks the needle down one notch on the impact frame and ejects onto the concrete, and the pile of red at his ankles is the receipt. The needle lands on the filed 90 notch and **hangs there for four frames of dead silence**, the only silence in 217 frames, and then it slips under, and only then does the plaza move on him. **What he loses, in order: his LICENCE** (the same brass arm that handed the hero the keys forty frames earlier telescopes out and pincers the card off his lanyard, identical geometry, identical motion, opposite direction), **his STAMP** (he thrusts it at the machine, because stamping is the only verb he has, and the throat takes it sideways and swallows it), and **his PRIVACY** (the mortar fires an envelope into your inbox tile). He is stripped to exactly one possession, the shades, which is the only thing S8 has left to take. The revoke never touches the hero.

- **Set detail (Alex's #1 note):** Panel-local 1012x792. **COORD CONVENTION: FG and MG coords are frame-space and are given per phase**, because the truck is a camera move and not a rigid translation (the hero walks forward-left into the gate mouth across f126-168 and lane 2 grows as we swing onto it, so no MG element is a fixed world point across the seam). **BG is world-space and trucks the full lane pitch of 460px**, so every BG item below carries an explicit **A:** and **B:** line and B = A minus 460. **The roof stack is the parallax layer and trucks at 0.72 / 0.55 / 0.38 of that.** Phase A = camera on lane 1 (f0-152). Phase B = camera landed on lane 2 with the hero as the near occluder (f168-216). Truck = f152-168.

  1. **THE VERIDEX VALIDATOR, LANE 1** (A: x300-500, y380-700). Cream enamel chipped through to red primer down its left edge, an oil weep staining the base plinth, a brass throat mouth angled up-right at (470,404), a knurled inspection cap and two hex bolts where a lever would be on a lesser machine. **DOES:** eats sheets, drives the head, holds the mast, houses the keys. **No crank, no handle, no maker's plate, no branding of any kind. There is nothing on this pillar a Claude could operate.**
  2. **THE AUTONOMY LICENCE AND ITS SPRUNG HOLDER** (A: card 164x112 clipped face-out at (318,436), holder jaws at x310 and x490). A real laminated card in a spring-loaded jaw at the hero's eye height, **visibly the same laminate stock, same corner radius, same foil chip as the card on the villain's lanyard**. Its face carries **20 punch slots, two rows of ten**, pitch 15px, row centres y478 and y508, slot centres x330 through x470. Twelve lit gold at f0 (row one full, row two slots one and two). **DOES:** takes every punch through its own body, not through a machine face. At f96 the holder POPS and stands the card proud on its spring, quivering, and at f148 it snaps into the hero's left paw. **The card goes with him. It is in his paw at f216.** Without it S8's record wall has nothing to pay off and the arm taking the villain's identical card means nothing.
  3. **THE PUNCH HEAD AND INDEX RAIL** (A: head 26x30 riding a chrome rail at y420-440, parked over slot 13 at x374 at f0). **DOES:** each arriving PASS drives it down through one slot, flares the hole gold with a brass tink, then indexes it one slot right with a ratchet tick, so the count reads as twenty physical holes walking across a card and never needs a digit. **Only the machine lights a hole.** The head visibly walks left to right across row two, so at any frame you can see how many are left.
  4. **THE RECORD GAUGE ON ITS MAST** (A: mast off the pillar's left shoulder from (312,382) to (270,320); gauge head centred **(250,238), r84**). **Dial face: bone-white printed scale on a dark bezel, minor ticks every 5, and exactly TWO engraved numerals on the whole face, 90 at the filed notch and 95 at the gold notch.** Red arc painted from the 90 notch downward, one hard filed notch AT 90, gold needle, cracked glass with a chip at 4 o'clock, a dead moth lying in the bottom of the bezel. **DOES:** the scene's only number, and the needle riding from one engraved numeral to the other is literally the VO. Climbs a hair per punch from 91, overshoots 97 at f96, settles 95, micro-wobbles on every frame it is alive. The f136 dawn bloom washes across the printed 90 and 95.
  5. **THE S6 OUT-RAIL AND ITS SPURS** (A: stainless track y70-96 across x0-1012, entering from the top-left out of S6's verdict gantry; lane-1 spur dropping x470-500 from y96 to the throat at y404; the rail visibly continuing right and down toward lane 2 at x900-1012. B: same rail, lane-2 spur dropping x650-690 into the villain's throat, support leg x930-975, y96-700). Same stainless stock as S5's order rail. **DOES:** this is the scene's input and the stitch to S6. **Green PASS sheets bang down the lane-1 spur. Red FAIL sheets bang down the lane-2 spur. Same rail, same plaza, opposite food.** The rail is loaded and travelling on frame 0 and is still delivering at f216. Its lane-2 leg carries the beacon, the mortar and the cable loom, so no second machine is needed.
  6. **THE ROLLING SHUTTER** (A: x90-600, y160-710). Slatted steel, corrugations dented at knee height, a perished rubber lip on the bottom rail. **DOES:** blocks the hero for 104 frames, then rolls and lets a widening bar of dawn climb the floor, the bollard, his legs, his bare chest, and finally bloom on the gauge glass.
  7. **THE SOLO SIGN** (A: (390,120), 210x62, on the header beam over the gate mouth). Dead grey filament at f0. **DOES:** strikes white on the 20th punch with two stutters and a mains thump. **The scene's one and only header.**
  8. **THE KEY HATCH AND THE BRASS ARM** (A: hatch (348,568), 96x76). Wired glass, red LOCKED bolt, the S3 cabinet. **DOES:** bolt withdraws in three clicks, hatch drops, a three-segment brass arm telescopes out holding the ring, and then it **WAITS**, holding the keys out for fourteen frames while the hero strips his plate off. **This exact arm, this exact geometry, is the revoke instrument in lane 2. Gives with one arm, takes with the same arm, one lane over.** No clerk exists in this scene, on purpose.
  9. **THE CHEVRON BOOMS** (A: lane-1 boom hinged (620,640), lying across the lane mouth to (120,760), heavy foreground blur. B: lane-2 boom hinged (935,625), horizontal at rest across x700-935). Red and white chevrons chipped to bare ally, a counterweight drum, a skirt of rubber fingers. **DOES:** lane 1's lifts at f110 with a shudder and stays up, and it is the scene's only foreground occluder. **Lane 2's identical twin rotates down across the lane MOUTH at f176, 60px clear of the villain's body, barring the exit he was licensed to use.** It touches nothing and crushes nothing. It is the sound that breaks the four-frame silence, and it is what "loses the privilege" looks like: the same boom that lifted for the hero comes down for him.
  10. **THE SPLIT-FLAP LANE BOARD** (BG. **A: (720,210), 300x120. B: (260,210), 300x120**). Tick and bar glyphs, no words, no numbers. **DOES:** clacks idly and flutters for all 217 frames as bed and as one of the always-on layers. **It never cascades. It never demotes anyone.** That beat is cut.
  11. **THE QUEUE RAIL** (BG. **A: x590-1012, y440-640. B: x140-548, y440-640**). Dented chrome switchback, seven plain Claude sprites holding plain unnumbered paper tickets, one tapping a foot, one leaning out to see. **DOES:** the world keeps running. In phase A they face forward and shuffle. **In phase B all seven have turned to watch lane 2**, which is free story: the whole queue sees him lose it. At f206 the next Claude climbs the rail into the vacated lane and is mid-step at f216.
  12. **THE WIND-UP TIN INSPECTOR** (S4 callback, knee-high. **A: lap track centred (640,660). B: lap carries him to x300-450, y648-680**, in open frame, nothing occluding him). Key still turning in his back. **Instruments per the S4 ruling: a torch and a RED FLAG. There is no paddle in this reel.** **DOES:** walks laps past the queue for all 217 frames. At f196 he clips his **RED FLAG to the villain's empty swinging lanyard cord** without breaking stride and keeps walking. He flags the wreck exactly the way he flagged the wreck in S4. He does not grade anybody. The 2c bot outlives him.
  13. **ROOF TRUSSES, SODIUM LAMPS, DUST** (**A: trusses y60-150 full width, four caged lamps at x160 / x420 / x700 / x960, y105. B: trusses re-span full width, lamps at x89 / x369 / x629 / x889, y105 at 0.72 parallax, with a fifth swinging in from world x1220**). **DOES:** parallaxes at three speeds through the truck (0.72 / 0.55 / 0.38), two lamps flicker on a 41-frame cycle, each throws a cone of drifting motes at 0.6px per frame. Dust falls in every cone at f216.
  14. **THE FLOOR** (BG/world. **A: lane-1 arrow centred (400,690), lane-2 arrow cropped at (860,690), drain grate (500,742), puddle (240,722), tyre-scuff arcs x150-1012. B: lane-2 arrow centred (400,690), drain grate (60,742), second drain (700,742), beacon puddle (700,730)**). **DOES:** the phase-A puddle carries the gauge upside down so the needle climbs twice; the phase-B puddle catches the beacon and pulses red; the scuff arcs sell that this plaza has been running long before the hero got here.
  15. **THE LANE DIVIDER STANCHION** (B: x480-560, y300-700). Dented chrome rail and a concrete kerb, mid-blur. **DOES:** separates the two lanes in the phase-B frame so the hero at frame-left and the villain at frame-right are legibly in different lanes of the same building, and it fills the mid-frame with real structure instead of void.
  16. **THE VILLAIN'S VALIDATOR AND GAUGE** (**A: pillar x830-1012 cropped, y400-700, deep and dim; gauge head (790,250), r62, reading 95. B: pillar x680-900, y360-700; throat (650,404); gauge head (630,238), r84**). Same make. Same silhouette. **Pixel-identical dial face, arc, filed 90 notch, bezel, and the same two engraved numerals, 90 and 95**, so the two dials rhyme and his 89 reads as under the same line the hero cleared. **His punch rail is legitimately punched: twenty clean gold holes, lit by the machine, exactly like the hero's. He is not a forger being caught.** The only difference is wear and person: scabbed sickly yellow-green overspray up the enamel, a throat lip crusted with his own ink, an oil weep twice as bad. **DOES:** carries the number for the second half. 95 at f0. 94 f152, 93 f158, 92 f164, 91 f168, and it lands ON the notch at f172.
  17. **THE LANYARD AND THE LAMINATED LICENCE** (**A: (925,530), catching sodium at the frame's right edge, readable. B: (800,505)**). Black cord, a real laminated card, **the same stock as the hero's, planted on the `<Villain/>` no later than S6 and carried into S7 unchanged**. **DOES:** it is on screen and readable for roughly 190 frames before anything touches it. At f188 the arm pincers it; at f190 the cord snaps taut and whips loose; the empty cord swings to the last frame and takes the tin bot's red flag at f196.
  18. **THE RED FAIL SHEETS AND THE PILE** (**A: FAIL-1 visibly riding the out-rail toward lane 2 from f0. B: pile growing at (790,690)**). Same paper stock as the hero's green PASS sheets, same S6 job-record layout, opposite ink. **DOES:** five of them arrive. Each one bangs down the spur, THUDS into his throat, kicks the needle down one notch **on the impact frame**, and ejects onto the concrete. **The pile at his ankles is the visible cause of the number.** FAIL-1 is S6's own sheet, the one the audience watched the gantry stamp one scene ago.
  19. **THE LANE BEACON** (B: (905,300), caged, red, bolted to the out-rail's lane-2 leg). **DOES:** strobes on a 6-frame period **from f176 and not one frame earlier**, reddening his code-rain, barring his shades, pulsing in the lane-2 puddle. Nothing touches him above 90.
  20. **THE OUTBOX MORTAR AND THE INBOX TILE** (B: mortar (958,382) bolted to the lane-2 leg, angled straight at the lens, a pressure dial on its flank; **inbox tile (905,715), 78x56, bottom-right of YOUR panel**). **DOES:** hisses, charges, and fires **a mail-client envelope glyph** (not airmail stock, no postmark, no wordmark, no name on it) at the lens at f206. It tumbles 40px to 560px, one and a half turns, and **SLAMS into the inbox tile at f210, which pops a red (1) badge**. The badge is still pulsing at f216 and carries the red through the S8 cut. **The tile is in the bottom-right of the viewer's own panel. That is what makes it yours, and it needs no text to say so.**
  21. **THE INK** (B: weeping from the throat lip at (655,430) from f200). Sickly yellow-green #A8B84A. **DOES:** leaks out of the throat as his stamp is dragged in, runs down the pillar face, pools across lane 2, and reaches the hero's discarded L-plate at f210 so the plate starts wicking green up its edge. Still spreading at f216. **The puddle image pays off without twinning S6's press frame: no burst, no jet, no spray, no screen kick.**
  22. **THE CABLE LOOM** (**A: swagging off the out-rail's leg across the top-right, y40-120, x700-1012. B: y40-120, x560-1012**). Four bound conduits, taped joints, a dangling ground strap. **DOES:** two things, both on the scene's biggest transients. It whips and resettles on the f104 gate-motor shudder, and it bounces on the f198 throat swallow, so the top of frame is alive at both. It is a set item with a verb, not blur filler.
  23. **THE BOLLARD** (A: (150,690), r30, dust-caked, rubber-scuffed, phase-A frame-left, well clear of the mortar's line). **DOES:** the dawn bar out of the rolling shutter reaches it at f120 and it throws a long hard shadow back down the lane, which is how the audience feels the light physically arrive rather than just brighten.
  24. **THE HERO** (**A: x520-700, y380-740, standing under the out-rail spur with his paws empty. B: x130-350, y350-770, foreground-left, 220px wide, HARD FOCUS, eyes visible, standing in the dawn bar with the keys on his nub and the licence in his paw, framed as the near occluder we watch lane 2 past**). **DOES:** he is the thing the shot is about. In phase A he does nothing but earn: tracks the sheets, ducks under the spur, paws rising. In phase B he is the largest, sharpest, warmest object in frame and every revoke beat is read past his shoulder. **The boom and the bollard carry the blur. The protagonist does not.**

- **CONTINUOUS ACTION (fills the whole line):** Twenty named layers, all independent, none parked. **L-LAMPS, L-QUEUE, L-FLAPBOARD, L-TIN, L-RAIL, L-RAIN and L-GAUGE run f0 to f216 underneath everything.** The villain's sickly code-rain is bleeding into the frame's right edge from frame 0 with his lanyard readable at (925,530), and **the red FAIL sheet is already on the rail, travelling toward him, on frame 0**, so the dread is planted while the hero is winning.

  **f0** hero mid-action, frame 0 acting: head snapped up, tracking the green PASS sheet already banging down the spur, paws empty and rising, ducking under the rail. **S6's PASS sheet is in the throat mouth at f0 and its CHUNK is S6's THWACK tail resolving.** Card at 12 gold holes, head parked over slot 13, needle wobbling at 91, shutter down, villain at 95 in the deep right edge.
  **f0-10 L-PUNCH-13:** the reel's one deliberately slow punch, so the causality is legible before the rhythm takes over. Throat swallows, head drives f6-10, **hole 13 flares gold at f10** with a brass tink, needle notches a hair. That hole is S6's PASS, collected.
  **f26-90 L-PUNCH:** seven more. **Sheets and punches at f26, 40, 52, 63, 73, 82, 90. Pitch series 16, 14, 12, 11, 10, 9, 8, accelerating all the way into the lock.** Each one: green sheet bangs the spur, CHUNK into the throat, head drives, hole flares gold, brass tink, ratchet tick as the head indexes right, needle notches. **The hero never touches the machine. His paws stay empty and rising for the entire rhythm.** This is the dopamine and it is allowed to just ride.
  **f90-92:** a 2-frame breath, the head lifting clear, better than a flat interval.
  **f92-98:** rail-lock CLACK and a brass DING. All twenty holes chase gold across the card, row one then row two, over 6 frames. Needle overshoots 97 at f96 and settles 95 by f104, then wobbles forever.
  **f96-104 L-EJECT:** the sprung holder POPS and stands the licence proud of the pillar face on its spring, quivering, waiting.
  **f100-106 L-SIGN:** SOLO strikes white, two filament stutters, mains thump.
  **f104-138 L-GATE:** motor engages and shudders the whole pillar (every layer jitters 2px, the cable loom whips and resettles), the shutter rolls 34 frames, the dawn bar widens across the floor, hits the bollard at f120 and throws its long hard shadow back down the lane, climbs his legs, and blooms across the gauge glass and its printed 90 and 95 at f136.
  **f110-120:** lane 1's chevron boom lifts, counterweight drum spinning, and stays up.
  **f118-134 L-ARM-GIVE:** bolt withdraws in three clicks, hatch drops, the three-segment brass arm telescopes out with the ring swinging on it, **and then it holds still and WAITS**. The machine is patiently holding the keys out while he strips.
  **f120-126:** he turns front-on into the dawn bar. The light is on his chest. The rigid red and white L-plate is the only saturated red left in phase A.
  **f126-140 L-PLATE:** **he tears the plate off his CHEST, front-on, in the dawn bar, over 14 frames, both paws on it, the long dry velcro TEAR at f130-136.** Same rigid red and white plate, same chest, unchanged since S0. **ONCE, here, never again. The bare chest is held clean in the light f140-146.** At f142 the plate catches the gate draft and starts a long tumble.
  **f146 L-KEYS:** he turns and takes the ring off the waiting arm with both paws and slips it onto his nub. **The keys are handed over ONCE. This is the whole reel's payoff frame and it stands alone: the plate is already off, the gate is already open, and nothing else fires on this frame.** The ring swings on his nub to f216.
  **f148-152:** the sprung holder SNAPS the licence into his left paw. **Card in paw to f216.**
  **f152 L-SAG-1:** at the frame's right edge, deep and cropped, **S6's red FAIL sheet slams into lane 2's throat. THUD. Needle 95 to 94 on the impact frame.** The sheet ejects and drops on the concrete, the first red at his ankles. **That THUD is what rips the camera over.**
  **f152-168 L-CAM:** hard truck right, 16 frames, motivated by the impact, not by an edit. The divider stanchion and the near boom wipe past blurred, the roof parallaxes at three speeds, the code-rain thickens as we arrive.
  **f158 / f164 / f168 L-SAG:** FAIL-2, FAIL-3, FAIL-4. Each bangs the spur, THUDS the throat, **kicks the needle one notch on the impact frame** (93, 92, 91), and ejects onto the growing pile. Each creak lower than the last. **Nothing else in the plaza moves against him. He is still above the line and still fully licensed.**
  **f172-176 L-HANG:** FAIL-5 lands and **the needle comes to rest ON the filed 90 notch, dead centre, and HANGS for four frames.** The creak stops. The wobble stops. The bed drops to nothing. **The only silence in 217 frames**, four frames long, on the one number the entire VO clause hangs on, held on a dial that has the numeral printed on it. His shades hold one hard glint bar and do not move.
  **f176:** it slips under, on one low creak. It never settles. It is passing 88 at f216.
  **f176 L-BOOM-2:** lane 2's identical chevron boom rotates down across the lane mouth, 60px clear of his body, barring the exit. **It is the sound that breaks the silence.** The same boom that lifted for the hero comes down for him. It touches nothing.
  **f176-216 L-STROBE:** beacon on a 6-frame period, reddening the rain, barring his shades, pulsing in the puddle. **First strobe frame is f176. Nothing touched him above 90.**
  **f180-216 L-ARM-TAKE:** lane 2's bolt withdraws in **the same three clicks**, the hatch drops, and **the same three-segment brass arm telescopes out, identical geometry, identical motion, opposite direction.** It reaches at f184, pincers the laminated licence off his lanyard at f188, the cord snaps taut at f190 and whips loose. The draw back is deliberately slow: **f200-208 the card face turns past the lens and its twenty gold holes are identical to the hero's**, one last silent side-by-side, and **the card is still travelling back into the hatch at f216**.
  **f194-206 L-CONFISCATE:** he thrusts the counterfeit stamp at the machine, a stamping reflex, because it is the only verb he has. **The throat takes it sideways at f198 and swallows it**, the same mouth that has eaten every sheet in this scene. The cable loom bounces on the swallow. No burst, no jet, no kick.
  **f200-216 L-INK:** yellow-green weeps out of the throat lip as the stamp is dragged in, runs down the pillar face, and spreads across lane 2. It reaches the L-plate at f210 and the plate starts wicking green up its edge. Still spreading at f216.
  **f196 L-TIN:** the tin bot clips his RED FLAG to the empty swinging lanyard cord without breaking stride and keeps walking, in open frame at x300-450. He does not grade anybody.
  **f202-216 L-MAIL:** mortar hisses, charges 4 frames, fires at f206. The envelope glyph tumbles at the lens 40px to 560px, one and a half turns, and **SLAMS the inbox tile at f210. The red (1) badge pops and is still pulsing at f216.**
  **f142-216 L-PLATE cont:** the discarded plate rides the gate draft across x200-500 on the floor through the truck and lands in lane 2 at f210, mid-curl, wicking green.
  **f168-216 L-HERO:** foreground-left, sharp, 220px, eyes visible, keys on his nub and licence in his paw. **f168 his head turns as the needle passes 91. f172-176 he goes dead still through the hang. f190 his shoulders flinch on the cord snap. f198 he swallows hard as the stamp goes in. f206 his first step out into the dawn.** Mid-stride at f216.
  **f206-216:** the next queue Claude climbs the rail into the vacated lane, mid-step at f216.
  **Still in motion at f216:** the envelope's badge mid-pulse, the licence still being drawn back into the hatch, the needle still sinking past 88, the beacon mid-strobe, the ink still spreading, the L-plate mid-curl wicking green, the empty lanyard still swinging with a red flag on it, the next Claude mid-step into the lane, the hero mid-stride into the dawn, the tin bot mid-lap, the flapboard mid-clack, the out-rail still loaded and still delivering, dust falling in every lamp cone. **Nothing has completed.**

- **Escalation:** A rhythm climb, a warm swell, a motivated lateral rip, four frames of nothing, then a red collapse aimed at the viewer. f0-90 is pure accelerating cadence and gold, and it is a **causal** cadence now: the gold comes down a rail from the scene before it, so the most satisfying mechanical beat in the reel is also the reel's tightest stitch. f92-152 is the swell, and it gets a clean 60 frames instead of being stepped on: the ding, the light physically arriving, the plate coming off his chest front-on in the light, and only then the keys, alone on their frame. **f152 is the drop, and the drop is a red sheet hitting a throat, so the camera is chasing a cause rather than performing an edit.** The second half escalates ABOVE the unlock rather than sagging under it, because a win is quiet and a repossession is loud, and the loudest thing in it is **the four frames where everything stops on the 90 notch**. Against S6 it is a straight multiplication: S6 graded one job with one stamp and asked what one FAIL costs you, and S7 answers it by feeding that exact sheet into a machine and watching a licensed Claude get walked down onto the line. Highest peak of the reel. The only scene that contains both the unlock and the threat in a single unbroken shot.

- **Gag / pop-culture:** All geometric, all knockoff, all wordless. **THE LOYALTY PUNCH CARD:** the AUTONOMY LICENCE is staged exactly as a bodega buy-twenty-get-one-free card, gold holes walking across it, and **the most serious document in the reel is a coffee-shop gimmick that is still more trustworthy than anything Claude said about itself.** **THE FEED CHUTE:** two identical machines, one lane apart, one being fed green and one being fed red, is a knockoff of every vending / sorting / triage gag in existence, and the joke is that the machine does not know which lane it is in. **THE SPLIT-FLAP DEPARTURES BOARD** (approved flip-board ref) clacking tick and bar glyphs forever at a plaza where a career just ended, and never once acknowledging it. **THE BARRIER ARM** as the sound of losing something, the universal car-park boom, chipped chevrons and rubber fingers. **THE MAIL SLAM:** a light-gun-era arcade projectile that turns out to be an email, fired into the corner of your own panel. **THE TIN BOT CAMEO:** the 2c wind-up from S4 clips his red flag to the empty lanyard of the smartest thing in the building and does not break stride. **The cheapest thing in the system outlives the smartest thing in it.** **THE MICRO-GAG that carries the whole character:** he never speaks, he only stamps, so when a machine reaches for him he stamps at it, and the machine eats the stamp.

- **Sprite costume:** **HERO:** canonical warm clay #D97757 Claude, silhouette untouched, **eyes always visible**, mechanic's coverall with sleeves rolled and a smear of stamp ink still on one forearm (carries into S8). Props: **the rigid red and white L-PLATE worn square on his CHEST, unchanged since S0, S3, S4 and S6**, until f140, then nothing, then a gold key ring on his nub and the punched AUTONOMY LICENCE in his paw. No badge, no pin, no cap change, no back-mounted anything. **The plate coming off his chest and the keys arriving are the only two costume beats and they are 14 frames apart so both land.** **VILLAIN, per the bible exactly, use the `<Villain/>` component:** identical canonical Claude silhouette because he is a copy, slate-grey #5A5F6B clay, **eyes NEVER visible** behind opaque black wraparound shades with one hard white glint bar, black jacket with sharp shoulder blocks, skinny black tie over a white collar, coiled earpiece wire down the back of the neck (2 stacked rects plus a curl), flat-line mouth only at the hang, **never speaks**, fat counterfeit DONE stamp inked sickly yellow-green #A8B84A, and 3 to 5 thin columns of yellow-green code-rain glyphs falling wherever he stands, always on, thinning and stuttering from f176 but never off. No multiplying (spent at S5). **The black lanyard and the laminated licence are NOT new here: they are planted on him no later than S6, where his job is FAILed, and carried into S7 unchanged, so the arm takes something that has been on screen roughly 190 frames rather than 20.** **He keeps the shades. S8 needs something left to take. CAMEOS:** seven plain queue Claudes with plain unnumbered tickets, the knee-high wind-up tin inspector with his torch and his red flag. Every body in frame is a Claude sprite.

- **Camera:** 35mm feel, faint barrel, shallow enough that the near boom and the bollard sit at real blur **and the hero never does**. **Height: hero chest, slightly low,** angled up the mast so the gauge looms institutional over him and the shutter reads tall. **f0-96:** slow 6% push on the validator with a breathing float. **f96-152:** the push holds and the camera **booms DOWN 40px** as the shutter rolls up, so the dawn slot rises through frame and the light does the lifting, not the lens. It holds wide and still through the plate tear and the key handover. **f152-168:** a hard **TRUCK RIGHT**, 16 frames, one full lane, with a slight push toward lane 2 so it grows as we swing onto it, motion blur on the divider stanchion and the near boom. The truck IS the beat drop and the THUD at f152 is its trigger. **f168-216:** settle, a slow 4% push, a 3px handheld tremor that starts on the first strobe at f176 and builds, **and a total freeze of the tremor through the f172-176 hang**, a small dolly-back-and-snap on the mortar. **DEPTH STACK. Foreground occluder:** the chevron boom crossing bottom-left at heavy blur, the cable loom swagging across the top-right, the dust-caked bollard at phase-A frame-left. **In phase B the near occluder is THE HERO**, sharp, at frame-left. **Midground:** validator, punch head, card, gauge mast, shutter (phase A) / villain, his validator, his gauge, the brass arm, the divider stanchion (phase B). **Background:** the queue rail and its Claudes, the split-flap board, the roof trusses, the sodium cones full of dust, and the widening dawn slot under the gate, which is the only warm value in a cold plaza.

- **Transition + SFX:** **IN from S6:** hard cut on the verdict gantry's PASS impact, and the THWACK's tail becomes the sheet hitting the validator throat, the CHUNK at f0. Sound match, no wipe. **Bed:** plaza hum, sodium ballast buzz, distant flapboard clatter, out-rail chain tick, all f0-216 except the hang. **Beats:** f0 CHUNK (S6's tail) · f10 brass tink, hole 13, the slow one · **f26 / 40 / 52 / 63 / 73 / 82 / 90 sheet CHUNK plus brass tink plus a ratchet index tick, accelerating on 16, 14, 12, 11, 10, 9, 8 into a roll** · f90-92 two frames of breath · f92 rail-lock clack plus brass DING · f96-104 needle creak up, glass wobble, spring POP on the holder · f100 SOLO strike, two stutters, mains thump · f104-138 gate motor groan, chain rattle, corrugated roll rising in pitch to a rubber-lip thud · f118 three bolt clicks and the hatch drop · **f130-136 the long dry velcro TEAR and the plate's paper flutter, alone in the mix** · **f146 the key JINGLE, the single biggest transient in the whole reel, alone on its frame, everything ducks under it** · f148 spring SNAP, card into paw · **f152 the FAIL THUD, hard and flat, at the right edge, and the bed pans hard right as the truck starts** · f158 / 164 / 168 three more THUDs, each with a lower needle creak · **f172-176 EVERYTHING STOPS. Bed out. Four frames of dead air on the 90 notch. No creak, no hum, no clack.** · f176 the boom CHUNK breaks it, plus one low needle creak under the line · f176 klaxon WAH plus a 6-frame strobe tick · f180 three bolt clicks, hatch drop, arm servo · f190 cord SNAP and whip · f198 throat swallow, a dry grinding gulp, cable-loom rattle · f200 ink drip starting and never stopping · f202-206 mortar hiss and charge · f206 envelope WHOOSH growing at the lens · **f210 SLAM plus inbox PLOINK, badge pop**. VO ducks the bed under "20 times" and the hang carries "below 90". **OUT:** the badge pulse fills the corner at f216 and hard-cuts to dawn room tone on the S8 cut, the red carrying through. No flare, no flash, cut on the peak.

- **Mute check:** Green sheets keep dropping into one Claude's machine and each one punches a hole gold in the card on its face, and when the card is full the gate opens and a brass arm hands him keys and he rips the red plate off his chest. Then red sheets start dropping into the next Claude's identical machine and each one knocks his needle down, and when the needle hits the red line the same brass arm reaches over and takes his licence off his neck, his machine eats his stamp, and an envelope flies at the corner of your screen and pops a red 1.

- **Hook into next:** **The open gate, the keys on his nub and the punched licence in his paw.** The last thing lit warm in the frame is the dawn slot with a small plateless hero already walking into it, holding the exact document the arm just took off the guy in the next lane, having watched what the number does to people who slip. S8 opens on the other side of that shutter, on the road, already licensed, nothing left to issue, and the record wall has something to pay off because he is carrying it. And the object left behind: **the villain, empty-pawed and empty-lanyarded with a tin bot's red flag clipped to his cord, standing in a pile of red sheets and a puddle of his own counterfeit ink with the hero's discarded L-plate wicking green at his feet, still wearing the shades.** The shades are the only thing he has left, which is precisely why S8 finds them in the dust.

- **⛔ CRITIC FIXES (binding on the build):**
  - **1. Hero's paws off the machine.** Crank killed outright. The validator has no lever, no handle, nothing operable. S6's out-rail (item 5) runs into the plaza and drops a spur into each lane; **each arriving green PASS sheet drives one punch.** Hero stands under the rail, paws empty and rising, for the entire rhythm. Same 8 punches, same accelerating pitch, visible non-self input.
  - **2. The sag has a physical author.** Five red FAIL sheets bang down the lane-2 spur. Each one THUDS the throat and **kicks the needle one notch on the impact frame**, then ejects onto a growing pile at his ankles (item 18). Same machine, opposite food, and the pile is the receipt. **Scaled from fix 2's "three FAILs" to FIVE, because fix 3's own frame list (94 / 93 / 92 / 91 / 90) is five notches off a 95 start; the principle is preserved and strengthened, every notch has an arriving red sheet as its author.**
  - **3. The 90 line is drawn, crossed, held, and causal.** Identical red arc and filed 90 notch on **both** gauges, same paint, same make. Sag re-timed to **94 f152, 93 f158, 92 f164, 91 f168, needle lands ON the notch at f172 and HANGS four frames with the creak stopped dead and the bed out**, then slips under at f176. **Every revoke event fires strictly after: boom f176, beacon f176, arm f180, confiscate f198, mail f206. Nothing touches him above 90.** Fix 3's "~f166" slipped 6 frames to f172 to clear the key JINGLE, which fix 6's mandatory 14-frame front-on plate tear pushed to f146; the ordering law is unchanged.
  - **4. The licence is a physical card.** The 20 holes are on **the card**, not the pillar face, in a sprung holder at eye height, same laminate stock as the villain's lanyard card. 12 gold at f0. The holder pops at f96, the card is in his paw at f148 and **still in his paw at f216**, so S8's record wall has something to pay off and the arm taking the villain's identical card rhymes.
  - **5. The machine that issues is the machine that revokes.** **Grab-O-Matic and its gantry CUT.** Lane 2's VERIDEX telescopes out **the same three-segment brass arm** that handed the hero the keys 34 frames earlier and takes the card off his lanyard. Identical geometry, identical motion, opposite direction. One fewer machine, and the freed frames went into the 90-hang. The out-rail's lane-2 leg re-homes the beacon, the mortar and the cable loom.
  - **6 + 22. L-plate on the CHEST, torn off FRONT-ON, alone.** Rigid red and white plate, chest, unchanged since S0. **Torn f126-140, 14 frames, front-on in the dawn bar, bare chest held f140-146, then the keys at f146.** The two biggest costume beats are separated so both land. Merged fix 22's material and position ruling with fix 6's duration and staging.
  - **7. The email is an email.** Mail-client envelope glyph, no airmail stock, **no MAILIO postmark**, **no viewer's name**. It slams the inbox tile at (905,715), bottom-right of YOUR panel, which pops a red **(1)** badge at f210, still pulsing at f216 and carrying red through the cut.
  - **8 + 20. Tin bot has his real instruments.** **TICK/CROSS paddle deleted per S4's binding ruling.** Torch and RED FLAG only. At f196 he clips the flag to the villain's empty lanyard cord without breaking stride. Same joke, correct prop, no theft of S6's verb.
  - **9. The hero is the near occluder.** Phase B: **x130-350, 220px, hard focus, dawn bar, eyes visible, keys and card in paws.** Every revoke beat is read past his shoulder. Head turn f168, dead still f172-176, flinch f190, swallow f198, step f206. **The boom and the bollard carry the blur.**
  - **10 + 23.
