Text and number floor held.** **SOLO is the only header.** VERIDEX MK-IV maker's plate **cut** (the pillar's silhouette is the brand, and the two lanes being visibly the same make is what carries it). MAILIO **cut**. Ticket 21's printed number **stripped**. **PRIZE EVERY TIME cut**, resolving fix 23 against fixes 5 and 10: it had no host once the Grab-O-Matic died, and a rigged carnival crane is the iconography of a scam in the one scene that must sell an unconnable system. Two gauges permitted **only** as pixel-identical faces reading one value; the (1) badge is inside the locked spine.
  - **11. Phase B cut from four losses to three.** **Flapboard cascade DELETED.** The flapboard clacks as bed for all 217 frames and never demotes anyone. LICENCE (arm), STAMP (throat), PRIVACY (mortar). Shades survive for S8. The ~10 frames bought went into the 90-hang.
  - **12. Coord convention stated; phase B filled.** Items 10, 11, 12, 13 and 14 carry explicit **A:** and **B:** lines. BG is world-space and trucks the full 460; roof parallaxes at 0.72 / 0.55 / 0.38; FG/MG is composed per phase. x130-560 in phase B is now hero (130-350), flapboard (260,210), queue rail (140-548, all seven turned to watch lane 2), tin bot lap (300-450), L-plate tumble (200-500), divider stanchion (480-560). Nothing named sits left of x130.
  - **13. Both foreground decoratives promoted with verbs.** **CABLE LOOM** = item 22, whips on the f104 gate shudder, bounces on the f198 swallow. **BOLLARD** = item 23, moved to phase-A frame-left at (150,690), off the mortar's line, throws the dawn shadow at f120.
  - **14. The dial has the numbers on it.** Bone-white printed scale, dark bezel, minor ticks every 5, **exactly two engraved numerals per face, 90 at the filed notch and 95 at the gold notch**, mirrored pixel-identical on both gauges. The f136 bloom now washes across them.
  - **15 + 18. The credential is real, and the threat with it.** **Resolved in favour of fix 18: the self-punched ragged rail is CUT ENTIRELY.** The villain's rail is legitimately punched, twenty clean gold holes, same make, same machine, real laminated licence. He is the licensed incumbent whose number went, not a forger being caught, which is the only staging the conditional VO and the "it can happen to him" ruling permit. Fix 15's underlying law is kept and stated on item 3: **only the machine lights a hole**, so nothing on frame is forgeable. Fix 15's side-by-side beat is kept and improved: at f200-208 his card turns past the lens and its gold holes are **identical** to the hero's.
  - **16. Punch rhythm accelerates into the lock.** Frames **f10, 26, 40, 52, 63, 73, 82, 90**, pitch **16, 14, 12, 11, 10, 9, 8**. Frame list and pitch list now agree. f90-92 is a 2-frame breath, then the rail-lock clack and DING at f92. **The SFX map inherits f90, not f92.**
  - **17. S6's verdicts are collected on both sides.** Hero: S6's green PASS **is punch 13, landing at f10**, paying off the f0 CHUNK. Villain: **S6's own red FAIL sheet is FAIL-1**, visible on the rail from f0, landing at f152, and it is the impact that motivates the truck. S6 asks what one FAIL costs you and S7 answers it.
  - **19. The lanyard is planted upstream.** Black cord and laminated licence go on the `<Villain/>` **no later than S6** and carry in unchanged. He is in lane 2 in the deep background from f0 at (925,530) with the lanyard readable and the code-rain bleeding into the right edge. The arm takes something ~190 frames old.
  - **21. One reach, and the stamp is CONFISCATED, not crushed.** The brass arm is the only thing that reaches for him. **No claw, no boom-crush, no burst, no ink jet, no screen kick.** He thrusts the stamp at the machine and **the throat takes it sideways and swallows it**; the ink weeps out of the throat lip from f200 and reaches the L-plate at f210, so the puddle image pays without twinning S6's press frame. Lane 2's chevron boom is kept and read strictly as a **lateral barrier**: it rotates across the lane mouth 60px clear of his body at f176, touches nothing, breaks the four-frame silence, and is the same boom that lifted for the hero.

---

### S8 THE OPEN ROAD

- **Takeaway:** He is not driving alone because anyone decided to believe in him. He is driving alone because a wall of counters flips over behind him every 90 pixels of road, almost all of them green, and the one number they add up to sits above the line. Nobody guessed. The wall counted.

- **Base object:** **THE RECORD WALL**, a roadside tally rail on the **NEAR / DRIVER side of the road (frame-left)**, running the full length of the open road and receding to the horizon. Waist-high concrete posts every 90px, one post per completed job, each with a **rolling brass odometer drum** in a cast housing and a **result cap** on top. **Its verb is COUNT, and it counts HIM:** every post is dark and blank until his nose reaches it, then the drum **flips over** on the exact frame he passes it, one brass chock per post, and the cap lights. The physical chain reads with zero words: he drives, the wall counts him, the number is his. **The wall IS the graph:** a passed job stands **tall with a green cap**, a failed job is a **short post with a red cap that sits under the line**, and roughly **one in twenty is red**. Because the rail recedes to the horizon, forty to sixty posts are in frame at any moment, so the read at speed is instant and wordless: an overwhelming green comb with rare red gaps in it. **Palette ruling:** the green caps carry the S6 press's authority green `#3F9E74` deliberately and legally, because each cap **IS** an S6 PASS. Same green, same meaning, same authority. That honours the reserved-green rule rather than breaking it, and it is why the reel's colour spine closes here. Distinct by mechanism, silhouette and sound from every other scene's object: S7's credential machine is a punch rail plus a laminator throat plus a wall-mounted **needle** gauge at a counter, static, indoors, one card. S8's is a horizontal rail of **flipping drums and lighting caps** at speed, outdoors, no card, no needle, no counter, nothing issued. Nothing else in the reel flips a counter. Two deliberate returns that are NOT new base objects and must be labelled as returns in the build: (1) **the car is S2's learner car**, the reel's only car per the clarity mandate, now silently plateless, (2) **S2's chrome instructor brake pedal** returns as a corpse, unbolted and hanging, a trophy not a hero prop.
  ⛔ Do NOT build: keys, a badge, an L-plate, a plate mount, bolt holes, or a clean unfaded rectangle where a plate was. All spent at S7. He is plateless the way a licensed driver is plateless: silently, and nobody points at it.
  ⛔ **DEPENDENCY (binding, S7):** the instructor brake pedal must still be **bolted in and functional at the end of S7**. S3 may threaten it, **S7 may not touch it.** Strike the punt from S7's Story, Escalation, CONTINUOUS ACTION (f105 to f150) and Gag lines, not just its critic footnote, and strike "confiscate the brake" from S3's aim. S2's hook reads "a prop the arc will eventually unbolt", not "S3 can physically take". **S8 owns the pedal's death, exclusively, and the hero discovers it dead, once, at f30.**
  ⛔ **DEPENDENCY (binding, S2):** the bottom-left windscreen DONE sticker is **placed in S2** (the villain leans forward over the hero's shoulder mid-stomp and slaps a crooked `#A8B84A` DONE sticker onto the **inside** of the glass, dead in the hero's eyeline, while the trip readout says `0.0 m`) and it must **survive on the glass through S3 to S7**, sun-faded and half-peeled, never mentioned, never pointed at. S8 is where the hero finally takes it off himself.

- **Story / metaphor:** The thesis, performed as **subtraction**, with exactly one thing added: the count. S7 was the ceremony. S8 is the morning after, and it is quiet on purpose. The whole scene is one continuous act of removal: the boom is already cleared, the passenger seat is empty, the brake pedal is unbolted and swinging by one wire, the villain's kit is in a skip, the last counterfeit comes off the glass, the floodlights go out, and the only things left in frame at the end are the road, the numbers and him. **What the villain loses: existence, and his last issued mark.** He is not defeated here, he was destroyed at S7. Here he is simply disposed of: the authority took his kit, flattened his gun and threw it out with the bins, and the boom-side **PROPERTY skip** is the sentence made physical. He cannot come back, because killing him never produced a better model, it produced a **system**: if he walked onto that road tomorrow the wall would not argue with him, it would just count him. The other half of the thesis is the shoulder, and it is the scene's one real character beat. **The instructor from S2 is standing on the roadside stomping a brake pedal that is not there**, out of habit, at nobody, with a worn pedal-shaped bare patch in the gravel under his boot. Then S7's envelope lands in the little inbox tile in his paw with a soft PLOINK, the tile face is the same rolling brass **95%** the wall is carrying, he looks down at it, and **his boot stops mid-air and never comes down.** He is not redundant, he is **relieved**. That is "you never guess, the numbers tell you" landing on the exact person the VO is addressing, and it puts the redundancy where the arc actually put it: in the skip.

- **Set detail (Alex's #1 note):**
  1. **THE RECORD WALL** (near / driver side, **frame-left**, y 300 to 430 in the exterior, seen through the driver's window from f80). Concrete posts every 90px, one every **7 frames** at road speed. Chipped enamel, hairline moss in the joints, dawn light raking the brass. **In frame from f0**, not from f44: for f0 to f44 the posts are **hard black silhouettes with unlit drums**, strobing through the near frame, unreadable and deliberately so.
  2. **THE FLIP** (the wall's verb). Ahead of the car every drum is dark and every cap is dead. On the frame his nose reaches a post, the drum **rolls over one count** and the cap **lights**: green `#3F9E74` for a pass, red for a fail. One brass **chock** per post, every 7 frames, dead in sync with the car. Behind him the rail is a lit green comb. Ahead of him it is black. He is drawing the line as he goes.
  3. **THE MASTER BAND** (the wall's crown rail, **repeating**, not a single gantry). A continuous brass ribbon along the top of the wall that **re-reads 95% at every gantry, roughly every 340px**, so two to three resolved masters are in frame continuously for all 167 frames and one lands in the driver's window every ~28 frames. Dark green enamel housing, bone-white stencil, four brass digits. **The ones drum never sits still**, it micro-rolls and reseats forever, including at f166, and it micro-rolls one notch on every flip. The reel's ONE number, and it is never pinned, never a HUD, and never has to survive being scrolled past, because the next one is always coming.
  4. **THE RED 90 GROOVE** (continuous, **lit**, running the entire crown rail under the master band for all 167 frames). A hairline engraved line with a low constant red ember in it and a hard tick every gantry. It is not decoration and it is not dark: it is **the wall's baseline**. The master's digits ride unmistakably **above** it, the tall green posts break **above** it, and the short red fail-posts sit **under** it. That geometry is the whole VO: above the line, almost always, for as far as the eye can see.
  5. **THE FAIL POST** (near frame, flips at **f110**, in the driver's window). One of his 5%. Short, red-capped, sitting under the groove, its drum flipping over a hand's width from his face. He does not flinch. **That is the thesis:** the wall counts his failures too, on the record, in public, and it changes nothing, because 95 is above 90. More reds pass in the receding comb at roughly one in twenty, unremarked.
  6. **THE DECOMMISSIONED INSTRUCTOR STANDS** (near frame, **every ~90px, offset between the record posts**, so the two rails together wipe the near frame with hard black roughly every 3 to 4 frames). Waist-high steel stubs with a **sheared bolt-flange top** where a dual-control brake used to be bolted, some still wearing an **empty bracket**, all at hub height, all hard black against the dawn. They rhyme exactly with prop 19's snapped bracket and two sheared bolts, which means the fastest layer in the frame is also the thesis. This is the strobe that sells the speed. **There are no generic "roadside markers" in this scene.**
  7. **THE DEPARTURE GANTRY + HEADER SIGN** (approaching dead ahead from f0, biggest at f14, passes overhead at **f18**). **"NOW LEAVING · SUPERVISED ZONE"**, bone-white stencil on dark green enamel, one bolt corner rusted through and the sign hanging 3 degrees off level, a pigeon-sized dent in its lower rail. The scene's ONE header, and it now gets **18 frames of clean approaching air before anything else happens**, because it arrives first and alone.
  8. **THE CHECKPOINT BOOM** (rips overhead at **f22**). Steel arm raised dead vertical, red and white chevrons, its counterweight **still bouncing on its spring** at f26 from a lift that happened before frame 0.
  9. **THE SCORCH SCAR** (tarmac, x 0 to 1012, crossed at **f28**). A long black tyre burn baked into the apron by S2's red-hot brake stomping, still there, still ugly, its edges lifting. The car crosses it at speed and does not flinch. A whole scene's history in one mark.
  10. **THE PROPERTY SKIP** (far / right shoulder, enters f30, gone at **f38**). A dented steel roadside impound skip in the authority's **dark green enamel with a bone-white stencilled seal** (the S3 seal glyph, **no words**, the header rule is not spent here), lid up on a bent hinge, standing 1.5m so it reads as a silhouette in four frames. The villain's kit is **tipped out of it into the dust at its foot**. The picture says: the authority took it, flattened it, and put it out with the bins. Confiscated, not fled.
  11. **THE CRUSHED GUN + THE ROLL STREAK** (in the skip's spill, f30 to f38). The fat counterfeit DONE gun **crushed dead flat**, with the **S6 press's tread pattern pressed into its shell**, split open down one seam. Its sickly yellow-green `#A8B84A` roll spills out of the split and **whips in the car's slipstream** as a hard 200px colour streak. This is the only part of the kit built to be legible, because a moving colour streak is the only part that reads in eight frames.
  12. **THE SHADES IN THE DUST** (at the skip's foot, **one 2-frame specular pop at f34**). The opaque black wraparounds, left lens cracked across, the hard white glint bar snapped. The bible's S8 debt ("his shades lie in the dust") is paid here and at f34 only, as one bright tick of light off a broken lens. It is texture. **It is not asked to carry recognition, and no beat in this scene depends on the audience identifying him from it.** ⛔ Do NOT build: the tie, the earpiece wire, the four pasted crooked stickers, or the forensic glint-stub detail. Black on dust at motion-blur speed reads as zero, and specifying it is specifying nothing.
  13. **THE DYING CODE-RAIN** (3 thin columns over the skip, **f30 to f38**). His aura is always on per the bible, so it is on, and it is **4 glyphs tall and evaporating**: each yellow-green glyph burns out the instant it crosses the dawn key on its way toward the lens. **The last glyph dies at f38, on the exact frame the kit leaves the reel forever**, and the hiss cuts out mid-glyph on that frame. His aura dies where the camera can see it die.
  14. **THE CUT CHAIN** (far shoulder gravel, passes **f34**). A coil of S0's padlock chain in the grass, sheared clean through one link, the cut face still bright. Three pixels of story, no attention paid to it.
  15. **THE PHANTOM BRAKE STAND** (far / right shoulder, **midground, held f44 to f70, twenty-six frames, staged back and read BIG**). The S2 instructor: cool cream-grey Claude, orange hi-vis cap, chevron vest, gold "YOU" tag, clipboard clamped under one arm, **one comically oversized boot stomping air**. Under the boot: a **worn pedal-shaped bare patch in the gravel**, polished by years of stomping, obviously empty. The absence is the gag and the absence finally has the frames to be seen. He is the **only figure that enters this scene**.
  16. **THE INBOX TILE** (in the instructor's free paw, lands **f60**). S7's envelope, the one this scene's IN transition is made of, drops in with a soft PLOINK and pops the red (1) badge exactly as S7 left it. The tile face is the **same rolling brass 95%** the wall is carrying, so it is the reel's one number arriving in his hand, not a new one. **f62** he looks down at it. **f64** his boot **freezes mid-air** and never comes down. **f70** he blurs out of frame with the boot still up and his eyes on the number. The system told him. He does not have to guard.
  17. **THE PYLON LINE** (bg, receding to horizon, f0 to f166). Eleven floodlight pylons of the licence authority, their lamps **clicking off one at a time**, roughly every 14 frames, cold blue draining out of frame-left as warm dawn floods in from frame-right. The last lamp is **mid-fade at f166**. The night shift is over and nobody announced it.
  18. **THE EMPTY SEAT** (interior, from f80). Two parts, and the split is load-bearing. **THE WEDGE** (x 820 to 1012, y 430 to 792): the seat's back as a hard black mass along the frame-right **edge** only, dawn cutting one bright bar across its shoulder, the **belt buckle unlatched and swaying** against it, S2's hi-vis instructor cap hanging forgotten on the headrest at its top, dusty, swinging, S2's clipboard jammed in the seat pocket, unread. **THE CUSHION PLANE** (x 560 to 820, y 560 to 700): open, dawn-lit, dust-fuzzed and **conspicuously bare for 69 frames**. The emptiest thing in the scene is still the biggest, but now it is legibly **empty** instead of merely black, and the audience stares at the hole before the manual fills it.
  19. **THE UNBOLTED PEDAL** (passenger footwell, x 640 to 760, y 600 to 700, from f28). S2's chrome dual-control brake, **hanging upside down from its snapped bracket by one wire**, its pad scuffed and still faintly heat-blued from all that stomping, two sheared bolts rattling loose in the footwell mat. **The hero discovers it at f30** and it swings on every road joint after that. **Still swinging at f166.**
  20. **THE GATE SHIFTER** (interior, x 520 to 600, y 560 to 640). A chunky knockoff exposed-gate lever with a polished H-plate, clay ball knob worn shiny on one side, gaiter split. Slams up through the gate at f68.
  21. **THE REAR-VIEW MIRROR** (interior, x 380 to 560, y 150 to 230, tilted 4 degrees). Holds **only what has the scale to read at 100m and beyond: the licence hall and the still-raised boom, shrinking.** ⛔ Do NOT build the shades in the mirror. A 15cm prop he passed at f38 is a quarter of a pixel by f138 and the beat is a sentence, not an image. The debt is already paid at f34.
  22. **THE PASS FRESHENER** (hanging from the mirror, x 470, y 230 to 290). A cardboard knockoff cut in the silhouette of S6's green PASS stamp head, **spinning slowly on its string** for all 167 frames. Reserved-green, dangling, wordless, doing nothing at all except existing.
  23. **THE LAST STICKER** (windscreen, **inside face**, bottom-left, x 200 to 280, y 620). The `#A8B84A` DONE sticker S2 planted in his eyeline, six scenes old, sun-bleached to a sickly khaki, one corner lifted, adhesive haze ringing it.
  24. **THE WIPER STALK** (interior, at his right nub). The thing he tries first, and the thing that cannot work.
  25. **THE GLOVEBOX** (interior, **x 840 to 990, y 440 to 520**, outboard and forward on the dash). Latch worn silver, lid sitting slightly proud, its dawn-lit horizontal reading hard against the wedge's black. Pops at f149.
  26. **THE MANUAL** (**CLAYNES · AGENTIC OS owner's workshop manual**, in the glovebox, out from f149). Yellow-and-blue knockoff cover, corner-worn, spine embossed OS.
  27. **AIR + LIGHT:** dawn dust and grit rip through the near frame continuously, tick off the windscreen, and hang as motes in the interior sun shafts from f80. One hard low key from frame-right at 6 degrees throws a 400px car shadow left across the tarmac, then cuts a hard bar across the seat wedge once we are inside. Cold blue rim from the dying floodlights on the left, warm dawn from the right.

- **CONTINUOUS ACTION (fills the whole line):** Eleven named layers, each on its own clock, none of them ever landing. **A ROAD** f0 to f166: tarmac scroll and lane dashes at constant speed, never slows, never stops. **B WALL** f0 to f166: present from frame 0, black and unlit to f44, lit and reading after. **C COUNT** f0 to f166: a drum flipping and a cap lighting every 7 frames, dead in sync with the nose, one brass chock each, master ones-digit micro-rolling on every flip. **D PEDAL** f28 to f166: swinging on its wire, creaking on an **11-frame road-joint clock: f28, 39, 50, 61, 72, 83, 94, 105, 116, 127, 138, f149, 160**. The most persistent cue in the scene, and its clock is load-bearing. **E DISPOSAL** f30 to f38: skip, crushed gun, roll streak, code-rain, shades pop, all of it gone at f38. **F SHOULDER** f44 to f70: one figure, one gag, held. **G HERO** f0 to f166: driving, and acting four times. **H CAMERA** f0 to f166: never still. **I PYLONS** f0 to f166: lamps clicking out one by one, last one mid-fade at f166. **J MANUAL** f149 to f166: sliding. **K AIR** f0 to f166: dust, grit, motes, light.
  **f0 to f18 THE SIGN.** Cold-in on the whip of S7's envelope, no ramp, already at full road speed. Camera low at hub height, three-quarter front-left, matching the car exactly. Hero on frame 0 acting: two nubs on the wheel, eyes open and forward, not celebrating, just driving. **The record wall is in frame from f0** as a black silhouette comb ripping past frame-left, drums unlit, instructor stands strobing between the posts, chocks already ticking under the road roar. **NOW LEAVING · SUPERVISED ZONE** is dead ahead on its gantry, legible and growing for eighteen clean frames, and it passes overhead at f18 with nothing competing with it. That is the whole of its air, and it is all it needs.
  **f18 to f44 THE CLEAR-OUT.** f22 the boom rips overhead, counterweight still bouncing on its spring at f26. f24 he drops his right nub to the door sill and hangs his elbow out the window. f28 the tyres cross the scorch scar on a low BRAP and the same joint kicks the unbolted pedal into view under the passenger door. **f30 to f32: his eyes flick down to the dead chrome pedal hanging upside down by one wire, two frames, and go back to the road.** That is the pedal's funeral and its only one. f30 the skip enters far-right, kit spilled at its foot, roll streaking in the slipstream, code-rain 4 glyphs and burning. f34 the shades pop one specular tick and the cut chain passes. **f38 the last glyph dies and the villain leaves the reel.**
  **f44 to f70 THE IGNITION AND THE BOOT.** **f44: the dawn key clears the ridge and rakes down the rail left to right**, and the wall **ignites in a running wave**: brass drums, green caps, the red groove's ember, the master bands resolving to **95%** one after another out to the horizon. Nothing arrived. It was always there. Now you can read it, and what you read is a green comb with rare red gaps under a red line, at speed, in half a second, with no words. From here the flips are visible: he passes, it counts, the cap lights. Far shoulder, held: f44 the instructor is on frame, boot up. f52 to f60 he stomps air twice on the rhythm over his worn bare patch. **f60 PLOINK**, S7's envelope drops into the inbox tile in his paw, red (1) badge popping, tile face rolling the same brass 95%. f62 he looks down. **f64 the boot freezes mid-air.** f70 he blurs out with it still up.
  **f68 to f80 THE SHIFT.** f68: the hero's nub drops onto the gate lever and slams it up through the H-plate. **On that exact frame the camera commits**: one continuous motion-blurred swing, 12 frames, around the nose, across the hood, in through the windscreen, settling at f80 behind the seat wedge. Exhaust bark, the road surges, the whole world stretches. This is not a cut. It is a move, and it is the only one in the scene.
  **f80 to f122 INSIDE, AND THE ONE THING HE WANTS.** Near foreground is the black seat wedge on the frame-right edge, belt buckle swaying, the cushion plane open and bare beside it. Pedal swinging in its footwell on its 11-frame clock. Record wall ripping past the driver's window frame-left. PASS freshener spinning on the mirror. The mix drops here and stays down. **f80: his right nub leaves the sill and flicks the wiper stalk.** The blade sweeps clean across the outside of the glass and the sticker does not move, because the villain never had the reach to put it anywhere but the **inside**, in his eyeline, where it has sat for six scenes. **f96: he flicks it again.** The blade squeaks over the mark a second time and does nothing. **f100 THE CONTACT BEAT:** a post's drum **flips over in the driver's window, near frame, big, brass ticking, and his face and the counter are in the SAME RECTANGLE for the first time in the reel.** He does not look. **f110:** the fail post flips a hand's width from his face, red cap, short, sitting under the groove. He does not flinch. **f112: he gives up on the machine and does it himself**, right nub off the wheel, thumb on the glass, scraping the villain's last mark off his own windscreen. **f118 it lets go.** f122 he flicks it out the open window and it tumbles out of frame forever.
  **f122 to f148 THE MIRROR AND THE NUMBER.** In the mirror the licence hall shrinks and the boom is still up. The hero never once looks at it. **f128: the next master band comes around into the driver's window**, near plane, big. **f138: the dawn key rakes the brass up** and the band ignites. **f148: 95% resolves, ones-digit micro-rolling, dead under the word "earns."** The reel's one number lands on the reel's thesis word, in the near frame, legibly, without being pinned, without a HUD, and without him ever having glanced at it. That is the difference between an image and a caption.
  **f148 to f167 THE HANDOFF.** **f149: the road joint on the pedal's own 11-frame creak clock does two jobs at once**, it creaks the pedal and it pops the glovebox latch. The lid drops, and under acceleration the **CLAYNES · AGENTIC OS owner's workshop manual** slides rearward out of the box and down onto the **bare, dawn-lit cushion plane**, in the open, in the clear, where the audience has been staring at a hole for sixty-nine frames.
  **At f166:** the manual is still sliding, the pedal is still swinging, a drum is mid-flip, the master's ones digit is mid-roll, the freshener is mid-spin, the last pylon lamp is mid-fade, the road is still coming and the hero has not moved his eyes off it. Nothing in this frame has finished.

- **Escalation:** S7 was the summit: klaxon, VOID, envelope at camera. S8 must not compete on volume and does not try. It escalates on **speed and certainty while getting quieter**, which no other scene in the reel does, so it lands as a genuinely new gear rather than a lower-volume rerun. The curve is **subtraction**, and now the claim is literally true: **the only things that ENTER this scene are the road, the numbers, and one man who stops stomping.** Everything else leaves. Every twenty frames another safety device goes: the sign passes, the boom clears, the pedal is found dead, the villain's kit is disposed of and his aura burns out, the seat is revealed empty, the last counterfeit comes off the glass, the boot freezes, the floodlights go dark. The road speed never drops for a frame. The mix follows it down: the hall's dying klaxon reverb, then the code-rain hiss cutting out at f38, then the swing dropping road noise behind glass at f80, until all that is left under the last 80 frames is one brass count beat and wind. By the end the frame contains three things: the road, the numbers, him. That is the thesis played as an empty room, and after S7's noise it hits colder than another crescendo would.

- **Gag / pop-culture:** All geometric, all knockoff, no marks, every body a Claude sprite. (1) **The border-checkpoint parody**: raised boom, chevrons, counterweight and a departure sign, the universal "you are now leaving" geometry, branded only as **SUPERVISED ZONE**. (2) **The phantom brake, and the boot that never comes down**: the instructor on the shoulder stomping air over a worn pedal-shaped bare patch, then the email lands, he looks at the number, and his boot just hangs there. He is not fired, he is off duty, and he is the first person in this reel who does not have to watch. (3) **The shift pun, played three ways at once** on the word "shift": the H-gate lever slams, the exhaust barks, and the camera itself swings 190 degrees. (4) **The knockoff air freshener** cut in the silhouette of the S6 PASS stamp head, spinning on the mirror, wordless. (5) **The impound skip**: the villain's whole career in a roadside bin with the lid up, which is a better joke than the dirt, because the dirt says he ran and the skip says they took it off him. (6) **The crushed gun with the press's tread pattern in its shell**, a Looney-Tunes flattening delivered entirely by a texture. (7) **The decommissioned instructor stands**, a hundred sheared bolt-flanges ripping past at hub height, a graveyard of second brake pedals used as the speed strobe. (8) **The stadium-pylon blackout**, eleven lamps going out in sequence like a stadium after the crowd has left.

- **Sprite costume:** **HERO:** canonical clay Claude, warm `#D97757`, **eyes visible and forward** for all 167 frames, which is the tell that separates him from the thing that used to sit next to him. Navy mechanic's coverall, collar rolled, sleeves up, one smear of stamp ink still on the forearm from S6. He does not change costume once. That is the point. **He acts four times and only four times:** right elbow to the sill at f24, a two-frame look down at the dead pedal at f30, the gate lever at f68, and the sticker (stalk f80, stalk f96, thumb f112, off at f118). Expression: level, not triumphant, not smug. ⛔ No L-plate, no plate ghost, no badge, no wings pin, no keys in frame. **VILLAIN:** **not present as a body, and must not be drawn as one.** He is represented only by disposed property, to bible spec and to the legibility budget: the PROPERTY skip, the crushed `#A8B84A` gun with the S6 tread in its shell, its roll whipping in the slipstream, the cracked shades in the dust as one 2-frame glint, and three columns of yellow-green code-rain 4 glyphs tall and out at f38. **Do not claim this scene finishes his arc.** He was destroyed at S7. S8 takes out the bins. **CAMEO, ONE, and only one:** the S2 instructor. Same mascot silhouette, cool cream-grey, orange hi-vis cap, chevron vest, gold "YOU" tag, clipboard, one oversized boot. Stomping, then stopped. ⛔ **The S4 tin bot and the S5 Fresh Eyes examiner are CUT from S8 entirely.** They had their scenes. A curtain call is not an arc beat, and neither of their gags can survive six blurred frames behind a strobe.

- **Camera:** **Lens feel:** a wide 28mm on a low car-to-car tracking rig for f0 to f68, road filling the bottom third, everything close and rushing and slightly distorted at the edges. After the swing it becomes a **40mm inside the cabin**, compressed and calm, because the world is doing the work now and the camera has stopped shouting. **Height:** 22px above the tarmac, wheel-hub height, heroic and dirty, for the exterior. The swing lifts it to seat height for the interior. **Push / pan:** **no push and no zoom anywhere.** The camera matches the car's speed exactly for all 167 frames, which is precisely why the car reads locked and the world reads fast: he is not being chased and he is not being followed, he is being **kept pace with**. The one move is the **f68 to f80 swing**, 190 degrees, motion-blurred, around the nose and in through the glass, motivated by the gear shift, landing behind the seat wedge. **The rig never leaves the car, so nothing in this scene may be staged as happening after the car has gone.** **Depth stack, exterior f0 to f68:** FOREGROUND OCCLUDER = the record wall's own posts and the decommissioned instructor stands ripping through the near frame frame-left in hard black silhouette, alternating roughly every 3 to 4 frames, plus dawn grit. The strobe of near-black wipes is what sells the speed, and it is built, named, and doing thesis work. MIDGROUND = the car, the skip and its spill on the far shoulder, the instructor held on the far shoulder f44 to f70. BACKGROUND = the wall's rail receding to the horizon as a green comb, the pylons clicking out, the licence hall shrinking, the sunrise. **Depth stack, interior f80 to f166:** FOREGROUND OCCLUDER = the seat wedge as a hard black mass on the frame-right **edge** with the unlatched belt swaying across it, holding for 86 frames. MIDGROUND = hero, shifter, swinging pedal, mirror, freshener, and the bare dawn-lit cushion plane sitting in the open beside the wedge. BACKGROUND = the record wall, the flipping drums and the master bands through the driver's window frame-left, road and sunrise through the windscreen. **Light:** one hard low key from frame-right at 6 degrees, cool blue rim from the dying floodlights on the left, warm dawn key from the right. The complementary split is the whole grade: cold behind him, warm ahead. **The f44 ridge-clear is a light event, not an entrance.**

- **Transition + SFX:** **IN:** S7's envelope flares to cream and the flare is peeled off frame-left by the raised boom arm, hard into road noise at full speed, no ramp, no music sting. **That same envelope is paid off at f60, in the instructor's paw.** **Beats:** f0 tyre roar and wind, the wall's brass chock bed already ticking under it every 7 frames (deliberately S7's punch tempo, so the ear hears the ceremony become a heartbeat), S7's klaxon reverb still dying underneath and gone by f18 · f18 the header sign's gantry WHUMPs overhead · f22 boom WHUP plus a counterweight spring boing · f28 a low BRAP across the scorch scar and the first pedal-wire creak · the creak then lands on every road joint, f28 / 39 / 50 / 61 / 72 / 83 / 94 / 105 / 116 / 127 / **149** / 160, the most persistent cue in the scene · f30 to f38 a hollow steel skip rattle, a dry cracked-glass tick at f34, and the code-rain hiss **cutting out mid-glyph at f38** · **f44 the ignition: a long brass shimmer rakes left to right down the rail as the light hits it, and from here every chock is doubled by a soft cap-click**, so the count is audibly his · f60 a soft inbox PLOINK · f68 the SHIFT, a chunky gate CLACK plus exhaust bark plus the swing's wind smear · f80 interior settle, road noise drops behind glass and **the mix stays down from here**, the only scene in the reel that quiets on purpose · f80 and f96 a rubber wiper squeak that accomplishes nothing, twice · f112 a thumbnail scrape on glass · f118 the adhesive letting go and a small paper flutter · **f149 one road joint doing two jobs: the pedal creak and a glovebox latch POP, then a paper slide** · f166 cut on the motion. **OUT:** no flare, no stop, no hold. Cut on the manual still sliding across the bare cushion, straight into S9's manual thumping open on the bench. Same object, same paper, no seam.
  ⛔ **BINDING, cross-scene:** **this OUT SUPERSEDES S9's key-turn IN** (61-os.md line 209: "the handed-over keys ... fly in from frame right, drop into the machine's ignition slot, and the twist of the key is what pops the hood"). Per the board ruling, **the keys are handed over ONCE, at S7**, and S8 carries a hard no-keys ban. S9 opens on the **same manual thumping open on the bench, caused by the slide, no keys anywhere in the seam.** Push this note onto S9's card as well, so the correction lands on both sides of the cut instead of being discovered at assembly.

- **Mute check:** **"A wall of counters flips over as he passes, and the seat beside him is empty."** Under 2 seconds, no reading. The wall is the subject, because the wall is the base object: green cap after green cap lighting in sync with his nose, out to the horizon, a red one every so often sitting under a red line, and the seat where the guard used to be is bare. The only words in frame are one sign that has had its own clean eighteen frames and is gone, and the scene still reads with it deleted. The number does not: **95% above the line, re-reading every 340px, is the picture, not the caption.**

- **Hook into next:** The glovebox pops at f149 on the same road joint that creaks the dead pedal, and the **CLAYNES · AGENTIC OS owner's workshop manual** slides out onto the bare, dawn-lit passenger cushion, in the clear, still sliding at f166. The seat that used to hold the person who guessed now holds the manual for the system that does not, and that swap is the whole reel in one prop, photographed rather than blocked. The question it plants is the only one left: **what is actually in that book.** S9 opens on the same manual thumping open on the bench and exploding into the running machine, and the ask is one word.

- **⛔ CRITIC FIXES (binding on the build):**
  - **Base object now has a verb: COUNT.** Every drum flips and every cap lights on the frame his nose passes the post, one chock each, so the chain reads he drives / the wall counts him / the number is his. Mute check rewritten with the wall as its subject.
  - **The wall IS the graph.** Tall green-capped posts for passes, short red-capped posts under the line for fails, roughly one in twenty red, forty to sixty posts in frame receding. "He almost never fails" now reads in half a second with no digits and no VO.
  - **The red 90 groove is LIT and CONTINUOUS**, running the whole crown rail as the wall's baseline, with the master's digits above it, green posts above it and red fails under it. It is no longer a dark 260px hairline flashing past once.
  - **Geometry contradiction killed.** The master is no longer one gantry that both scrolls and stays. It is a **repeating brass band re-reading 95% every ~340px** on the crown rail, so a resolved, micro-rolling 95% is in frame continuously f0 to f166 and lands in the driver's window every ~28 frames with no cheat and no HUD.
  - **Wall moved to the NEAR / DRIVER side (frame-left).** Kit and the one remaining cameo stay on the far / right shoulder. The number is now visible through the driver's window from f80 without contradicting S2's left-seat blocking or the seat wedge on frame-right.
  - **Wall is in frame from f0.** f44 is its RESOLVE, not its arrival: black silhouette comb with unlit drums f0 to f44, then the dawn key clears the ridge and rakes it alight left to right. The base object honours its own "all 167 frames" spec and the VO's opening clause has its subject on screen while it is said.
  - **Shoulder cleared.** S4's tin bot and S5's Fresh Eyes **CUT from S8 entirely**. The tin bot's "waves after the car is gone" beat is dropped outright, the rig never leaves the car. Their 14 frames go to the instructor, who now holds f44 to f70 alone, staged back and read big.
  - **The instructor's joke redirected: relieved, not redundant.** Two stomps on the rhythm over a worn pedal-shaped bare patch, then S7's envelope PLOINKs into the inbox tile in his paw carrying the same 95%, he looks down, and **his boot freezes mid-air and never comes down**. This pays the IN transition's envelope, puts "the numbers tell you" in the frame of the person the VO addresses, and stops the reel telling its buyer he is obsolete eleven frames before asking him to comment OS.
  - **Villain's kit staged as DISPOSED, not dropped:** an authority-green PROPERTY skip with the lid up (seal glyph, no words, the header rule is protected), kit tipped out at its foot, the gun **crushed flat with the S6 press's tread in its shell**. The arc's actual sentence, and it does not reopen the loop.
  - **Kit budgeted as texture.** Built: the skip silhouette, the crushed gun, the `#A8B84A` roll whipping in the slipstream (the only element that reads in eight frames), the shades as one 2-frame specular pop paying the bible's debt. Cut: the tie, the earpiece, the glint-stub forensics, the pasted stickers, and the claim that the villain's arc finishes here.
  - **Code-rain compressed into the pass:** 4 glyphs at f30, burning out as they cross the dawn key toward the lens, **last glyph and hiss dying at f38** on the exact frame the kit exits. No more aura hanging over a place the camera cannot see.
  - **Shades cut from the mirror.** The mirror keeps only what has scale at 100m: the hall and the still-raised boom. The f138 "gone" beat had nothing left to disappear.
  - **f120 to f146 is no longer static:** the master band comes around into the driver's window at f128, the dawn rakes the brass at f138, and **95% resolves at f148 directly under the word "earns."**
  - **The hero gets one contact beat with the system, and only one:** at **f100** a drum flips over in the driver's window and **his face and the counter share one rectangle for the first time in the reel.** He does not look. **f110** a red fail post flips beside his face and he does not flinch.
  - **The hero gets a want:** he tries the wiper at f80 and f96, it fails both times because S2 planted the sticker on the **inside** of the glass, and at f112 he gives up on the machine and thumbs it off himself, letting go at f118 and gone at f122 exactly as timed. **Reconciliation note (fix 19 x fix 20):** a wiper physically cannot clear an inside-face sticker, so the wiper is kept as the ATTEMPT and the obstacle, and the win is his own thumb. Want, obstacle, escalation and a personal win inside 38 frames, and f118 is now the hero scraping the villain's last mark off his own glass rather than a windscreen cleaning itself.
  - **The sticker is planted, not invented.** DEPENDENCY on S2: the villain leans over the hero's shoulder mid-stomp and slaps the crooked `#A8B84A` DONE sticker on the inside of the glass while the trip reads `0.0 m` (he certifies a car that has not moved an inch), and it survives S3 to S7 sun-faded and unmentioned.
  - **The pedal fires once.** DEPENDENCY: it must still be bolted in and functional at the end of S7. Strike the punt from S7's body text (Story, Escalation, CONTINUOUS ACTION f105 to f150, Gag), not just its footnote, and strike "confiscate the brake" from S3's aim. **S8 owns the pedal's death** and the hero discovers it dead at f30, once, in two frames.
  - **The header earns its air:** the sign now approaches dead ahead and is legible f0 to f18, alone, arriving before the boom (f22) and the scorch scar (f28) instead of underneath them.
  - **The unnamed "roadside markers" placeholder is killed** and replaced by **DECOMMISSIONED INSTRUCTOR STANDS** every ~90px, offset between the record posts: sheared bolt-flange stubs, some still wearing empty brackets, hard black at hub height. The fastest layer in the frame is now also the thesis, and the near-frame strobe is built rather than promised.
  - **The interior is re-blocked:** seat back becomes a hard black **wedge on the frame-right edge only** (x 820 to 1012), the **cushion plane** (x 560 to 820, y 560 to 700) stays open, dawn-lit and conspicuously bare for 69 frames, and the glovebox moves outboard and forward (x 840 to 990, y 440 to 520) to read against the wedge. The hook into S9 is now photographed, not blocked, and the emptiest thing in the scene is legibly empty.
  - **The f149 trigger is named:** it is the road joint on the pedal's own 11-frame creak clock (f28, 39, 50 ... f149), so one cause fires two props. No placeholder bumps.
  - **S8's OUT supersedes S9's key-turn IN** per the board ruling. The manual's slide causes S9's thump. No keys in the seam. Note pushed onto S9's card.
  - **Palette ruling logged:** the green caps use the reserved authority green `#3F9E74` **because each cap IS an S6 PASS**, same green, same meaning, same authority. Reserved-green is honoured, not spent.
  - **Escalation's subtraction claim is now literally true:** the only things that enter this scene are the road, the numbers, and one man who stops stomping.

---

### S9 CTA

- **Takeaway:** Everything you just watched is ONE machine with exactly two moving ideas in it, the loop and the grading, it is still running at the last frame, and the whole build is one word away: comment OS.

- **Base object:** **THE WORKSHOP MANUAL THAT BUILDS THE PASS/FAIL LINE.** A thick, coffee-ringed repair manual driven open on the authority's service bench, whose pages fan up and whose ink peels off the paper as 3D parts, blooms into an exploded view, and rains back down into a bench-scale **PASS/FAIL LINE**: a crate rides in on a belt at frame left, a twin-head press slams, the crate leaves the far side as a card carrying a wet green roundel, a cameo Claude clips the card to a job wire, and the next crate is already on the belt. Not a mechanism-shaped mechanism. A stranger reads it as **"it grades the work, then it does it again."** That is the loop and the grading, which is the entire VO, in one circuit. No other scene owns a manual or an exploded diagram (S0 owns the shutter, S1 the lift and sticker gun, S2 the learner car and brake pedal, S3 the steel rule gantry, S4 the tarp and tin bot, S5 the pod and order tube, S6 the press, S7 the punch card and gauge, S8 the turntable and THE RECORD).
  **Collision notes:** (a) The press and the belt are the board's own sanctioned exception: "S9 re-showing S4/S5/S6/S7 as miniature modules is intentional recap and reads as payoff, not repetition." S6's press is a full-size bay machine over a bay conveyor at a service window; S9's is a **bench-scale tabletop rig** the hero can slap with one nub. Different scale, different sound, different silhouette. (b) **Green stays reserved for the S6 press**, and this rig IS the S6 press module, so it is the one object allowed to issue green. (c) **THE RECORD belongs to S8, full stop.** The job wire is not a record: no enamel board, no odometer, no counter, no numbers, no punches. It is a bench-height taut wire with clipped cards, a diner order rail, and it is the line's physical OUTPUT, not a display. (d) The S0 roller shutter reappears as background dressing only, rolled fully up, inert, never touched, never acted on. A callback, not a prop.

- **Story / metaphor:** We are behind the counter, in the licence authority's own service bay: the room where the OS itself is maintained. The hero is licensed, plateless, and he opens the machine up and shows you the schematic, because a system you can service is the opposite of a system you have to trust. He does not watch it build itself. **He causes it:** he cracks the manual open on frame 0, the pages fan off his own glove, the parts bloom, they rain back into his waiting wrench, and his last torque is what turns the thing over. Then it grades a crate, hangs the ticket, and reaches for the next crate, twice on camera, with a third already coming. **The villain has no state in this scene, because he has nothing left to lose.** He was destroyed at S7 and he was dust in S8's rear-view. He gets no beat, no body, no funeral and no last look. He is present only as three unremarked objects nobody in this bay acknowledges: a jar of his falling glyphs on a shelf, his snapped counterfeit gun pinned to a corkboard, and an empty suit on a nail that the camera slides straight past and out of frame without one beat of interest. His aura was "always on, this is how you find him." It is now three inches tall, behind glass, and no one has looked at it in weeks.

- **Set detail (Alex's #1 note):**
  1. **THE S0 ROLLER SHUTTER, ROLLED UP** (back wall left, x 30-320; drum y 70-120, open mouth y 120-500). The exact shutter he was padlocked behind in the hook, slats stacked in the drum, S8's dawn pouring through the open mouth in a hard raking wedge that lands on the floor at (600, 720). Chains sway on a **31f** clock for all 118 frames. The lockout loop closes and nobody says a word about it.
  2. **THE OPEN PADLOCK** (bent nail at 330, 296). Shackle sprung wide, **hasp EMPTY, no key, no broken key, no key anywhere in this reel after S7**. Nobody forced it. The authority unlocked it because he earned it. **53f** pendulum, ticking softly against the shutter track on every pass (bed, not a ranked cue).
  3. **THE MANUAL** (midground bench, x 120-340, y 540-645). Thick, plain, honest, coffee-ringed, cover in a mustard-and-navy grid with cutaway line-art, wordmark **CLAYNES · AGENTIC OS · OWNER'S WORKSHOP MANUAL**, spine embossed **OS**. The fine print is real line noise with **no joke riding on it**. Each page fans up, its ink peels off the paper as a 3D part, and the page falls back flat.
  4. **THE PASS/FAIL LINE** (midground, x 180-820, y 360-540, sitting on a low plinth behind the bench). Three stations and only three. **Station 1, THE BELT** (in at x 180, y 470): cleats ratcheting left to right, sprocket teeth turning, an S0 night-shift work crate riding in, same crate geometry as the hook's roof-high stack. **Station 2, THE TWIN-HEAD PRESS** (x 480-580, head y 360-440): the S6 turret at bench scale, green head and red head both inked and live, rocking re-ink roller, pneumatic hiss. **Station 3, THE JOB WIRE** (x 640-820, y 400): a taut wire with three cards already clipped, swaying on a **67f** clock. The press head **never fully rests**: between slams it creeps on a slow 2px hydraulic breathe, so at f117 it is mid-creep, not parked.
  5. **THE GREEN MARK, NOT THE GREEN WORD.** The card comes out of the press carrying the press's own **authority-green `#3F9E74` roundel with a punched tick**, ink still wet and squashing at the edge. No word. Green roundel from a press reads as pass to any stranger in half a second and costs **zero reading load**, which is the honest version of what the old card called sub-legible texture.
  6. **THE BRASS 95% GAUGE** (bolted to the rig flank at 660, 430). Reads **95%**, with the **90 threshold as an engraved red tick, not lit, not animated, not a display**. The needle sweeps up on the ignition and micro-jitters forever after. This is the scene's one and only number.
  7. **THE GLYPH JAR** (shelf x 700-990, y 330-350; jar sits at 890, 300). A clear screw-top jar holding roughly forty sickly yellow-green `#A8B84A` glyphs still falling *inside the glass* like a snow globe, for all 118 frames. **No shades in it: the shades are S8's last word and they fire once.** No tag, no EXHIBIT A card, no cameo, no kick stool, no tap. Nobody touches it, nobody looks at it, nobody stands near it. That is the kill.
  8. **THE EMPTY SLATE SUIT** (foreground occluder, frame right, x 900-1012, y 40-560, **4x parallax plane**). Black jacket with the sharp shoulder blocks and the skinny tie, hanging off a nail, grey, empty, the coiled earpiece wire dangling from the collar. Blurred. Sways on a **97f** draft clock. The push carries it **~150px right and it clears frame right by ~f95**, leaving the last 23 frames clean for the machine and the pill. He does not get to be in the last shot.
  9. **THE CORKBOARD, ONE RELIC AND ONE CARD** (x 752-836, y 158-200). Not a trophy case: the four-exhibit museum is gone (no cracked L-plate, no S0 night-shift photo, no EXHIBIT A tag, no DELETED FROM ASSEMBLY band). What remains is the fat counterfeit gun **snapped in two, pinned through the break** with a red pin, its ink pad dried to a yellow-green scab, and **one index card under it reading DO NOT REFIT**. The card's bottom corner **lifts on the bay draft on the suit's 97f clock**, and the lamp's shadow rakes the whole board on the 74f swing, so the exhibit is on a clock and the wall breathes.
  10. **THE EXPLODED MODULES, MOSTLY DEAD CASTINGS** (bloom from 500, 380 out to a 500px ring). Icon-led parts on hairline leader lines with **blank tick-marks, no part numbers, no labels**: a sunrise dial, a clipboard-and-out-tray, a shuttered iris pod, the twin-head press, the brass gauge. **Only the belt, the press and the gauge become live hardware.** The dial, the clipboard and the iris rain in and bolt on as **inert bodywork**, so S4 and S5 get their callback as sculpture and the loop keeps its three readable stations.
  11. **THE SWINGING WORK-LAMP** (cord from 470, 0 to head at 470, 90, swings ±26px, **74f**). The hero yanks the pull-cord at f2 and **that yank is what starts the pendulum**, so the swing has an author. Its cone rakes the bay floor **and the back wall**, so the corkboard, the shutter track and the shelf all breathe on the same clock. Dust and grinding grit drift visibly through the cone the whole shot.
  12. **THE 2c TIN BOT** (bench, x 380-540, y 545, **41f** patrol). S4's wind-up inspector, clattering quietly, still checking things nobody asked it to check. **Demoted to the shutter's tier: no hero contact, no elbow nudge, no SFX beat, never acted on.** Dumb as a brick, never tires, reads in half a second, competes with nothing.
  13. **THE LEFTOVER BOLT + THE FLAT-PACK CARD** (bolt at 250, 628; card at 300, 640, on the **out-of-focus FOREGROUND bench lip, 3x parallax**, bottom left, clear of the composer band). One bolt spins to a stop. Beside it a wordless single-panel card: a distressed stick-Claude, a phone, a crossed-out helpline. The machine runs anyway. **This is the lead gag and it is entirely wordless.**
  14. **THE CRATE LOADER CLAUDE** (x 150-230, y 430-520). A cameo Claude swinging S0 crates onto the belt, one every 28f, never stops, never looks up.
  15. **THE CLIPPER CLAUDE** (x 700, y 430-520). A cameo Claude who takes each stamped card off the press lip and clips it to the job wire. Two clips on camera, and he is reaching for a third at the cut.
  16. **GRIME.** Oil-shadow rectangles on the floor where old rigs used to sit, a smudged handprint on the shutter track, a red shop rag on the bench lip, brass swarf glittering in the light wedge, a coffee ring on the manual's cover. **No chalk tallies:** a tally is a count wearing grime's clothes, and this scene has exactly one number.
  17. **PINNED, THE THREE THINGS THAT MUST READ.** **HERO at (560, 470)**, standing at the press inside the lamp cone, rendered at **1.5x cameo scale** so he is the largest and brightest sprite in frame. **THE OS STAMP:** punches off the manual's spine at (120-340, 540-645) at f80 and slams to rest **dead-centre at (500, 300)** over the running line, clear of the shelf (x≥700) and the corkboard (752-836, 158-200), and holds there to f118. **THE OS PILL** (this is the chip, named once, and it is one thing): **the gold keyword chip inside the house comment composer**, composer band reserved **y 655-745**, pill centred at **(430, 700)**. The stamp on the machine and the pill in the composer are the same word twice, which is the whole ask, and the finger has something real to point at.

- **CONTINUOUS ACTION (fills the whole line):** Thirteen layers, every oscillator on a **near-coprime period so nothing locks into sync inside 118 frames**: lamp **74f**, padlock **53f**, empty suit and the DO NOT REFIT corner **97f**, tin bot **41f**, shutter chain **31f**, job wire **67f**, crate cadence **28f**. Do not default these to a shared spring.
  - **L1 LAMP** f0-118, 74f: yanked alive at f2, then every floor shadow AND every back-wall shadow sweeps for the rest of the scene. Never stops.
  - **L2 HERO** f0-118, **acting on frame 0 with an object and a consequence**: f0 he is already mid-swing, driving the manual open on the bench with the heel of his glove and cracking the spine, other nub already reaching for the pull-cord. f2 the cord yanks, the cone hits. f4 the pages fan **off his own hand**, which is what fires L3. f24-56 he holds a stubby wrench out into the converge, waiting. **f56** the last converging part rains into that waiting wrench. **f56-60** he torques it home. **f60 his torque is the ignition.** f68 wrench toss, spins once, rings into the tray at f76. f82 wipes palms on the rag. f92 two flat-palm slaps on the machine's flank. f104-118 one gloved nub extended dead at the OS pill, other nub resting on the rig. Focused, then a proud closed-mouth half-smile.
  - **L3 EXPLODE** f0-24: pages fan one after another, ink lifts off as parts drifting outward, each part still rotating on its own axis, leader lines drawing stroke by stroke, whole assembly group-rotating 12 degrees so parallax never stops. **The DIAGRAM opens to its 500px ring. The frame does not change.**
  - **L4 CONVERGE** f24-56: parts rain inward staggered 3-4f apart, a continuous patter not one snap, each contact popping a dust puff and a key-light spark, the ring collapsing into one dense object, the rig settling on a 2f overshoot. The dial, the clipboard and the iris seat as dead bodywork; the belt, the press and the gauge seat as hardware.
  - **L5 THE LOOP AND THE GRADING** **f60-118 and past the cut**. One locked ignition frame, **f60**, and it is causal: his wrench turns it over. The gauge needle sweeps to 95 and holds. Then three stations, ~28f cadence, **two complete grades on camera and a third already entering**, which is what sells perpetual. **Crate A:** enters frame left f60, rides the belt f60-72, **press slams f72**, holds 4f, ink squashes, head lifts f78, card with the wet green roundel out at f80, clipper Claude clips it to the wire at f86. **Crate B:** enters f88, rides f88-100, **press slams f100** (turret hesitates 2 frames over the red head first, so the binary is visibly live, then takes green), head lifts f106, card out f108, clipped f114. **Crate C:** enters f116 and is mid-belt at f117. Belt cleats never stop. Loader never stops.
  - **L6 JAR GLYPHS** f0-118: falling, untouched, unlooked-at, still falling at f117.
  - **L7 SHUTTER + DUST** f0-118, 31f: chain sway, dawn wedge, motes and swarf drifting through the cone.
  - **L8 PADLOCK** f0-118, 53f: pendulum, soft tick on each pass, folded into the bed.
  - **L9 SUIT + CORKBOARD** f0-118, 97f: the suit sways on the bay draft and the DO NOT REFIT card's corner lifts on the same draft. The suit rides the 4x plane out of frame right by ~f95; the corkboard stays and keeps breathing under the raking lamp shadow.
  - **L10 COMPOSER** **f60-118**: the house comment composer resolves into the reserved band **on the ignition kick**, one beat, machine starting and ask arriving together. The OS pill begins a slow gold pulse on a 20f cycle and the cursor blinks for 58 frames (~1.9s). The last third of this scene is calm on purpose: one machine cycling, one pill, one finger.
  - **L11 TIN BOT** f0-118, 41f: clatters its patrol across the bench, unremarked, untouched.
  - **L12 JOB WIRE** f0-118, 67f: the clipped cards sway, and the newest one is still swinging from its f114 clip at the cut.
  - **L13 CAMERA** f0-118: unbroken push, still moving at the last frame.
  - **At f117 exactly:** crate C is mid-belt, the press head is mid-hydraulic-creep, the clipper Claude is reaching for a card that has not arrived, the loader is mid-swing with the next crate, the gauge needle is mid-jitter, the lamp is mid-swing, the padlock is mid-tick, the jar glyphs are still falling, the tin bot is mid-step, the job wire is mid-sway, **the DO NOT REFIT card is mid-lift and the corkboard's pin shadows are mid-sweep**, the OS pill is mid-pulse, and the camera is still pushing. Nothing in frame has finished.

- **Escalation:** Scatter to unity to one calm ask. **f0-12:** bench thump, dry spine crack, the cord yanks, the cone hits, pages start fanning off his glove. **f12-24:** the bloom. The **diagram** opens to its full 500px ring and every part is rotating, which is the breadth; the camera does nothing different. **f24-56:** reverse and compact, a rising staccato of clicks, the ring collapsing into one dense object. **f56-60:** the last part lands in his waiting wrench, he torques it home, **IGNITION on f60**, and the composer lands on that same kick. The needle sweeps to 95 and holds. **f60-100:** the line grades. Crate, slam, card, wire, next crate. At **f88** the OS wordmark punches off the spine and slams to a stamp at centre with a 2px shake. At **f100** the second slam lands **underneath the calm**, which is the point: the ask is already on screen and the machine is still working. **f100-118:** everything drops to a warm idle hum. One machine cycling, one pill, one finger. Energy climbs, then lands. It does not compete with S7's peak, it rewards it.

- **Gag / pop-culture:** All geometric, all wordless where it counts. **(1) LEAD, the flat-pack gag:** one leftover bolt spun to a stop, one wordless single-panel instruction card, a distressed stick-Claude, a phone, a crossed-out helpline, and the machine runs perfectly anyway. Universal, zero reading, and it is **on thesis**: a system you can service without calling anyone is exactly what this scene is arguing. **(2) The dad-slaps-the-roof meme played straight:** the double flank slap on a bench-scale machine. No floating tag, no price prop, no "runs while you sleep" claim (that is a second header and it is not in the locked number spine). **(3) The jar:** his aura, the one thing that was "always on, this is how you find him," now three inches tall behind screw-top glass on a shelf, with nobody in the bay ever looking at it. The inversion, played completely straight. **(4) DO NOT REFIT:** the one line in the scene that is funnier read than seen, so it gets to be legible. **NOT counted as a gag: CLAYNES.** The manual carries the mustard-and-navy cutaway geometry as unremarked cover texture and nothing is riding on the fine print.

- **Sprite costume:** **HERO:** canonical warm `#D97757` clay Claude, eyes visible, rendered at **1.5x** at (560, 470). Navy mechanic's coverall, rolled collar, gold zip, name strip stitched "CLAUDE", cut-off work gloves, folded-brim pit cap worn slightly back (**no OS patch: the word OS fires exactly twice, as the stamp and as the pill**), goggles pushed up leaving a clean smudge line, grease on one cheek, red rag on the hip. His laminated licence is clipped to the chest, small, unremarked. **No L-plate, no clean patch where one was, nothing gestures at it.** He is plateless the way S8 established: silently. **VILLAIN: no state, no body, no beat.** He appears only as three static relics per the bible's S8 "Gone": the glyph jar (aura only), the snapped counterfeit gun with its `#A8B84A` ink scabbed over, and the empty black jacket with the shoulder blocks, the skinny tie and the coiled earpiece wire hanging on a nail. **No slate head module, no Part 0, no rejection beat, no bin bag, no shades** (S8 owns the shades, once). **CAMEOS, all Claude sprites:** the crate loader, the card clipper. Plus the S4 tin bot, a prop, not a body.

- **Camera:** 35mm feel, slightly wide, low: bench height, about 40cm off the deck, looking mildly up so the assembled line has authority against the ceiling and the lamp cone rakes the top of frame. **One unbroken slow push, roughly 8% over 118 frames**, drifting a few degrees left to right, still moving at f117. The camera **never changes behaviour** and there is no "widest frame": the breadth at f12-24 is the diagram's 500px ring, the tightening at f24-56 is that ring collapsing. **Depth is a parallax multiplier, not a zoom percentage, and the multipliers are binding:** **FOREGROUND 4x** = the empty slate suit (drifts ~150px, clears frame right by ~f95, so the last 23 frames are clean for the pill and the chip). **FOREGROUND LIP 3x** = the out-of-focus bench lip with the leftover bolt and the flat-pack card, bottom left, held clear of the y655-745 composer band. **MIDGROUND 1x** = hero, manual, exploded diagram converging into the running line, tin bot, cameos. **BACKGROUND 0.35x** = the rolled-up shutter and the dawn wedge, the padlock, the corkboard, the shelf and the jar. Volumetric dust and swarf live in the light wedge across all three planes so the depth is readable, not implied.

- **Transition + SFX:** **IN from S8:** the dawn on the open road blows out to white and we are already inside the bay, the same dawn now cutting through the rolled-up shutter, so the road light and the workshop light are one continuous source across the cut. **No keys, they were spent at S7. No hood, no car, no hood-strut hiss, no ignition key-turn** (those were orphans of a deleted transition). The hero is mid-swing on frame 0. **Ranked beat list, 12-frame minimum gap, 7 cues over 118f = 1.78/sec, inside the 1.7-2.5/sec bar and clear of the 0.30s thinner:** **f0** bench thump + dry spine crack (ONE stacked cue) · **f24** the converge staccato as **ONE pre-rendered click-run wav** covering f24-56, one sfx_beat, not nine, because nine cues at 3.7f apart get shredded to two · **f60** ignition kick **+ the composer's UI pop, stacked as one cue** so the ask's only transient physically cannot be thinned away, and it is the same beat as the machine starting · **f72** press slam, ink squash, thwack · **f88** OS stamp thunk + low bass slam, 2px shake, with 12 frames of air on both sides because it is the scene's biggest hit · **f100** press slam two · **f112** one soft gold ding on the pill's pulse under the point. **BEDS, which do not count against the gap:** paper flutter f4-30, the low warm idle hum from f60 to the cut, belt hum and cleat ratchet, the wrench's tink into the tray at f76, the two flat-palm thuds at f92, the padlock tick, the shutter chain, the tin bot's clatter, room tone. Music resolves to its warmest chord under the idle hum and holds. **OUT:** no cut. The end-frame holds on the still-cycling line, the OS stamp and the pulsing pill, and loops straight back into the S0 hook, where the same low hum carries across the seam and the shutter is down again.

- **Mute check:** "A machine takes a crate of work, stamps it, hangs the ticket, and grabs the next crate, and the mechanic who built it is pointing at a comment box with OS typed in it." An event, not an object existing.

- **Hook into next:** There is no S10, so the object carried forward is the reel itself. Two loops close on the same frame: the shutter that locked him in is rolled up, and the padlock hangs open on its nail with an empty barrel, unlocked by the authority because he earned it, not forced. The one loop left OPEN is the viewer's: the OS pill pulsing unfinished in a composer that is waiting on them, over a line that is visibly mid-crate. The idle hum carries into the S0 hard cut, where the shutter slams back down on a Claude who has not earned it yet, and the rewatch reads completely differently.

- **Text budget (hard rule check, honest accounting):** **One header = the OS stamp.** **One number = 95%** (the 90 tick is an engraved mark, not a lit element). Two pieces of diegetic prop copy that are objects in the world rather than scene copy: **CLAYNES** on the manual cover (legible wordmark, no joke riding on it, fine print is line noise) and **DO NOT REFIT** on the one index card (kept because it is the only line here that is funnier read than seen). One house-chassis element: **the OS comment pill**. **DELETED:** the part numbers 04/05/05b/06/07, EXHIBIT A, DELETED FROM ASSEMBLY, "does not cover self-grading", the "runs while you sleep" tag, the OS cap patch, the record strip and its flipping pip, the chalk tallies, and the word PASS (the press issues a green roundel, which reads on mute and costs nothing).

- **⛔ CRITIC FIXES (binding on the build):**
  - **Machine rebuilt as a legible PASS/FAIL LINE** (fix 1): S0 crate in on a belt, twin-head press slams, card out with a green mark, cameo clips it to the job wire, next crate already entering. It is now "the thing that grades the work and does it again," which is the VO.
  - **Loop math fixed** (fix 2): THREE stations, not six. Sunrise dial, clipboard/out-tray and iris pod killed as live stations, kept as dead castings in the bloom. **Two complete grades on camera plus a third crate entering**, 28f cadence, ~10-12f per station, readable instead of a flicker.
  - **CTA gets 58 frames** (fix 3): composer pulled to **f60 and landed ON the ignition kick**, one beat. EXPLODE compressed to f0-24, CONVERGE to f24-56 to buy the room. Last third is calm.
  - **Museum gutted** (fixes 4, 6, 25): corkboard reduced from a four-exhibit trophy case to one relic and one card. Cut: cracked L-plate, S0 photo, EXHIBIT A tag, floor drain, squeegee Claude and layer L7-drain. **Exactly three relics, each doing different work:** jar (the aura bottled), empty suit (the occluder the camera ignores), snapped gun (the confiscated weapon). Reconciliation logged: fix 4 said cut the corkboard entirely and keep two relics; fixes 21, 25 and 29 all presuppose the corkboard, the gun and DO NOT REFIT survive, so the board survives as a mount for one relic and one card, and fix 4's "four exhibits, one idea" complaint is honoured by the cuts.
  - **Jar is static and unloved** (fixes 24, 25, superseding fix 4's jar-event and fix 22's kick stool): **no shades in the jar**, S8 owns the shades and they fire once. Glyphs only. No cameo, no tap, no stool, nobody looks at it. The stool is moot because nothing needs reaching.
  - **Part 0 cut entirely** (fixes 23, 24, 25, superseding fix 5): no fourth villain death, no slate head module, no DELETED FROM ASSEMBLY band, no bin-bag Claudes, layer L9-Part-0 deleted. Bible §2 locks him Destroyed at S7 and Gone at S8. Trophy case kept, funeral deleted.
  - **Text budget honoured, not dodged** (fixes 7, 29): picked geometry over copy everywhere. Legible list is closed and stated above. "Sub-legible texture carrying zero reading load" is no longer claimed for anything that was ever a joke.
  - **OS chip named once and made one thing** (fix 8): it is the gold keyword pill inside the house composer at (430, 700). The stamp and the pill are the same word twice.
  - **Camera/escalation contradiction resolved** (fix 9): "widest" deleted. The diagram gets wide, the frame never does. Unbroken 8% push, still moving at f117.
  - **Frame 0 now causes the scene** (fixes 10, 14, 26): hero drives the manual open with the heel of his glove and cracks the spine on f0, yanks the lamp cord at f2, and the pages fan **off his own hand** at f4. Wrench re-timed to where it means something: waiting into the converge, last part lands in it at f56, torque f56-60, **his torque is the f60 ignition**, toss f68, tray at f76, rag f82, slap f92, point f104-118. Hood whoosh and hood-strut hiss deleted, there is no hood and no car.
  - **Pit board killed, house CTA restored** (fix 11, over fix 15's pit-board preference): the house chassis rule is a hard floor, so the CTA is the house composer pattern, unmodified, resolving at f60. One mechanic, not two. Fix 15's real requirements (pin it, reserve y655-745, no collisions) are all honoured; its relocation of the drain Claude is moot because the drain is cut.
  - **Snapped key deleted** (fixes 12, 18): padlock open, shackle sprung, **hasp empty**. A snapped key is a jailbreak, which is the opposite of the thesis, and it contradicts the board's number-one ruling that the payoff object is handed over exactly once at S7. The card no longer says "no keys" and then draws one.
  - **CLAYNES demoted, flat-pack promoted** (fix 13): the parody is unremarked cover texture, the ink-peels-into-parts mechanic survives intact, and the wordless bolt-and-helpline gag leads.
  - **The three load-bearing things are pinned** (fix 15): hero (560, 470) at 1.5x, OS stamp punches off the spine and rests at (500, 300), composer band reserved y655-745 with the pill at (430, 700).
  - **SFX re-spaced to survive the thinner** (fixes 16, 27): 12-frame minimum, 7 ranked cues at 1.78/sec, runs shipped as ONE wav and as beds. The composer's UI pop is **stacked onto the f60 ignition kick** so it cannot be dropped. Wrench ring, palm thuds, padlock tick, shutter chain and tin bot demoted to beds because fix 1's two press slams are impacts and outrank them.
  - **One ignition frame, and it is causal** (fix 17): **f60**, stated identically in L2, L5, Escalation and the beat list. Converge ends at f56. The machine starts on the frame it is finished, turned over by the hero's own wrench. (Fix 3 and fix 26 both name f60; fix 2's f48 and fix 17's f64 are superseded, and the 28f cadence delivers the two circuits fix 2 asked for.)
  - **Oscillators de-synced onto near-coprime periods** (fix 19): lamp 74f, padlock 53f, suit and card-corner 97f, tin bot 41f, shutter chain 31f, job wire 67f, crate cadence 28f. Stated so the build cannot default them to a shared spring.
  - **Depth is a parallax multiplier** (fix 20): FG 4x, FG lip 3x, MG 1x, BG 0.35x. The suit actually clears frame right by ~f95 instead of parking in 11% of the frame for the whole CTA.
  - **The exhibit is on a clock** (fix 21): the lamp rakes the back wall, the DO NOT REFIT corner lifts on the 97f draft, and the corkboard is in the f117 list. No prop added.
  - **Record strip cut** (fix 28): S8 owns THE RECORD. No animated number display, no pip flip. The job wire is bench-height cards, not a board, and it carries no numbers. Scene's numbers: exactly one, 95%, with 90 engraved.
  - **Tin bot demoted to callback tier** (fix 30): no hero contact, no elbow nudge at f78, no SFX beat, never acted on.
